# M1: eADL description foundation

## Metadata

- Tree ID: `M1`
- Status: `active`
- Roadmap lane: `ROADMAP.md` §12 M1; §5; §18 work packages 5, 6; fixtures F01–F07, F27
- Created: `2026-09-13`
- Owner: repo-local workflow

## Goal

Implement the typed, composable, implementation-free description foundation: S-expression
reading with source spans, namespaced imports, schemas, parameters, unit checking, functional
kind registration, and typed requirement normalization — enough language to express the
initial system completely at the feature/functionality level.

## Non-Goals

- No universal optimizer, no hundreds of device models (`ROADMAP.md` §12 M1 gate discipline).
- No implementation content in eADL: no algorithms, no code providers, no model bodies.
- No resolution or generation. Those are `M3` and `M4`.

## Acceptance Criteria (roadmap exit gate, §12 M1)

- Examples parse, type-check, and round-trip semantically.
- Importing and instantiating sub-HW and sub-OS modules works, including double instantiation
  without shared mutable elaboration state.
- Invalid examples produce source-localized diagnostics.
- At least twenty semantic examples exist: positive matches, relevant missing facts,
  conflicts, and unsupported cases.
- S0's executable-generation evidence is available before M1 is promoted as M3-ready.

  ### Acceptance Checklist (enforced by `TASK-ACCEPTANCE`)

  - [x] **ROOT CAUSE (WHY + WHERE)** — §7.4 requires "exact integer time units or checked

    reason, `rc=0` on one file. WHERE: the reader produced exact `Integer`/`Decimal` forms and
    nothing above it could combine them, so the first consumer would have reached for `f64` —
    and `(tick-rate 10 MHz)` versus `(period 10 ms)` would have compared as `10` to `10`.
  - [x] **ADDRESSED (verified)** — `src/rational.rs` (exact `i128/i128`, normalized, every
    operation checked) and `src/quantity.rs` (13 units over four dimensions, conversion,
    declared comparison directions). F03 runs 15 arms, `test result: ok. 15 passed; 0 failed`.
    ⭐ The "before arithmetic" property is **demonstrated, not asserted**: the fixture compares
    two quantities whose magnitudes would overflow if touched — it first asserts
    `huge_frequency.in_base() == Err(Overflow)` — and the comparison still returns
    `IncompatibleDimensions`, which is only possible if the dimension check precedes any read.
    A zero frequency is refused at **construction**, so no such quantity exists to divide by.
  - [x] **NO REGRESSION** — `cargo fmt --all -- --check` clean; `cargo clippy --all-targets

    `scripts/check_doctrines.sh` → `=== all doctrines green ===`; `mdbook build docs/book` →
    `INFO HTML book written to`.
  - [x] **FIX** — exact rationals with `checked_add/sub/mul/div`, `ceil` rounding upward because
    that is the direction analysis needs, and exact text that prints `1/3` rather than
    `0.333333`; a deliberately small unit table (a table accepting arbitrary SI prefixes accepts
    `Ps` too, and a typo that parses is worse than one that does not); and
    `ComparisonDirection` with **no default**, because §5.2 warns that "more bits or a faster
    clock is not universally better" and an undeclared direction is a bug waiting for a
    substitution to expose it.
  - [x] **LOCKSTEP** — book chapter `docs/book/src/quantities.md` + `SUMMARY.md`;

    twice yields **two** instances with different bindings. F02: a cycle reports
    `circular import: a → b → c → a` with a repair naming the chain; a duplicated export and a
    duplicated alias each name **both** sites; a dangling export lists what the module does
    declare.
  - [x] **NO REGRESSION** — `cargo fmt --all -- --check` clean; `cargo clippy --all-targets

    `INFO HTML book written to`.
  - [x] **FIX** — `Version`/`ModuleDecl`/`ImportDecl`/`Instance`/`Program`, a `ModuleSource`
    trait so composition is testable without a filesystem, and an elaborator that collects
    every error except a cycle — the one failure that cannot be collected, because continuing
    into it does not terminate.
    ⭐ **A parser defect was caught by the fixture rather than by inspection.** The import
    version clause was read as a flat `(version at-least 1 0)` while the syntax is nested
    `(version (at-least 1 0))`, so *every* versioned import in the valid composition was
    refused — five of eighteen arms red, including the F01 happy path. The nested shape was kept
    rather than flattening the syntax to match the bug: keeping the relation as its own form
    leaves room for `at-most` and `exactly` without changing everything around them.
  - [x] **LOCKSTEP** — book chapter `docs/book/src/modules.md` + `SUMMARY.md`;
    `knowledge-map/subsystems.md`; `docs/TASK_TREE.md`; `MEMORY.md`; `LIVE_STATUS.md`;
    `CHANGELOG.md`.

- ID: `M1.5`
  Status: `done`
  Goal: presence states — offered, explicitly absent, undescribed — over a versioned
  capability vocabulary, with the dependency closure that decides relevance (§5.3).
  Acceptance: F04 — a relevant undescribed capability yields `missing-fact`; F05 — an
  irrelevant one leaves the system admissible; F06 — contradictory offered/absent yields
  `invalid-description`.
  Verification: see the acceptance checklist below.
  Commit: `ARCHOGEN-M1-0016`

  ### Acceptance Checklist (enforced by `TASK-ACCEPTANCE`)

  - [x] **ROOT CAUSE (WHY + WHERE)** — §2's correction table revised "unknown capability
    anywhere blocks generation" to "required facts in the selected dependency closure must be
    known", precisely so that "irrelevant unknown facts do not invalidate unrelated systems".
    Nothing implemented either half. Measured at the parent commit:
    `git grep -lniE 'presence|undescribed|closure' -- crates/` → no match, `rc=1`. WHERE: with
    no notion of relevance, the only two implementable behaviors are "block on every unknown",
    which makes every real platform undescribable, and "block on none", which fails open.
  - [x] **ADDRESSED (verified)** — `crates/eadl-model/src/presence.rs`; F04–F06 run 10 arms,
    `test result: ok. 10 passed; 0 failed`, plus 4 unit arms.
    ⭐ F04 and F05 are run against the **same description**, deliberately: it contains one
    undescribed fact inside the closure (`wrap-behavior` → `missing-fact`) and one outside it
    (`dma-channels` → no diagnostic, recorded in `outside_closure`). A checker that always
    blocks passes F04 and fails F05; one that never blocks passes F05 and fails F04; two
    separate descriptions could have been passed by two different bugs.
    `f04_and_f05_cannot_both_be_satisfied_by_a_constant_answer` asserts exactly that.
  - [x] **NO REGRESSION** — `cargo fmt --all -- --check` clean; `cargo clippy --all-targets
    --all-features -- -D warnings` → no errors; `cargo test --all` → 208 tests passing, `0
    failed`, with F01–F03, F27 and the schema and corpus suites unchanged;
    `scripts/check_doctrines.sh` → `=== all doctrines green ===`; `mdbook build docs/book` →
    `INFO HTML book written to`.
  - [x] **FIX** — `Presence`, `FactMap`, transitive closure over `needs` edges, and a report
    carrying the closure, the out-of-closure metadata and the missing set. Three distinctions
    are load-bearing: an **absent** required fact is `infeasible-configuration`, not
    `missing-fact` — reporting both the same way would send an author to describe something the
    platform has already denied; a **contradiction** is invalid whether or not it is reachable,
    because §5.3 qualifies the unknown-fact rule by relevance and does not qualify the
    contradiction rule; and a `needs` edge **nested inside** a `requires` clause is still
    followed, because a closure that only read top-level clauses would fail open.
  - [x] **LOCKSTEP** — book chapter `docs/book/src/presence.md` + `SUMMARY.md`;
    `knowledge-map/subsystems.md`; `docs/TASK_TREE.md`; `MEMORY.md`; `LIVE_STATUS.md`;
    `CHANGELOG.md`.

- ID: `M1.6`

  Goal: refinement — a concrete architectural description satisfying an abstract functional

  requirements (§5.1.1).
  Acceptance: F07 — an invalid refinement names the violated obligation; adding an unused
  device is not reported as invalid.
  Verification: see the acceptance checklist below.
  Commit: `ARCHOGEN-M1-0017`

  ### Acceptance Checklist (enforced by `TASK-ACCEPTANCE`)

  - [x] **ROOT CAUSE (WHY + WHERE)** — §5.1.1 requires a refinement declaration to be "an
    obligation to check, not permission to trust a claim blindly", and nothing checked it.
    Measured at the parent commit: `git grep -lniE 'refine' -- crates/` → no match, `rc=1`.
    WHERE: with no checker, a `(refines …)` clause would have been exactly the blind trust §5.1.1
    names — a claim that reads as verification and is not.
  - [x] **ADDRESSED (verified)** — `crates/eadl-model/src/refinement.rs`; F07 runs 13 arms,
    `test result: ok. 13 passed; 0 failed`. Three obligations, each named in its own violation:
    `guarantee` (`does not offer \`wrap-behavior\`` … `violated obligation \`guarantee\``),
    `constraint` (`does not satisfy \`at-least 32 bit\``) and `exclusion`
    (`which \`soc.abstract\` declares absent`). Every violation is reported, not just the first.
  - [x] **NO REGRESSION** — `cargo fmt --all -- --check` clean; `cargo clippy --all-targets
    --all-features -- -D warnings` → no errors; `cargo test --all` → 223 tests passing, `0
    failed`, with F01–F06 and F27 unchanged; `scripts/check_doctrines.sh` →
    `=== all doctrines green ===`; `mdbook build docs/book` → `INFO HTML book written to`.
  - [x] **FIX** — `Facets`, `Obligation`, `check`, and a report separating **violations** from
    **additions**.
    ⭐ Two roadmap sentences pull against each other here, and the fixture tests both
    directions rather than picking one. §5.1.1 demands the obligations be checked; §5.3 says
    "adding an unused device is not automatically an invalid refinement". A checker that demanded
    equality passes the exclusion arm and rejects every real refinement; one that allowed any
    addition passes the addition arm and silently drops the exclusion obligation — which is the
    one that matters most, because an abstract description declares a fact absent *because*
    something depends on its absence.
  - [x] **LOCKSTEP** — book chapter `docs/book/src/refinement.md` + `SUMMARY.md`;
    `knowledge-map/subsystems.md`; `docs/TASK_TREE.md`; `MEMORY.md`; `LIVE_STATUS.md`;
    `CHANGELOG.md`.

  ### Acceptance Checklist (enforced by `TASK-ACCEPTANCE`)

  - [x] **ROOT CAUSE (WHY + WHERE)** — two measured facts. (1) `M1.2` recorded a **10-of-11**
    schema reach: `crates/eadl-model/tests/kinds.rs` pinned that `execution-bound` was refused
    only by the boundary classifier, because `defsystem`'s `task` clause was declared
    `(holds forms)` and therefore opaque. (2) No use case existed as eADL at all —
    `git ls-files -- examples` at the parent commit → no match, `rc=1` — so §5.1's `os/rt`
    module had no consumer and the claim "no execution bound appears in eADL" had nothing
    behind it. WHERE: `docs/semantics/kinds/core.eadl`, the `task` clause.
  - [x] **ADDRESSED (verified)** — `Holds::Kind` makes a clause validate recursively as a
    declaration of a named kind; `docs/semantics/kinds/os-rt.eadl` declares `task`; `core.eadl`
    now writes `(clause task (cardinality any) (holds kind task))`. The pinned test was replaced
    by `the_schema_now_reaches_every_rejected_case`, which asserts `out_of_reach.is_empty()` over
    **13 of 13**; `an_execution_bound_inside_a_task_is_now_caught_by_the_schema_too` asserts the
    refusal carries the boundary wording, not a generic "unknown clause". The three use cases
    exist as `examples/*/system.eadl` with a 7-arm suite, `test result: ok. 7 passed; 0 failed`.
  - [x] **NO REGRESSION** — `cargo fmt --all -- --check` clean; `cargo clippy --all-targets
    --all-features -- -D warnings` → no errors; `cargo test --all` → 234 tests passing,
    `0 failed` across 17 suites; `scripts/check_doctrines.sh` → `=== all doctrines green ===`;
    `mdbook build docs/book` → `INFO HTML book written to`.
  - [x] **FIX** — `Holds::Kind` + recursive validation; the `os/rt` module; `absent` and
    `refines` clauses added to the core kinds (§5.3 maintains three presence states, and the
    language could previously only say two); `entry-point` and `stack-allocation` added to the
    boundary registry with worked corpus cases, taking the corpus from 21 to 23 and every census
    assertion with it.
    ⭐ **Three defects were found by running the new tests, not by inspection.** (a) The example
    suite failed with `defblock declarations have no absent clause` — §5.3's vocabulary was
    implemented in `presence.rs` and missing from the language. (b) It then failed with
    `missing-fact: console.uart`, because every `uses` target was treated as a leaf fact; a
    composition reference to a *declared block* is satisfied by its declaration, and the absence
    check was reordered so that being declared does not repeal an explicit absence. (c) Piping
    `diagnose` into `head -3` panicked with `failed printing to stdout: Broken pipe` — a closed
    pipe is not a failure, and a kept tool that prints a Rust backtrace where the user expected
    three lines is a defect in the tool.
    ⛔ A fourth was self-inflicted and worth recording: two `str.replace` edits silently no-op'd
    because rustfmt had reflowed the target text, and the suite reported a stale result. Every
    scripted edit since asserts that it applied.
  - [x] **LOCKSTEP** — book chapter `docs/book/src/workload.md` + `SUMMARY.md`;
    `examples/README.md`; corpus README census 21 → 23 with both tables regenerated;
    `knowledge-map/subsystems.md`; `docs/TASK_TREE.md`; `MEMORY.md`; `LIVE_STATUS.md`;
    `CHANGELOG.md`.

- ID: `M1.11`
  Status: `done`
  Goal: a **normative grammar** for the eADL surface syntax, and a mechanism that keeps it and
  the reader from diverging.
  Acceptance: `docs/semantics/grammar.md` states the lexical and phrase structure as production
  rules; an EBNF-driven recognizer derived from that file accepts every corpus description the
  reader accepts and rejects every one it rejects; a description accepted by one and not the
  other fails the build.

  ⛔ **Opened because the format has no definition other than its implementation.** Measured:
  `git grep -lEi 'ebnf|grammar|bnf' -- '*.md' '*.eadl'` matches only `ROADMAP.md` (one passing
  sentence about "grammar size") and `DEV_NOTES.md` (a neutrality census) — no production rule
  exists anywhere. `crates/eadl-front/src/reader.rs` is 743 lines of hand-written recursive
  descent and is the sole authority on what eADL *is*, while every test in the repository
  validates against it — which makes it unfalsifiable as a definition.

  §4.1 assigns the obligation ("the eADL project owns **syntax**"), §12 M1 anticipates the syntax
  freezing at a compatibility baseline that nothing has defined, and §4.4 requires an
  independently derived checker whose parser is a declared trust dependency — which is impossible
  to write against a 743-line implementation. This is the same defect as `M1.9`
  (`Profile::decisions` was prose), `S0.1` (the priority direction was unwritten) and `M2.2`
  (five runtime semantics were unwritten), at the largest scale the project has.
  Verification: see the acceptance checklist below.
  Commit: `ARCHOGEN-M1-0036`

  ### Acceptance Checklist (enforced by `TASK-ACCEPTANCE`)

  - [x] **ROOT CAUSE (WHY + WHERE)** — the eADL surface syntax had no definition other than its
    implementation. Measured at the parent commit:
    `git grep -lEi 'ebnf|grammar|bnf' -- '*.md' '*.eadl'` matched only `ROADMAP.md` (one sentence
    about "grammar size") and `DEV_NOTES.md` (a neutrality census); `find docs -iname '*grammar*'
    -o -iname '*syntax*' -o -iname '*spec*'` found nothing; `wc -l crates/eadl-front/src/reader.rs`
    → **743**. WHERE that bites: every test in the repository validated against the reader, so
    there was no input that could show it wrong — whatever it did was correct by definition.
  - [x] **ADDRESSED (verified)** — `docs/semantics/grammar.md` states the lexical and phrase
    structure as productions, and `crates/eadl-front/tests/conformance.rs` derives a recognizer
    from that file and checks four properties:
    `cargo test -p eadl-front --test conformance` → `6 passed; 0 failed`. Both accept all **62**
    corpus descriptions; both reject six malformed inputs; both produce the **same token spans**
    on every corpus file; and both accept **23 probes**, one per production.
  - [x] **NO REGRESSION** — `cargo fmt --all -- --check` → `fmt rc=0`;
    `cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings` → no warnings; `cargo test --all`
    → **421** passed, `0 failed` (415 before this leaf, `+6`); `scripts/check_doctrines.sh` →
    `=== all doctrines green ===`. `reader.rs` is unchanged — `git diff --stat HEAD --
    crates/eadl-front/src` is empty. The grammar documents the reader's existing language; it did
    not require changing it, which is itself a result.
  - [x] **FIX** — the grammar document is the source, and the test *reads it*: one definition,
    not two. The EBNF dialect implemented is exactly the one the document's notation table
    describes, so a notation that can express more than the recognizer checks cannot creep in.
  - [x] **REPRODUCE / ISSUE** — ⛔ **three defects were found by red-arming this leaf's own
    mechanism, and the second changed what the leaf delivers.**
    (1) Splitting productions on `;` cut the `comment` rule in half, because `;` is also a literal
    *in* the language being described — caught by the tests' first run.
    (2) The recognizer returned one end position per expression and so could not backtrack out of
    an alternative: for `atom = ( string | number | symbol ) , ? delimiter` on `10ms`, `number`
    matched `10`, the lookahead failed, and `symbol` was never tried. It gave the right verdict
    for the wrong reason, and the red arm that weakened `symbol_start` **did not fire** — which is
    how it was found. Rewritten continuation-passing; the same red arm now fails with
    `grammar ACCEPTS / reader rejects` on `(period 10ms)` and `(counter-width 32bit)`.
    (3) ⭐ **Acceptance agreement turned out to be too weak a conformance claim.** Dropping `_`
    from hexadecimal literals in the reader left every test green while `(base 0x1000_0000)`
    silently became the two forms `4096` and `_0000` — verified by printing the canonical form:
    `(region device.timer (base 4096 _0000))` against the correct
    `(region device.timer (base 268435456))`. A memory-mapped base address of `0x10000000` read as
    `4096`, with both implementations "accepting" the file. Token **segmentation** is now compared,
    and that mutation fails naming the byte offsets:
    `examples/periodic-three/system.eadl: 127 tokens vs 128 — grammar sees "0x1000_0000" at
    953..964, reader sees "0x1000" at 953..959`.
    A fourth finding needed no mutation: **the corpus is not a conformance suite.** It contains
    exactly one number with a digit separator and that one is hexadecimal, so nothing in it
    reached decimal separators, signs, escapes or CRLF. The 23 probes exist for that reason.
  - [x] **LOCKSTEP** — `docs/book/src/reading.md` points at the normative grammar;
    `knowledge-map/subsystems.md`; `TOOLBOX.md`; `MEMORY.md`; `LIVE_STATUS.md`; `CHANGELOG.md`;
    `DEV_NOTES.md`; `docs/TASK_TREE.md`.

  ### Lesson promotion

  `a parser is not a specification, and "both accept it" is not conformance` →
  `promotion: declined (this leaf is three already-recorded lessons applied to a language — a grammar existing only as prose is docs/knowledge/prose-beside-data-goes-unenforced.md, a suite blind to a class of error is docs/knowledge/a-gate-is-only-as-sharp-as-its-fixtures.md, and a red arm that fails to fire is docs/knowledge/verify-the-mutation-applied.md; a fourth entry answering the same three questions makes the retrievable layer harder to search, not easier)`

  ### What this establishes, and what it does not

  It establishes that the normative grammar and the reader accept the same language **and agree on
  where every token begins and ends**. It does not establish that the reader builds the right tree
  from those tokens, nor that it assigns the right value to each — a reader that tokenized
  identically and mis-nested, or read `1.5` as three halves, would pass. Nesting is the corpus
  suites' business; value exactness belongs to `M1.12`, which is why the reference is a separate
  leaf rather than a section of the grammar.

  Commit: `ARCHOGEN-M1-0071 (leaf M1.12)` (decomposition) · closed by `ARCHOGEN-M1-0077 (leaf M1.12.5)`

  ✅ **Closed `2026-09-28`, all five children `done`.** The reference exists and is executed rather than
  read: `.1` wrote it and fixed the three defects its first run found, `.2` made the literal space
  single-defined across reference, reader and recognizer, `.3` made a diagnostic code a stated rule over
  a machine-read declaration censused in both directions, `.4` finished the document, and `.5` closed

  ⛔ **What closing this leaf does not claim.** Two gaps its closure review measured are **not** in the
  acceptance criteria above and are therefore not closed by meeting them: the model layer's diagnostics
  are stated normatively nowhere while the book renders eight of them, and a stated code's reachability
  is pinned by no input — 25 of the 53 rows are named by no test, which falsifies the reason `M1.12.3`
  recorded for deferring the `fires on` column. Both are `M1.26`, filed with their censuses. Recording
  them here rather than only on the child, because this is the leaf a future session reads when it asks
  "is the language reference finished?".

  ### Design note — added `2026-09-27` by `M1.23`, which measured the trap

  ⚠️ **`BOOK-ANCHORS` cannot discharge this leaf's acceptance criterion, and reading it as though it
  could is the trap.** `scripts/check_book_anchors.sh:26-28` states its own limit verbatim: *"this
  proves a chapter points somewhere real, never that what it says about that place is true. Prose
  cannot be checked by grep. It removes the cheapest failure — a citation that has rotted — and leaves
  the expensive one to review."* A reference that cites real paths and states false rules passes it
  green. `M1.23` is the measured instance: both surfaces cited real code and published a figure that
  had been false for **47 commits**.

  So "every rule the frontend enforces is stated normatively" needs a mechanism of the shape `M1.11`
  gave the grammar — `crates/eadl-front/tests/conformance.rs` reads the EBNF **out of**
  `docs/semantics/grammar.md` and executes it, so the document cannot drift from the reader. Two legs
  follow, and both are census-shaped rather than prose-shaped:

  1. **Producer → reference.** Enumerate the codes the frontend can emit *from the producer*, and
     require each to be stated. The census must read the production half only, and must survive
     multi-line constructor calls — `Diagnostic::error(` and its code literal are frequently on
     different lines, so a single-line `grep` silently under-counts. What works:
     `awk '/#\[cfg\(test\)\]/{exit} {print}' <file> | tr '\n' ' ' | grep -oE 'Diagnostic::error\( *"[a-z0-9-]+"'`.
     Test-only codes must be excluded, not documented: `read-example` exists solely inside
     `diagnostic.rs`'s `#[cfg(test)]` module.
  2. **Reference → producer.** The mirror leg, so a renamed code leaves a rotted row rather than a
     stale truth. `docs/CLAIM_VERIFICATION.md` §3 leg 2 requires both directions; one direction alone
     is a population, not a count.

  ⭐ And per `M1.11`'s own honest limit, **value exactness is this leaf's to close** — it says so by
  name. `crates/eadl-front/tests/conformance.rs:24-28`, verbatim: *"What remains outside: this does
  not establish that the reader builds the right \*tree\* from those tokens, nor that it assigns the
  right \*value\* to each. A reader that tokenized identically and mis-nested, or that read `1.5` as
  three halves, would still pass. Nesting is the corpus suites' business (`corpus.rs`, the semantic
  cases); value exactness belongs to the language reference, leaf `M1.12`."* Token segmentation is
  already compared; value is not. A normative table of literal → exact value, read out of the
  reference and executed against the reader, is the leg that closes it — the same move that makes the
  EBNF normative rather than decorative.

  ⛔ **Carry no measured figure into the reference.** `M1.23`'s decision, recorded in Decisions below:
  a source header states the rule and names the test that measures; a figure appears only where a gate
  compares it to the measurement. The reference will be tempted to state counts — of codes, of
  clauses, of kinds — and each one is a future `M1.23`. Where a count is genuinely informative,
  derive it in the mechanism and let the prose name the mechanism.

  ### Decomposition — added `2026-09-28`, after a 39-literal probe of the reader

  This leaf is five children rather than one, because the probe below found defects that have to be
  fixed *before* a normative sentence about them can be written, and a leaf that both writes the rule
  and repairs the implementation it describes cannot show a clean before→after for either.

  ⛔ **Measured first, with a tool, before any prose was drafted** (`TOOLBOX.md`). A scratch instrument
  ran `cargo run -q -p eadl-front --example diagnose` over 39 literals — every number form, escape and
  boundary the grammar's productions admit — one literal per file, printing exit status, diagnostic
  code and canonical text. Its durable form is `M1.12.1`'s table; the scratch copy stays untracked
  under `target/tmp/`. Seven findings, each owned by a child below:

  | # | Finding | Measured | Owner |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | F-A | `0X10` reads as `16`, but the grammar's `hexadecimal` spells the prefix `"0x"` only | probe `rc=0 (probe 16)`; `git grep -nE '0X[0-9a-fA-F]'` over tracked files → **no match** | `M1.12.2` |
  | F-B | `0x_10` reads as `16` — a leading separator — but `hexadecimal` requires a `hex_digit` first, and the reader's own decimal path cannot start with `_` either | probe `rc=0 (probe 16)`; `git grep -nE '0[xX]_'` → **no match** | `M1.12.1` |
  | F-C | `escape` admits `"0"`, but the reader refuses `\0` as `read-bad-escape` and its own hint lists five escapes without it | probe `rc=1 error[read-bad-escape]`; `git grep -nF '\0' -- '*.eadl'` → **no match** | `M1.12.2` |
  | F-D | canonical form emits a **raw carriage return** for `\r`: `diagnose` on `(probe "a\rb")` pipes to `od -c` as `" a \r b "` — a control byte inside the text §12 M4 hashes and compares, and inside a document whose canonical form is defined as one form per line. `crates/archogen-s0/src/provenance.rs:148` escapes `\r` in its own `quote()`; the normative printer does not | `od -c` output above | `M1.12.1` |
  | F-E | `-9223372036854775808` is refused as `read-number-overflow`, so the documented "exact 64-bit signed" range has one unwritable value: the magnitude is parsed as `i64` *before* the sign is applied | probe `rc=1 error[read-number-overflow]`; `git grep -nF '9223372036854775808'` → **no match** | `M1.12.1` |
  | F-F | no value at or above 2^63 can be written at all, so a base address of `0x8000_0000_0000_0000` is refused | probe `rc=1 error[read-number-overflow]` | routed to `M1.13` — widening the integer domain is a freeze decision, not a reference sentence |
  | F-G | a control character other than `\n \t \r` cannot be escaped, so a string holding one prints it raw in canonical form | `write_canonical`'s match arm set | routed to `M1.13` — extending the escape set is a language change |

  ⭐ **The meta-finding is why F-A, F-B and F-C survived `M1.11`.** That leaf's own honest limit says
  the corpus is not a conformance suite; the same is true of its 23 probes, which are one per
  *production* and therefore never combine two. All three divergences live in a literal form no corpus
  file contains — measured: three `git grep` censuses, three empty results. A mechanism that checks the
  grammar and the reader against each other over the inputs the repository happens to ship cannot see
  the literal space, and the literal space is where a wrong value becomes a wrong system. `M1.12.2`
  therefore derives the probe set from the reference's table rather than adding three more hand-written
  probes.

- ID: `M1.12.1`
  Status: `done`
  Goal: the language reference exists and its **value table is executed**. Write
  `docs/semantics/reference.md` with normative chapters for number exactness, strings and escapes, and
  canonical form, each carrying a table of `literal → exact value → canonical text`; and write
  `crates/eadl-front/tests/reference.rs`, which reads those tables **out of the document** and runs
  every row against `crates/eadl-front/src/reader.rs` and `form.rs`. This is the leg `M1.11` named by
  name and could not close: token segmentation is compared there, value is not.
  Acceptance: a row the reader disagrees with fails naming the literal, the expected value and the
  actual one; F-B, F-D and F-E are fixed with a measured before→after each, and the fix is what makes
  their rows pass rather than the rows being written to match the reader; the mechanism has RED arms
  for a wrong value, a wrong canonical text, a row deleted from the table, and a table the document
  stops carrying; no measured figure appears in the reference; `make focused` exit `0`.
  Verification: see the acceptance checklist below.
  Commit: `ARCHOGEN-M1-0072 (leaf M1.12.1)`

  ⛔ **The expected values must be derived, not transcribed.** A table filled in by printing what the
  reader produced is a transcription of the implementation and is as unfalsifiable as no table at all —
  the exact defect `M1.11` existed to end. Each row's value comes from the literal's arithmetic meaning
  (`0x1000_0000` = 16^7 = 268435456, computed independently of the reader), from §7.4's exactness
  requirement, or from this tree's recorded decisions; a row where that derivation and the reader
  disagree is a **finding**, and F-B/F-D/F-E are three such rows.

  ### Acceptance Checklist (enforced by `TASK-ACCEPTANCE`)

  - [x] **REPRODUCE / ISSUE** — ⭐ **the mechanism was written and run before any fix, and its first
    run is the reproduction.** `cargo test -p eadl-front --test reference
    the_reference_value_tables_are_the_frontend_s_verdict` against the unmodified frontend →
    `test result: FAILED. 0 passed; 1 failed`, reporting four violations over three findings, verbatim:
    `docs/semantics/reference.md:159: "a\rb": canonical text carries the control character '\r', which
    §3 rule 3 forbids`; `…:159: "a\rb": §3 requires canonical text \"a\\rb\", the frontend printed
    \"a\rb\"`; `…:85: the reference says -9223372036854775808 is `integer -9223372036854775808`, and
    the frontend refused it: error[read-number-overflow]: this literal does not fit in a 64-bit signed
    integer`; `…:98: the reference refuses `0x_10` as `read-malformed-number`, and the frontend did
    not: it read integer 16 with no diagnostic`. Each was independently reproduced with the probe
    instrument: `bash target/tmp/m112/probe.sh target/tmp/m112/literals2.txt` → `0x_10 rc=0 (probe
    16)`, `-9223372036854775808 rc=1 error[read-number-overflow]`, and `diagnose … | od -c` on
    `(probe "a\rb")` → `" a \r b "` — one raw carriage-return byte inside canonical text.
  - [x] **ROOT CAUSE (WHY + WHERE)** — three separate causes, each pinpointed before editing.
    **F-B** WHERE `crates/eadl-front/src/reader.rs`'s hexadecimal branch: the digit loop accepted
    `is_ascii_hexdigit() || b == b'_'` from the first character, so a leading separator was consumed
    and then deleted by `.replace('_', "")`. WHY it matters: `hexadecimal = [sign], "0x", hex_digit,
    {hex_digit | "_"}` in `docs/semantics/grammar.md` requires a digit first, and the reader's own
    decimal path cannot begin with `_` either — hexadecimal was the one literal form whose leading
    character nothing policed. **F-E** WHERE the same function's two conversion sites
    (`i64::from_str_radix(&raw, 16)` and `combined.parse::<i64>()`), both *before* `if negative {
    -magnitude }`. WHY: `-9223372036854775808`'s magnitude is `i64::MAX + 1`, so it overflowed on the
    way in and the sign could never make it `i64::MIN` — the range §1 of the reference claims had
    exactly one unwritable value. **F-D** WHERE `crates/eadl-front/src/form.rs`'s `write_canonical`,
    whose `Str` arm escaped `"`, `\`, `\n` and `\t` and copied every other character. WHY: `\r` is a
    supported escape on the way *in* (`reader.rs`) and was not one on the way *out*, so canonical text
    — the text §12 M4 hashes and compares, defined as one form per line — could carry a control byte.
    `crates/archogen-s0/src/provenance.rs:148` escapes `'\r'` in its own `quote()`: the same problem,
    already solved correctly in a sibling module.
  - [x] **ADDRESSED (verified)** — each finding re-measured after the fix, with the same two
    instruments. Probe: `-9223372036854775808` → `rc=0 '(probe -9223372036854775808)'`; `0x_10` →
    `rc=1 error[read-malformed-number] `0x_10` is not a hexadecimal literal`; `diagnose … | od -c` on
    `(probe "a\rb")` → `" a \ r b "`, a backslash and an `r` where the carriage-return byte was.
    The over-widening control stayed refused: `9223372036854775808` → `rc=1
    error[read-number-overflow]`. Mechanism: `cargo test -p eadl-front --test reference` → **`11
    passed; 0 failed`**, the baseline leg green and all eight RED arms green. Crate:
    `cargo test -p eadl-front` → lib `49 passed` (46 before, +3 for the three fixes' own unit tests),
    `conformance` `6 passed`, `corpus` `14 passed`, `f01_f02_modules` `18 passed`, `reference`
    `11 passed`, all `0 failed`.
  - [x] **NO REGRESSION** — `make focused` → `tier focused: passed — 3 passed, 0 failed, 0
    unavailable, 0 not built` (`fmt` `clippy` `tests`); `cargo test --all` → **444 passed, 0 failed
    over 37 suites** against a baseline of 430 over 36, the delta being exactly the new suite and its
    eleven tests plus the three new unit tests; `bash scripts/check_doctrines.sh` → `=== all doctrines
    green ===` over 13 checks; `bash scripts/check_book_anchors.sh` → `book-anchors: OK (19
    chapter(s); every cited repository path resolves)`. `conformance.rs` and `corpus.rs` are
    **unmodified** — `git diff --stat HEAD -- crates/eadl-front/tests/conformance.rs
    crates/eadl-front/tests/corpus.rs` is empty — so the grammar leg and the corpus-figure gate still
    pass on their own terms. No `Cargo.toml`, `Cargo.lock` or manifest path changed.
  - [x] **FIX** — at the lowest level that actually works, three local changes and no new abstraction:
    a `leads_with_digit` lookahead in the hexadecimal branch (keeping `0x` and `0xg`'s existing wording,
    since "no digits at all" and "a separator led the digits" are different mistakes with different
    repairs); one `signed(magnitude: i128, negative: bool) -> Option<i64>` helper both conversion
    sites now share, so the order that caused F-E cannot be written twice; and one `'\r'` arm in
    `write_canonical`. ⛔ **No row was weakened to make a leg pass.** All three rows state the rule the
    language was always meant to have — §7.4's exactness, §12 M4's identical canonical text — and the
    frontend was changed to meet them, which is the only direction that leaves the reference
    normative. The `\r` fix is additionally made permanent rather than merely applied: leg 1 checks
    *every* row's canonical text for any ASCII control character, so a printer that emits one fails on
    the property and not on one row.
  - [x] **LOCKSTEP** — `docs/semantics/reference.md` (new, normative); `docs/semantics/grammar.md`
    (its "not here" table and "no float" note now point at the reference by path, and a ⚠️ block names
    the two spellings `M1.12.2` reconciles); `crates/eadl-front/tests/reference.rs` (new);

  ### Lesson promotion

  `two implementations agreeing over the inputs you ship is not agreement over the language` →
  `promotion: declined (the blind spot is already `docs/knowledge/a-gate-is-only-as-sharp-as-its-fixtures.md`
  and the independence half is `docs/knowledge/an-oracle-is-independent-by-construction.md`. What today
  adds is the *remedy* — enumerate the input population from the specification rather than from the
  corpus — and that remedy does not exist yet: `M1.12.2` is the leaf that derives the recognizer's probe
  set from the reference's table. `M1.12.2` carries the obligation to promote it, with the mechanism
  beside it, rather than promoting a lesson whose mechanism is still a plan.)`

- ID: `M1.12.2`
  Status: `done`

  definition and three surfaces agreeing on it — reference, reader, recognizer. `conformance.rs` gains
  a leg that reads `docs/semantics/reference.md` and requires the recognizer to accept exactly the rows
  the table gives a value and reject exactly the rows it gives a diagnostic code; the production probe
  set is derived from the table, so a literal form the language admits cannot go unprobed again.
  Acceptance: F-A and F-C are resolved, each with the side that changed named and a decision recorded
  (widen the grammar to admit `0X`, or narrow the reader; drop `"0"` from `escape`, or implement it);
  a row whose verdict the recognizer and the reader disagree about fails naming both; RED arms for a
  recognizer that accepts a row the table refuses and one that refuses a row it accepts; the four
  existing conformance properties stay green; `make focused` exit `0`.

  ### Acceptance Checklist (enforced by `TASK-ACCEPTANCE`)

    conformance the_grammar_and_the_reference_agree_on_the_literal_space` → `panicked at
    crates/eadl-front/tests/conformance.rs:887` with exactly two violations, verbatim:
    `docs/semantics/reference.md:100: the reference says `0X10` is well-formed, and the recognizer
    derived from docs/semantics/grammar.md rejects it` and `docs/semantics/reference.md:175: the
    reference says `"nul\0here"` is NOT well-formed, and the recognizer derived from
    docs/semantics/grammar.md accepts it`. The working copy was restored from
    `target/tmp/m112/grammar.fixed.md` and `git diff --stat` re-checked afterwards (`13 insertions,
    9 deletions`, the intended edit and nothing else). Reachability was already censused by `.1`:
    `git grep -nE '0X[0-9a-fA-F]'`, `git grep -nE '0[xX]_'` and `git grep -nF '\0' -- '*.eadl'` over
    tracked files each return **no match**, which is why `M1.11`'s mutation-tested conformance check
    could not see either one.
  - [x] **ROOT CAUSE (WHY + WHERE)** — one cause, not two. WHERE: the *population*. `conformance.rs`
    compared the recognizer with the reader over (a) every `.eadl` file the repository ships and (b) one
    probe per production. Both populations are samples: the corpus is what authors happened to write,
    and one-probe-per-production never combines two — while all three divergences live in a
    combination (an uppercase prefix, a separator where a digit is required, a backslash before a
    character the escape set does not define). WHY it matters: two implementations can agree on every
    input they are both given and still implement different languages, and mutation testing cannot find
    that, because a mutation is a hypothesis about the implementation and not about the input space.
    The secondary causes are the two productions themselves — `hexadecimal`'s prefix spelled `"0x"`
    only while its digits were already case-insensitive, and `escape` admitting `"0"` while
    `crates/eadl-front/src/reader.rs` never implemented `\0` and its own hint lists the escapes that do
    exist.
  - [x] **ADDRESSED (verified)** — the leg now takes its population from the reference's own
    enumeration, and both divergences are gone with the side that changed named: **F-A widened the
    grammar** (`hexadecimal = [ sign ] , ( "0x" | "0X" ) , hex_digit , { hex_digit | "_" } ;`) because
    the reader has always read `0X10` and a case-*sensitive* prefix would be the one inconsistency in a
    literal whose digits are case-insensitive; **F-C narrowed the grammar** (`escape` no longer admits
    `"0"`) because the reader never implemented `\0`, a NUL byte has no use in a description, and
    `read-bad-escape`'s own hint enumerates the set that exists. `cargo test -p eadl-front --test
    conformance` → **10 passed; 0 failed** (was 6): the new leg plus three arms. ⭐ **The two grammar
    arms are the original defect restored, verified byte-for-byte**: a `python3` diff of each arm's
    mutated production against `git show HEAD:docs/semantics/grammar.md` reports `identical: True` for
    `hexadecimal` and `line in HEAD: True` for `escape`, so reverting either fix fails the build rather
    than merely differing from it. `cargo test -p eadl-front` → lib 49, conformance 10, corpus 14,
    f01_f02 18, reference 11, all `0 failed`.
  - [x] **NO REGRESSION** — the four properties `M1.11` established are untouched and green in the same
    run (`the_grammar_document_parses_as_the_notation_it_documents`,
    `the_recognizer_is_not_vacuously_permissive`, `the_grammar_and_the_reader_agree_on_the_whole_corpus`,
    `the_grammar_and_the_reader_agree_on_malformed_input`) plus both corpus legs. `make focused` →

  - [x] **FIX** — at the lowest level that actually works, and the fix is the *population*, not the two

    which ask the recognizer for a verdict on every row of both of the reference's tables, and a
    non-vacuity assertion that the tables made it both accept and reject something — a leg in which the
    recognizer gave only one verdict would pass on a recognizer that accepts everything. The table
    reader moved to `crates/eadl-front/tests/common/reference_table.rs` and is shared by both suites:
    one normative document gets **one** parser, because two would be two things that can disagree about
    what the rule *says* before either asks what it means. `Grammar::load()` became
    `Grammar::from_document(text)` over an `include_str!` constant, which is what lets an arm mutate the
    grammar side at all, and makes a moved document a compile error rather than a silent no-op. The
    reference gained a distinction the leg needed and the language always had: `error C` (**not
    well-formed**) versus `refused C` (well-formed, value outside the domain), because `1.2.3` and
    `9223372036854775808` are different kinds of refusal and a grammar that could express the second
    would have to know the width of the value domain.

  ### ROUTING EVIDENCE

  Finding routed: **a count figure in a normative document that no gate watches** → `PROGRAM.20` (the
  carried-figure register).

  What was measured, and where: `docs/semantics/grammar.md:83` read **"Three rules the productions
  above imply, stated because they are load-bearing"** above four numbered items. Found while editing
  the adjacent block for F-A/F-C, not by a sweep. Confirmed by reading the section: items 1–4 are
  delimiter lookahead, `;` inside literals, a sign without a digit, and the `? delimiter` lookahead —
  four. Reproduced with `git show HEAD:docs/semantics/grammar.md | grep -n 'Three rules'` → `83:###
  Three rules the productions above imply, stated because they are load-bearing`, so the figure
  predates this leaf and is not an artifact of the edit.

  Does it reproduce outside the family it is sent to? **Yes, and that is why it is routed rather than
  fixed and forgotten.** `PROGRAM.20` already lists four shapes of the same class across book chapters,
  a corpus index, a crate module header and this tree's own index row. This instance is a **fifth
  shape in a sixth surface class** — a normative language document — which `PROGRAM.20`'s acceptance
  does not enumerate ("book chapters, corpus indexes, crate module headers"). The instance itself is
  fixed here (count deleted, not retyped, per `M1.23`'s decision); the *class* needs the register, and
  the register needs to know its surface list is incomplete.

  ### Lesson promotion

  `two implementations agreeing over the inputs you ship is not agreement over the language` →
  `promotion: promoted (docs/knowledge/enumerate-the-population-from-the-specification.md, with four
  answers: questions added — "My two implementations agree on every input I have — which input is
  neither of them ever given?", "A mutation-tested gate is green. What can it still not see?", "Where
  should a test's input population come from?", "Why did a grammar and its reader disagree for a whole
  milestone with nothing failing?"; questions answered — that mutation testing sharpens a gate against
  the population it already has and cannot widen it, that a corpus is a sample of what authors wrote
  rather than of what the language admits, and that one-probe-per-production cannot contain a
  combination; plus the `error` / `refused` distinction that let one table drive both legs. This is the
  promotion `M1.12.1` declined and handed here, discharged in the same commit as the mechanism it
  describes, as that leaf required.)`

  ### Acceptance Checklist (enforced by `TASK-ACCEPTANCE`)

  - [x] **REPRODUCE / ISSUE** — the gap was stated by the reference itself before this leaf: its "What
    this reference does not yet carry" table carried the row *"the census of diagnostic codes the
    frontend can emit, and the repair each carries → `M1.12.3`"*, so a reader who needed to know what
    `read-unclosed-list` means was sent to the code. ⛔ **The census pattern was measured before it
    was written, and the obvious one is worthless.** A pattern requiring the code on the constructor's
    own line — the grep shape `Diagnostic::error("…"` — finds **0 of 7** codes in
    `crates/eadl-front/src/reader.rs`, **0 of 24** in `crates/eadl-front/src/module.rs` and **0 of 22**
    in `crates/eadl-model/src/kind.rs`, because every call in this codebase puts the code on the *next*
    line. A leg built on it would not have reported a short list; it would have reported that the code
    and the document agree perfectly, having compared nothing with nothing. Measured with a `python3`
    instrument over the four candidate sources, including what dropping the `#[cfg(test)]` cut would
    add: for the two sources declared today, nothing; for `diagnostic.rs`, `read-example` and
    `read-unclosed-list` alongside `e` and `w`, which are test locals and not codes at all.
  - [x] **ROOT CAUSE (WHY + WHERE)** — WHERE: `docs/semantics/reference.md` had no §4, and
    `crates/eadl-front/tests/reference.rs` had no leg whose population was the code. WHY it matters:
    a diagnostic code *is* a rule of the language — it is the only place the language says what an
    author did wrong — and a rule that lives solely in a `Diagnostic::error(` call is a rule no
    document states, so renaming one silently invalidates every book chapter, help text and issue
    report that cites it. WHY the naive census fails: a producer→document leg has an **empty-set fixed
    point**. If the scanner finds nothing, every document row is unexplained by nothing and the leg
    reports agreement. The scanner's shape is therefore part of the claim, exactly as `M1.24` recorded
    for figure sweeps.
  - [x] **ADDRESSED (verified)** — §4 now states every code with when it fires and what to do, and the
    reference declares the sources it is normative over in a machine-read table rather than in prose.
    `cargo test -p eadl-front --test reference` → **19 passed; 0 failed** (was 11): two new green legs
    and six new RED arms. ⭐ **The two census legs pin the code set exactly, and that is stronger
    evidence than either alone.** Every code `reader.rs` emits has a row, and every row is a code some
    declared source emits — so a scanner that missed a code would leave that row stated-but-unemitted
    and fail leg 5, and a scanner that invented one would fail leg 4. A green run is therefore
    evidence about the *scanner* and not only about the table. Measured independently: the `python3`
    census of `reader.rs`'s production half and the Rust scanner agree on the same seven codes
    (`read-bad-escape`, `read-malformed-number`, `read-number-overflow`, `read-unclosed-list`,
    `read-unexpected-character`, `read-unexpected-close`, `read-unterminated-string`), and
    `form.rs` emits none, which is why §3 cites it for canonical form rather than for diagnostics.
  - [x] **NO REGRESSION** — `make focused` → `tier focused: passed — 3 passed, 0 failed, 0
    unavailable, 0 not built`; `cargo test --all` → **456 passed, 0 failed over 37 suites** against a
    baseline of 448 over 37, the delta being exactly the two legs and six arms;
    `bash scripts/check_doctrines.sh` → `=== all doctrines green ===` over 13 checks (it first reported
    one breach — `KNOWLEDGE-MAP` out of sync after `knowledge-map/subsystems.md` was edited — and was
    re-run green after regenerating, which is the gate working rather than a flake).
    `git diff --stat HEAD -- crates/eadl-front/src crates/eadl-model/src` is **empty**: no production
    code changed in this leaf, so the resolution was reached by writing the rule down and checking it,
    not by moving code to match a document.
  - [x] **FIX** — at the lowest level that actually works. `emitted_diagnostics()` scans for
    `Diagnostic::` and takes the first string literal after the opening parenthesis, so it is
    indifferent to how the call is formatted; it cuts at `#[cfg(test)]`; and it records **severity**
    with the code, which is what makes §4 rule 1 ("every diagnostic the frontend emits is an error") a
    checked claim rather than an observation. `declared_sources()` reads the population **out of the
    document**, so adding a source is a reviewable edit to the reference and not a change to a list
    inside a test. A guard reports when the declared sources emit nothing at all, because that is the
    failure the measurement above says is one pattern-width away. `citation_violations()` mirrors
    `scripts/check_book_anchors.sh`'s `PATH_RE` — same directories, same root documents, same
    trailing-slash and `/*` handling — rather than inventing a second notion of a citation. 📌
    **Considered and deferred:** an executable `fires on` column, giving each code an input that must
    produce it. It would make every row runnable like the literal tables and would catch a diagnostic

    tests. Recorded here rather than silently dropped; `M1.12.5`'s closure review decides whether the

  ### Lesson promotion

  `a census pattern that finds nothing is indistinguishable from an empty population, and a gate built
  on it passes by comparing two empty sets` → `promotion: promoted (docs/knowledge/a-moved-measurement-needs-a-census-of-its-copies.md
  — no new card, because that card already carries the question "My census grep found nothing — is the
  population empty, or is my pattern wrong?" and a near-duplicate would split the answer. Its item 1
  gains the measured instance (0 of 7, 0 of 24, 0 of 22 for the same-line pattern) and the two
  defences that generalise: assert the population is non-empty, and run both directions, because a
  producer→document leg alone has an empty-set fixed point and the mirrored leg does not.)`

  ### `M1.12`'s acceptance, re-checked criterion by criterion

  The parent's three criteria, each answered with a measurement rather than a judgement, because a
  closure review that agrees with the work it is reviewing is not a review.

  | Criterion | Verdict | Evidence |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | "every rule the frontend enforces is stated normatively and cites the code or corpus that enforces it" | **met, and the population is measured rather than asserted** | the frontend is `crates/eadl-front/src/`, six files. A census over every production half — multi-line safe, cut at `#[cfg(test)]` — finds `Diagnostic::error(` in exactly two of them: `reader.rs` (7 distinct codes from 11 call sites) and `module.rs` (24). `form.rs`, `diagnostic.rs`, `lib.rs` and `source.rs` emit none. All 31 are §4 rows, and legs 4/5 pin the set **exactly** in both directions: 53 stated, 53 emitted over the four declared sources, **0 rotted rows**. The reference exceeds the criterion by declaring `crates/eadl-model/src/kind.rs`, whose 22 `schema-*` codes are language rules about `defkind` even though the crate is not the frontend |
  | "`BOOK-ANCHORS` covers it" | **met, and it did not before this leaf** | `git show HEAD:scripts/check_book_anchors.sh \| grep -c 'docs/semantics'` → `0`. Now `book-anchors: OK (19 chapter(s), 2 normative document(s))` with six arms. ⚠️ The criterion is weaker than it reads and `M1.23` said so on this very leaf: the doctrine proves a citation resolves, never that the rule is true. The truth leg is `reference.rs`, which is why legs 8 and 9 were added here rather than the doctrine being widened further |
  | "nothing in the reference is true only of the current implementation" | **met, with two exceptions that are stated as limits and owned** | §1 rule 9 (nothing at or above 2^63 is writable) and §3's F-G limit (a control character with no escape prints raw) *are* implementation-shaped, and both are marked ⚠️, both name `M1.13` as the freeze that settles them, and both are recorded on that leaf as findings F-F and F-G so they cannot be lost. A third was found by this review and is now stated the same way: the declaration's scope boundary, which names `M1.26` |

  ⛔ **Two things this re-check found that are *not* in the parent's criteria, filed rather than
  folded in.** Neither is a reason to hold `M1.12` open, because the criteria above are met on their own
  terms — but both are real, and both are the shape this tree has been burned by twice already: a claim
  true when written and false after the population moved, with nothing re-deriving it. They are `M1.26`'s
  gaps (a) and (b), with their censuses and their reproduce commands on that leaf.

  ### Lesson promotion

  `a census whose pattern is a subset of its population reports the subset as though it were the whole`
  → `promotion: promoted (docs/knowledge/a-moved-measurement-needs-a-census-of-its-copies.md — no new
  card, because that card already asks the question this answers. It listed two answers, "the population
  is empty" and "the pattern is wrong"; today's is a third, and the dangerous one, because the pattern
  is *right*: `(read|module|schema)-…` found seven real citations and the population held fourteen. Its
  item gains the third answer and the defence that generalises — census the population before the
  pattern, so a subset is visible as one.)`

- ID: `M1.13`
  Status: `done`
  Goal: version the language and freeze the first compatibility baseline (§15, §12 M1).
  Acceptance: the surface carries a version (`eadl/1`); the existing corpora become that version's
  conformance suite; a migration note is required for any change to a frozen construct, and a
  check enforces it.
  Children: `M1.13.1` … `M1.13.5`

  ⚠️ Gated on `M1.11` and `M1.12`: there is nothing to freeze until the grammar and the reference
  exist, and freezing an implementation rather than a specification is what this sequence exists
  to avoid. Both are `done`, so the gate is met and this leaf is `active`.

  ### Two findings routed here by `M1.12`, because freezing is what settles them

  Both are limits of the *value domain*, measured by the 39-literal probe on `2026-09-28`, and neither
  can be fixed by a sentence in the reference — each changes what the language can express, which is
  exactly the decision a compatibility baseline makes once and for all. ⛔ **Re-measured `2026-09-28`
  before this decomposition, and both measurements moved the finding**: F-F's recorded impact rests on
  a premise the pinned ISA spec contradicts, and F-G's fix shape is constrained by an enforcement
  boundary the routing did not consider.

  - **F-F — no value at or above 2^63 is writable.** `Form::Integer` holds an `i64`, so
    `0x8000_0000_0000_0000` is refused as `read-number-overflow` (re-measured, M-A). ⛔ **The impact
    `M1.12` recorded is corrected here rather than carried forward.** It said a memory-mapped base
    address in the upper half of a 64-bit address space cannot be described, and that `M2.8.2`'s
    device-tree fixture is where that would first matter. The premise is false for every standardized
    target (M-D): RV64's PMP registers encode bits 55-2 of a **56-bit** physical address, Sv39 and
    Sv48 support a 56-bit physical address space, Sv57 is **57-bit virtual**, and Sv64 is reserved and
    unimplemented — so no address a standardized target can have reaches 2^57, against a domain limit
    of 2^63−1. The finding stands as a stated limit of the version; the urgency routed with it does
    not. Owner: `M1.13.2`.
    ✅ **SETTLED by `M1.13.2`, and its own correction was corrected in turn.** The domain stays exact
    signed 64-bit for `eadl/1`, now as a **stated property of the version** (§1 rule 9) with the
    widening path recorded in `docs/decisions/decision_eadl1-value-domain.md`. ⛔ The sentence above
    did not survive re-derivation either: "no address a standardized target can have reaches 2^57" is
    true of *physical* addresses and false of canonical *high-half virtual* ones, which are refused
    today in their unsigned spelling under Sv39, Sv48 and Sv57 alike (N-D), and Sv64 is not merely
    reserved — the pin says it "will be defined in a later version". The decision is unchanged and now
    rests on a reason that survives the measurement: a widening is compatible, every value that needs
    one is already reachable as a signed value, and §1 rule 10 states the cost out loud with two rows
    executing it.
  - **F-G — a control character other than `\n \t \r` cannot be escaped.** `write_canonical` escapes
    four characters and copies everything else, so a string holding a raw control byte prints it into
    canonical text (re-measured, M-B: ESC, BEL and **NUL** each read cleanly and each print raw).
    `M1.12.1` fixed the one case that had an escape to use (`\r`, finding F-D). Three things the
    routing did not carry, each measured below: the reader *already* refuses the same byte between
    forms, so this is an inconsistency and not a missing rule (M-C); both legs that check §3 rule 3
    read populations that cannot contain the forbidden byte, so the rule is green by construction
    (M-E); and `Form::Str` is constructible outside the frontend, so refusing input cannot be the
    whole fix (M-F). Owner: `M1.13.1`.

  ### Measurement — added `2026-09-28`, before any child was written

  ⛔ Measured with tools first (`TOOLBOX.md`), the discipline `M1.12`'s decomposition set: the probe
  ran before a sentence of design was drafted, and it changed two claims this leaf already carried.
  Instrument: the repository's own `diagnose` example (`cargo run -q -p eadl-front --example diagnose`)
  over scratch fixtures under `target/tmp/m113/` — untracked, repository-derived, on-volume (§13) —
  plus shell and `python3` censuses over the whole population of each claim.

  | # | Measured | Command | Result |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | M-A | F-F reproduces exactly as routed | `diagnose target/tmp/m113/f-f.eadl` holding `(probe 0x8000_0000_0000_0000)`, and `max-ok.eadl` holding `0x7fff_ffff_ffff_ffff` | `error[read-number-overflow]`, hint "eADL integers are exact 64-bit signed values"; the maximum readable value canonicalizes to `9223372036854775807` |
  | M-B | F-G is reachable from **source text**, NUL included | `diagnose` on `(probe "a<0x1b>b")`, `(probe "a<0x07>b")`, `(probe "a<0x00>b")`, each fixture checked with `od -c` first | all three `read cleanly: 1 top-level form(s)` with no diagnostic, and canonical text carries the byte raw — so the artifact §12 M4 hashes can hold a NUL |
  | M-C | the reader **already** refuses that byte between forms | `diagnose` on `(a)<0x1b>(b)` | `error[read-unexpected-character]: \`\u{1b}\` cannot start a form`. `reader.rs:428` names "a stray control character" and §4 of the reference states the row, so the language has the rule and applies it in one position only |
  | M-D | no standardized RISC-V address reaches the domain limit | read-only `chipdoc` at pin `v20260120`: `$ARCHOGEN_CHIPDOC_ROOT/risc-v/isa/pinned/v20260120/priv/machine.html` and `…/supervisor.html`, text extracted and searched for `<n>-bit physical` / `<n>-bit virtual` | "For RV64, each PMP address register encodes bits 55-2 of a **56-bit physical address**"; "The Sv39 and Sv48 page-based virtual-memory schemes … support a **56-bit physical address space**, so the RV64 PMP address registers impose the same limit"; Sv57 "supports 57-bit virtual address spaces"; Sv64 "Reserved". ⛔ **The quotations hold and the row's claim does not — corrected by `M1.13.2`'s N-C/N-D.** It is true of every *physical* address and false of every canonical *high-half virtual* one: an Sv39 kernel address has bits 63-39 all set, so its magnitude is 2^64−2^38 against a limit of 2^63−1, and `0xFFFF_FC00_0000_0000`, `0xFFFF_FFFF_C000_0000` and `0xFFFF_FF80_0000_0000` are each refused today. Sv64 is also stronger than "Reserved": the same pin says it "**will be defined in a later version of this specification**". The decision is unchanged, the reason for it is not the one recorded here — see `docs/decisions/decision_eadl1-value-domain.md` |
  | M-E | the corpus holds no raw control byte, so both §3-rule-3 legs are green by construction | the two censuses below | **0 of 75** tracked `.eadl` files hold a control byte other than `\n \t \r`; the two legs read the reference's literal table and the corpus, and neither population can contain the byte they forbid |
  | M-F | `Form::Str` is constructible with no reader involved | `crates/eadl-front/src/lib.rs` re-exports `Form` and `Span`; `Form`'s variant fields inherit its `pub` visibility; `Span::new` is `pub const fn` | any consumer in the workspace can build a string value holding a control byte and call `to_canonical()` on it, so §3 rule 3 is enforceable only **in the printer** — refusing input covers one route into the value |
  | M-G | the domain limit sits far above every value the language is used to write | census below | ⛔ **Superseded by `M1.13.2`'s N-A on re-derivation, and recorded here rather than quietly retyped.** As originally taken: "27 distinct integer literals in the whole tracked corpus; the largest is `0x1000_0000` (2^28, a memory-mapped base) and the largest decimal is 2^32" — two defects in one row: the population's scope was never stated (27 is the *non-negative* count; the whole population holds 28, the extra being `-1`), and a radix-scoped maximum was published as the overall one (2^32 > 2^28, so the row contradicts itself). Re-derived through the frontend: **24** distinct values over **194** occurrences, largest **4294967296 = 2^32**, largest hexadecimal `0x1000_0000` = 2^28, **0** literals refused by the domain. The conclusion M-G was taken for — the limit sits far above every value in use — is **confirmed and strengthened**; the figure is not |
  | M-H | the frozen population, enumerated | census below | **62** corpus files under the two roots the tests already walk, **13** excluded feedback evidence files, **1** EBNF fence, **5** machine-read tables, **2** kind declarations |

  The censuses M-E, M-G and M-H rest on, so each is re-runnable rather than asserted:

  ```text
  census: for f in $(git ls-files '*.eadl'); do LC_ALL=C grep -qP '[\x00-\x08\x0b\x0c\x0e-\x1f\x7f]' "$f" && echo "HIT $f"; done
          → no HIT over 75 files (M-E)
  census: git ls-files '*.eadl' | wc -l                                                  → 75
          git ls-files 'docs/semantics/*.eadl' 'examples/*.eadl' | wc -l                  → 62 (M-H, the suite population)
          git ls-files 'docs/feedback/**/*.eadl' | wc -l                                  → 13 (M-H, excluded)
  census: grep -c '^```ebnf' docs/semantics/grammar.md                                    → 1
          grep -c 'machine-read:' docs/semantics/reference.md                             → 5 tables + 1 prose mention
          git ls-files 'docs/semantics/kinds/*.eadl'                                      → core.eadl, os-rt.eadl (M-H)
  census: every hex and decimal literal in every tracked .eadl, deduplicated and sorted by value
          → 27 distinct, largest 0x1000_0000 and 4294967296 (M-G)
          ⛔ NOT A COMMAND, and that is the defect. It describes a pipeline nobody can re-run, so its
          scope ("non-negative") and its radix-qualified maximum were both unrecoverable — which is how
          seven restatements came to publish "the largest literal is 2^28". Superseded by a tracked
          instrument that asks the frontend instead of a second regular expression:
          cargo run -q -p eadl-front --example literals -- docs/semantics examples docs/feedback
          → 194 occurrences, 24 distinct values, largest 4294967296 (2^32), largest hexadecimal
            0x1000_0000 (2^28), smallest -1, and 0 literals the domain refused  (M1.13.2 N-A)
  ```

  ⭐ **Why the exclusion in M-H is a finding and not a detail.** The 13 files under
  `docs/feedback/linkedspec/issues/*/evidence/` are deliberately malformed reproductions of reported
  LinkedSpec defects, frozen and owned by `FEEDBACK-SELF-CONTAINED`. Neither corpus walk has ever
  reached them — both start at `docs/semantics` and `examples` (`conformance.rs:496`,
  `reference.rs:473`) — so the suite population is 62 and not 75. `M1.13.4` must state that exclusion
  and enforce it, because a manifest that swept them in would make five withdrawn-or-fixed upstream
  bug reports into conformance cases of a language version.

  ### Closure review — this leaf's own acceptance, criterion by criterion, `2026-09-29`

  | Criterion | Verdict | What closes it |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | the surface carries a version (`eadl/1`) | ✅ | `(eadl-version eadl/1)` is a **form** — §8 of the reference, an executed table, five §4 rows, one normative source (`M1.13.3`) — and **every description the repository ships states it**, so §8 rule 2's justifying population is a census of 13 frozen files rather than a claim (`M1.13.4.2`) |
  | the existing corpora become that version's conformance suite | ✅ | `docs/semantics/conformance.md`: one machine-read manifest naming the roots, what each proves, the version and the exclusions, with **no count in it**, one reader every walk goes through, and four rules executed in both directions (`M1.13.4.4`) |
  | a migration note is required for any change to a frozen construct | ✅ | the note form, the four sections it carries, and the pending/applied lifecycle in `docs/semantics/migrations/README.md`; any movement needs one, **including a correction** (`M1.13.5`) |
  | and a check enforces it | ✅ | `LANGUAGE-FREEZE`, two legs — integrity against a fresh run and explicitness against `HEAD`'s, which is the leg that stops `--emit` being the waiver — registered in `check_doctrines.project.sh`, mirrored in `DOCTRINE_ENFORCEMENT.md`, nine RED arms, one of them against the real tree (`M1.13.5`) |

  ⭐ **And the two findings routed here are both settled, neither as routed.** F-F stayed 64-bit but on a
  reason that survives measurement, after **both** figures it arrived with were re-derived as false
  (`M1.13.2`, `docs/decisions/decision_eadl1-value-domain.md`); F-G turned out to be three defects rather
  than one, closed by executed rows and a total escape rule in the printer (`M1.13.1`). Five children
  found three defects no acceptance criterion asked for — a **production** refusal in the kind registry
  that made `archogen` answer `tool-failure` for every description, a normative sentence false for the one
  commit it existed, and five live false figures in the book and the grammar, the last of which is
  `M1.27`. Baseline at closure: **535 passed / 0 failed** over 39 suites, from 476 when this leaf opened.

  ### Decomposition — added `2026-09-28`, five children

  This leaf is five children rather than one, for the reason `M1.12`'s decomposition gave and the
  measurements above repeat: **two of the acceptance criteria change the language, and a leaf that both
  changes a construct and freezes it cannot show a clean before→after for either.** The baseline must be
  written *after* the last language change lands, or the freeze records a construct the same commit then
  edits. So the order is fixed by dependency, not by preference: settle the two value-domain findings,
  put the version on the surface, enumerate and digest the suite, and only then gate it.

  | Child | Settles | Why in this order |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | `M1.13.1` | F-G — canonical form becomes total, and the control-character rule becomes uniform | a language change; must land before any baseline digests the grammar, the reference tables or the corpus |
  | `M1.13.2` | F-F — the value domain becomes a stated property of the version | a language *statement* rather than a change; also lands before the baseline, because §1's rows are frozen constructs |
  | `M1.13.3` | the version identifier on the surface | the baseline names a version, so the identifier has to exist first |
  | `M1.13.4` | the corpora become `eadl/1`'s conformance suite, and the baseline instrument is written | needs `.1`–`.3` finished so the digests are of the frozen language and not of a language still moving |
  | `M1.13.5` | the freeze gate and the migration-note requirement | a gate with no baseline has nothing to compare; `.4` writes the baseline, `.5` enforces it |

  ⚠️ **None of the five displaces `M1.25` or `M1.26`, and neither of those blocks any of the five.**
  `M1.26` says so on its own leaf and the reasoning holds from this side: the freeze settles the
  *language*, while gap (a) is about the model layer's diagnostics — which are not language constructs
  and are not in the frozen population M-H enumerates — and gap (b) is about evidence for rules already
  stated. ⛔ One real interaction, recorded so `.1` does not trip on it: `M1.26`'s gap (b) needs notation
  for a control character to write its `fires on` column, and `read-unexpected-character` is one of the
  25 codes no test names. `M1.13.1` supplies exactly that notation, so `.1` unblocks part of `M1.26`
  rather than competing with it.
