- ID: `M1.13.1`
  Status: `done`
  Goal: settle **F-G** — make canonical form total, so §3 rule 3 ("it carries no control character")
  holds for every value the printer can be given rather than for every value two populations happen to
  contain, and make the control-character rule uniform across the surface.
  Acceptance: the grammar's `escape` production admits a general escape and the recognizer derived from
  it agrees with the reader on every probe; §2 of the reference states what that escape denotes and §3
  states the total escaping rule, both as executed table rows; a raw control character inside a string
  is refused with a localized diagnostic, which is the rule `reader.rs:428` already applies between
  forms and not a new prohibition; canonical text of **any** string value carries no control byte and
  re-reads to a structurally equal value (§3 rule 5); RED arms whose fixtures *are* raw control bytes,
  including NUL, so the coverage hole M-E measured cannot reopen; `make focused` exit `0`.
  Priority: **high** — this is the only one of the five with a live hazard behind it: canonical text is
  the artifact §12 M4 hashes and compares, and M-B shows it can carry a NUL today with no diagnostic.

  ### Design constraints, recorded so the child does not have to re-derive them

  - ⛔ **The escape must be in the language, not only in the printer.** §3 rule 5 requires canonical
    text to read back to a structurally equal document. A printer that escapes a control byte into a
    notation the reader refuses produces canonical text that is not a description — which would trade
    F-G for a round-trip defect. This is why the reference's "either a general escape **or** a rule that
    a description may not contain one" is not a free choice: the second alone leaves the printer
    non-total, and M-F shows a value can reach the printer without passing the reader.
  - ⭐ **Both legs, and the order matters.** Refusing the byte at read time closes the route through
    source text; making the printer total closes every route. `docs/knowledge/make-the-rule-a-constructor-precondition.md`
    is the card: look for the illegal *state* (canonical text holding a control byte) rather than the
    illegal *action* (someone typing one into a file), and refuse it where the state is produced.
  - ⚠️ **The grammar change is a frozen-construct change and `.4` has not run yet**, so it costs no
    migration note. That is the whole reason `.1` is sequenced first, and it is the last moment the
    escape set can be widened for free.
  - Surrogates (`D800`–`DFFF`) are not Unicode scalar values and a Rust `char` cannot hold one, so the
    general escape must refuse them explicitly rather than panic or truncate. Whichever notation is
    chosen, `decode_escapes` and `encode_canonical` in `crates/eadl-front/tests/common/reference_table.rs`
    are the shared pair that must stay inverse — the existing legs already read both.

  ### Acceptance Checklist (enforced by `TASK-ACCEPTANCE`)

  - [x] **REPRODUCE / ISSUE** — measured on the unmodified tree, with the fixtures checked byte-first
    (`od -c`) so the byte probed is the byte claimed. `diagnose` on `(probe "a<ESC>b")`,
    `(probe "a<BEL>b")` and `(probe "a<NUL>b")` each printed `read cleanly: 1 top-level form(s)` with
    **no diagnostic**, and each printed the byte raw into canonical text — the NUL case truncated the
    captured output of the very test that reported it (`grep` called its own input a *binary file*).
    The same byte *between* forms was already refused: `(a)<ESC>(b)` →
    `error[read-unexpected-character]: \`\u{1b}\` cannot start a form`.
  - [x] **ROOT CAUSE (WHY + WHERE)** — two mechanisms, and the second is why the first survived every
    gate. **WHERE (1):** `crates/eadl-front/src/form.rs`, `write_canonical`'s `Self::Str` arm — four
    named escapes and `_ => out.push(ch)`, so any control character outside the four is copied. §3
    rule 3 of the reference claims "it carries no control character", which was true only of the
    characters that had names. **WHERE (2):** `crates/eadl-front/src/reader.rs`, `read_string`'s `_`
    arm — "Copy one whole character" with no control test, while the top-level dispatch at
    `reader.rs:428` refuses exactly that byte as "a stray control character". **WHY nothing caught
    it:** the two legs that check §3 rule 3 read populations that cannot contain the byte.
    ```text
    census: for f in $(git ls-files '*.eadl'); do LC_ALL=C grep -qP '[\x00-\x08\x0b\x0c\x0e-\x1f\x7f]' "$f" && echo HIT; done
            → no HIT over 75 files, so the coverage leg's population is empty of the case
    census: the other leg's population is §2's own table, written in the language's escape notation,
            so no cell could hold a raw byte either — and `encode_canonical` in the shared helper
            mirrored the printer's four arms exactly, agreeing with the defect rather than testing it
    ```
    So §2 rule 5 ("a character that has no escape cannot be written into a string") was a **false
    normative sentence**: measured, `(probe "a<ESC>b")` wrote one. `docs/knowledge/a-gate-is-only-as-sharp-as-its-fixtures.md`
    and `docs/knowledge/make-the-rule-a-constructor-precondition.md` are the two cards, and the third
    fact is new: **the second implementation was a copy of the hole**, so "two implementations agree"
    was not evidence.
  - [x] **ADDRESSED (verified)** — the same fixtures, after:
    ```text
    BEFORE  (probe "a<ESC>b") → read cleanly, canonical carries the raw byte, no diagnostic
    AFTER   (probe "a<ESC>b") → error[read-control-character]: a string cannot hold a raw control
                                character; `\u{1b}` writes this one
    AFTER   (probe "a<BEL>b") → error[read-control-character] … `\u{7}` writes this one
    AFTER   (probe "a<NUL>b") → error[read-control-character] … `\u{0}` writes this one
    AFTER   (probe "a\u{1b}b") → read cleanly; canonical: (probe "a\u{1b}b")
    AFTER   (probe "a\u{0}b")  → read cleanly; canonical: (probe "a\u{0}b")   ← no raw NUL
    AFTER   (probe "a\u{d800}b") → error[read-escape-out-of-range]: `\u{d800}` names no character
    ```
    Both legs fire on the rows rather than on prose: adding four raw-control rows to §2 **before** any
    production code moved gave `the_grammar_and_the_reference_agree_on_the_literal_space` **4
    violations** naming exactly those rows, and the reader leg **3** (the line-feed row is the
    `read-unterminated-string` arm's, not this one's). After the fix both are green.
    **474 passed / 0 failed over 37 suites** (baseline 471 over 37; delta = `arm_4`, `arm_5` and the
    new printer test). `make focused` → `passed — 3 passed, 0 failed`.
    `book-anchors: OK (19 chapter(s), 2 normative document(s))`, self-test `6 pass / 0 fail`.
  - [x] **NO REGRESSION** — both mutations seen firing and both restorations proven byte-identical,
    per `docs/knowledge/verify-the-mutation-applied.md`:
    ```text
    mutation A: delete `_ if ch.is_control() => …` from form.rs
      → `test result: FAILED. 49 passed; 1 failed`, the failure being
        `form::tests::canonical_text_escapes_control_characters_that_never_passed_a_reader`
      → restored: `diff -q` silent, `test result: ok. 50 passed; 0 failed`
    mutation B: `if ch.is_control() && ch != '\t'` → `if false && ch.is_control()` in reader.rs
      → the reader leg fires on §2 rows 217/218/219 — `<0x1b>`, `<0x0>`, `<0x9f>` — each
        "the reference refuses … as `read-control-character`, and the frontend did not"
      → conformance stayed `12 passed`, correctly: it compares grammar with recognizer, not reader
      → restored: `diff -q` silent
    ```
    `cargo test --all` → 474/0 over 37 suites; `make focused` → `passed — 3 / 0 / 0`;
    `scripts/check_book_anchors.sh` → OK, `--self-test` → 6/6; `knowledge-map` check silent at `rc=0`.
    No corpus file, example or fixture changed: `git status --short` lists the two production sources,
    three test files, the two normative documents and one book chapter.
  - [x] **FIX** — made at the producer, not the door, because `Form::Str` is constructible outside the
    frontend and need never pass a reader. (1) `form.rs`: the printer's fall-through now escapes every
    Unicode `Cc` character as `\u{…}`, keeping the four named escapes because `\r` is more readable
    than `\u{d}`. (2) `reader.rs`: `\u{…}` parsed — one to six hexadecimal digits, surrogates and
    code points past `10ffff` refused as `read-escape-out-of-range` (`refused`, well-formed and out of
    domain, exactly the `read-number-overflow` shape) and a malformed one as `read-bad-escape`
    (`error`, not well-formed); a raw control character other than tab refused as
    `read-control-character`, which is the rule `reader.rs:428` already applied between forms.
    (3) `grammar.md`: `escape` gains `unicode_escape`, and `string_char` gains `- control`, a new
    production naming `Cc` minus tab in the language's own escape notation. (4) The shared helper's
    `decode_escapes` / `encode_canonical` / `control_character` — the deliberately independent second
    implementations — rewritten from the reference, and `control_character` widened from
    `is_ascii_control` to `is_control` so the C1 range is inside the rule rather than outside it.
  - [x] **LOCKSTEP** — §2 rules 1, 2 and 5 rewritten so rule 5 is *true* rather than deleted, with
    eleven new executed rows; §3 rule 3 restated as total and its ⚠️ known-limit paragraph replaced by
    the closure and a new, measured honest limit; §4 gains two rows and two existing rows gain
    precision; the notation table gains `<0xNN>`. `grammar.md`'s notation table corrected on two
    counts and its `\0` rationale narrowed. `docs/book/src/reading.md`'s canonical-form section now
    tells the reader what they may write. `MEMORY.md`, `LIVE_STATUS.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`,
    `DEV_NOTES.md`, `docs/TASK_TREE.md`, this tree. `M1.26`'s gap (b) gains a note that its notation
    problem is solved.

  ### Four things this slice found that were not in its acceptance criteria

  ⛔ Each was measured, and each is corrected in place rather than left as a comment, because all four
  are false sentences in normative or near-normative surfaces — the class `M1.23` and `M1.24` exist for.

  1. **§2 rule 5 of the reference was false.** "A character that has no escape cannot be written into a
     string" — `(probe "a<ESC>b")` wrote one, cleanly. Fixed by making the code match the sentence and
     then restating the sentence as two halves (closed *and* sufficient), which is the stronger claim.
  2. **§3 rule 3 named `provenance.rs` as held to the same rule, and it is not.** That `quote()` writes
     **JSON** — `\b`, `\f`, `\u00XX` — so its output is deliberately not eADL text and was never held
     to this rule. The property the two share is that neither lets a raw control byte survive; the
     escape *sets* differ because the target languages differ. Corrected, with the reason kept, because
     `M1.12.1` used that file as evidence for F-D and the evidence was sounder than the sentence.
  3. **`grammar.md`'s notation table said `a - b` excludes single characters only.** `symbol_char =
     any - whitespace - …` already excluded a rule whose expansion is four characters, and this slice
     needed the general form for `control`. The table now says what the recognizer does.
  4. **The grammar accepted a raw line feed inside a string while the reader refused it.** A live
     grammar/reader disagreement that no probe and no corpus file could reach, because a corpus file
     containing one would not be a corpus file. `- control` closes it, and row `"a<0xa>b"` pins it.

  ⭐ **One question routed onward, measured and sized rather than raised as a worry.** Unicode `Cc` is
  not the whole of "invisible": a zero-width space, a BOM or a bidirectional override prints raw and
  nothing refuses it. Census: **0 of 75** tracked descriptions hold one, and every non-ASCII character
  the corpus does hold is visible (`§` `—` `…` `≤` `⛔` `⭐`). Stated as §3's honest limit and routed to
  `M1.13.3`, which decides what a description may contain at the same time as it decides what one must
  declare — and which is the last child that can narrow the language for free.

  ### Lesson promotion

  `a second implementation copied from the first is a mirror, not a check` →
  `promotion: declined (three already-recorded cards carry it, and a fourth entry answering the same
  question makes the retrievable layer harder to search: the population that could not reach the rule
  is docs/knowledge/a-gate-is-only-as-sharp-as-its-fixtures.md, the fix belonging at the producer
  rather than the door is docs/knowledge/make-the-rule-a-constructor-precondition.md, and the false
  sentence beside enforced data is docs/knowledge/prose-beside-data-goes-unenforced.md. What is new
  here is a *degree* rather than a kind — the helper was written as an independent second
  implementation and had drifted into mirroring the omission it was there to catch — and that degree is
  recorded where the next reader meets it, in `encode_canonical`'s own doc comment.)`

  Verification: see the acceptance checklist above.
  Commit: `ARCHOGEN-M1-0080 (leaf M1.13.1)`

- ID: `M1.13.2`
  Status: `done`
  Goal: settle **F-F** — state the value domain as a property of the language version rather than as an
  implementation limit, on the evidence M-D and M-G measured, and record the widening path so a future
  version can take it without re-arguing the question.
  Acceptance: a `docs/decisions/` record carries the decision with both measurements and their
  re-runnable commands, and is indexed; §1 of the reference states the domain normatively, so the ⚠️
  "implementation-shaped" marker `M1.12.5`'s closure review pinned to it is replaced by a stated rule;
  the `refused read-number-overflow` rows keep pinning the boundary in both directions; the recorded
  consequence names the one compatibility-corpus row a later widening would add (a literal refused
  under `eadl/1` and readable under the version that widens); `make focused` exit `0`.
  Priority: **medium** — no live defect. The value of this child is that the limit stops being an
  implementation accident and becomes a decision someone can find, with the measurement that justified
  it attached.

  ⭐ **Two acceptance criteria this leaf carried were extended when its own evidence was re-derived, and
  both extensions are recorded here rather than made silently.** The criterion "their re-runnable
  commands" was not satisfiable as written: M-G's recorded command is a *description* of a census
  ("every hex and decimal literal … deduplicated and sorted by value"), not one, which is why nobody
  could re-run it and catch what re-running it finds. So this child also lands a **tracked instrument**
  (`crates/eadl-front/examples/literals.rs`, named in `TOOLBOX.md`) that asks the frontend rather than a
  second regular expression, and the criterion "keep pinning the boundary in both directions" is
  measured rather than assumed — the hexadecimal lower boundary was pinned by nothing.

  ### Measurement — taken `2026-09-28` before a sentence of the record was drafted

  ⛔ Both of M-D and M-G were **re-derived rather than carried forward**, per `docs/CLAIM_VERIFICATION.md`
  leg 1, and both re-derivations moved. The auditor's asymmetry applies and is honoured: a re-derivation
  contradicts a figure that has at least been read, so each disagreement below is attributed by
  measurement, not announced.

  | # | Re-derived | Command | Result |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | N-A | M-G's figure, from the whole population | the instrument below over `docs/semantics`, `examples` and `docs/feedback` | ⛔ **28** distinct integer values (**27** excluding the one negative, `-1`), and the largest is **4294967296 = 2^32**, not 2^28. `0x1000_0000` is the largest *hexadecimal* literal. M-G's own row said "the largest is `0x1000_0000` … and the largest decimal is 2^32" — two radix-scoped maxima, self-contradictory as one sentence — and **all 7 restatements** dropped the qualifier |
  | N-B | the copies of that figure, in both directions | `grep -rn '2\^28\|27 distinct\|largest literal' --include='*.md' .` outside `target/` and `vendor/` | **7** occurrences over **3** live surfaces: this tree (5 — M-G's row, its census block, `.2`'s argument, the frontier row, Decisions), `docs/TASK_TREE.md` (1), `MEMORY.md` (1). No occurrence in the book, the reference or the grammar |
  | N-C | M-D, from the pinned spec at `v20260120` | read-only `chipdoc`: `priv/machine.html` and `priv/supervisor.html`, text extracted and searched for `<n>-bit physical` / `<n>-bit virtual` / `Sv64` | confirmed as recorded — "For RV64, each PMP address register encodes bits 55-2 of a **56-bit physical address**"; Sv39/Sv48 "support a **56-bit physical address space**"; Sv57 "supports **57-bit** virtual address spaces" — ⛔ and **sharpened**: Sv64 is not merely reserved, the spec says it "**will be defined in a later version of this specification**" |
  | N-D | whether any *standardized* address reaches the domain limit | `diagnose` over `0xFFFF_FC00_0000_0000`, `0xFFFF_FFFF_C000_0000`, `0xFFFF_FF80_0000_0000`, `0x00FF_FFFF_FFFF_FFFF`, `-1073741824` | ⛔ **M-D's conclusion is true of physical addresses and false of virtual ones.** Every canonical *high-half* RV64 virtual address is refused today — the first three are `error[read-number-overflow]`, because an Sv39 kernel address has bits 63-39 all set and so a magnitude of 2^64−2^38 against a limit of 2^63−1. The 56-bit physical maximum reads cleanly. Each high-half address *is* representable as a signed value (`-1073741824` reads cleanly), so no value is lost — only the unsigned spelling a datasheet or a linker script prints |
  | N-E | the boundary, in every spelling the language has | `diagnose` over nine fixtures: `±` the decimal endpoints, `±` one past each, and the hexadecimal pair | correct in **all four** directions: `9223372036854775807` and `-9223372036854775808` read cleanly, `9223372036854775808` and `-9223372036854775809` are refused, `0x7fff_ffff_ffff_ffff` and `-0x8000_0000_0000_0000` read cleanly, `0x8000_0000_0000_0000` and `-0x8000_0000_0000_0001` are refused. ⛔ **The hexadecimal lower boundary was pinned by no row and no test** — `reader.rs:755` and `:777` and §1's table all use decimal, and that spelling is exactly where `M1.12.1`'s `i64::MIN` defect lived |

  The instrument N-A rests on, and why it is tracked rather than a scratch script:

  ```console
  $ cargo run -q -p eadl-front --example literals -- docs/semantics examples docs/feedback
  ```

  ⭐ **It asks the frontend, not a regular expression.** `crates/eadl-front/tests/reference.rs:527`
  already states the rule this follows — "a second tokenizer here would be a second thing to be wrong
  about" — and M-G's census was a second tokenizer, which is the mechanism by which its scope went
  unstated. The instrument reads each description with `eadl_front::read`, takes every
  `Form::Integer`'s value **and its own span's spelling**, and separately counts `read-number-overflow`
  diagnostics, so it reports the decision-relevant quantity directly: how many literals in the real
  corpus the domain already refuses. Its population is its arguments, which is what makes the census
  re-runnable *and* its scope visible — the property M-G's figure lacked.

  ⚠️ **The instrument is a measurement, not a gate, so it asserts nothing.** A carried figure is a
  stale figure (`M1.23`, `M1.24`); this prints one. Its falsification leg is that it sees what it
  claims: pointed at a directory holding one overflowing literal, the refusal count moves 0 → 1 and
  names the spelling.

  ### The decision this child records, and the argument it rests on

  **Keep the exact 64-bit signed domain for `eadl/1`.** Three measurements, not a preference. ⛔ **Both
  of the measurements this argument was drafted on moved when `M1.13.2` re-derived them, and the
  argument is restated here in its corrected form — the record of the original is on M-D's and M-G's
  rows above, and the promoted version is `docs/decisions/decision_eadl1-value-domain.md`:**

  1. **No requirement reaches the limit.** The widest *physical* address RISC-V standardizes is 56-bit
     (imposed by the RV64 PMP registers; the RV32 line is narrower still at 34-bit), against a domain
     limit of 2^63−1. The census agrees from the other side and is now re-runnable: the largest literal
     in the whole tracked corpus is **2^32**, needing 33 of the domain's 63 magnitude bits, and **0**
     literals in it are refused by the domain. ⛔ The half this argument got wrong: a canonical
     *high-half virtual* address — kernel space under Sv39, Sv48 or Sv57 — **is** above the domain as a
     magnitude, so the honest reason to defer is not "nothing needs more" but "everything that needs
     more is already reachable as a signed value, and the unsigned spelling is a compatible widening".
     Sv64 is not merely reserved either: the pinned spec says it "will be defined in a later version".
  2. **Widening moves the hole, it does not close it.** `Form::Decimal` holds digits and a scale, so
     `i128` would leave the same class of limit one width up. Only arbitrary precision removes it, and
     the workspace carries **zero** third-party dependencies — a property `decision_zero-dependency-engine-core.md`
     records and §10.4's trust argument rests on — so arbitrary precision means hand-written arithmetic
     in the one crate every other layer's exactness depends on.
  3. **Deferring is cheap in a way that deferring most language changes is not.** A widening is
     *backward compatible*: every literal readable under `eadl/1` stays readable and keeps its value,
     so no description needs migrating and the only cost is one added compatibility-corpus row. That is
     the property §15 actually cares about — "a source description retains its meaning under its locked
     semantic version" — and a widening preserves it by construction.
  ⚠️ Counter-argument, stated because it is real: `M1.12` routed this here on the reasoning that a
  freeze is what makes a language change expensive, and pre-freeze is the free moment. That reasoning
  holds for *incompatible* changes and is what sequences `M1.13.1` first. For a compatible widening it
  does not hold, and point 3 is why.
  ⛔ One consequence to carry rather than hide: widening later would force `eadl-model` and
  `rt-analysis` to narrow explicitly at their own boundaries, adding diagnostics to a layer whose codes
  `M1.26`'s gap (a) measures as stated normatively nowhere. Deciding the domain now keeps that
  sequencing honest — the model layer's rules get written down before its population grows.

  ### Five things this slice found that were not in its acceptance criteria

  ⛔ Each was measured and each is corrected in place rather than left as a comment, because four of the
  five are false sentences in surfaces a later leaf would have trusted.

  1. **M-G's published figure was false in two ways** — an unstated scope (27 is the *non-negative*
     count; the population holds 28 with `-1`) and a radix-scoped maximum published as the overall one
     (2^32 > 2^28, so the row contradicted itself). Census of the copies, both directions:
     `grep -rn '2\^28\|27 distinct\|largest literal' --include='*.md' .` outside `target/` and
     `vendor/` → **7** occurrences over **3** live surfaces (this tree 5, `docs/TASK_TREE.md` 1,
     `MEMORY.md` 1), none in the book, grammar or reference. All 7 corrected.
     ⛔ Recorded so nobody "fixes" it back: the first re-derivation here was itself a regular
     expression and reported **28** distinct over **303** occurrences, both wrong for this question
     because digits in a *comment* are prose and not literals. The frontend's answer is 24 over 194.
     That disagreement is why the instrument is tracked.
  2. **M-D's conclusion is true of physical addresses and false of virtual ones.** Every canonical
     high-half RV64 virtual address is refused today in its unsigned spelling —
     `0xFFFF_FC00_0000_0000`, `0xFFFF_FFFF_C000_0000` and `0xFFFF_FF80_0000_0000` each
     `error[read-number-overflow]` — because an Sv39 kernel address has bits 63-39 all set, a magnitude
     of 2^64−2^38 against a limit of 2^63−1. No value is lost (`-1073741824` reads and round-trips);
     what is refused is the spelling a datasheet prints. Sv64 is also stronger than "Reserved": the pin
     says it "**will be defined** in a later version of this specification".
  3. **The hexadecimal lower boundary was pinned by nothing**, in the spelling finding F-E's defect
     originally lived in. Census: the `grep` in REPRODUCE above → 4 hits, none of them
     `-0x8000_0000_0000_0000`. Now a §1 row and a unit test, and mutation B shows both fire.
  4. **One diagnostic code carried three repair wordings** — two in the code, a third merged in §4's
     column — and none of the three told the high-half-address author anything usable, which §4's own
     preamble calls the most expensive diagnostic to receive. Now one shared const, and §4's column
     names the spelling that works.
  5. **`arm_24`'s mutation was scoped to one instance of the property it removes.** Adding a second
     citation to `reading.md` left the arm removing one of two, so it failed on its own count. Loud,
     which is the design working — but a needle scoped to one sentence measures "this line changed",
     not "this chapter cites nothing". Widened to every occurrence with a post-mutation assertion.

  ⭐ **One question routed onward, measured and sized rather than raised as a worry.** The census
  reports **`decimal literals : 0`** — no tracked description anywhere holds a rational value. Census,
  so the claim carries its population: the instrument over `docs/semantics examples docs/feedback` →
  `decimal literals : 0` across 75 files; independently, `grep -oE '[+-]?[0-9][0-9_]*\.[0-9][0-9_]*'`
  over `git ls-files '*.eadl'` → **0** matches. So §1 rule 2 (a decimal is an exact rational) is pinned
  by table rows and by the reader's own tests, but **no shipped description exercises it**, and the
  conformance suite `M1.13.4` builds from those 62 files would not conform-test the rational half of
  §1 at all. Routed to `M1.13.4`, which is deciding exactly what the suite proves and what its
  population must contain — same tree, so no routing evidence is owed. It is also the shape
  `docs/knowledge/a-gate-is-only-as-sharp-as-its-fixtures.md` names: a leg green because its population
  cannot reach the case.

  ### Lesson promotion

  `a red arm must destroy the property, not an instance of it` → `promotion: promoted
  (docs/knowledge/verify-the-mutation-applied.md gains the section "A mutation that applied
  *partially* is the same failure in a greener disguise" and a fourth `answers:` line, so the card is
  reachable by the question someone actually asks — "my mutation applied, its count assertion passed,
  and the arm still proves nothing". Promoted into the existing card rather than forked beside it,
  because the card already owns "did my mutation land" and a near-duplicate answering the same
  question makes the retrievable layer harder to search; what is new is that a count assertion on the
  *needle* cannot see a mutation that removed one of several instances, and only an assertion on the
  **post-mutation state** can.)` The figure-scope lesson is *not* promoted separately:
  `docs/knowledge/a-moved-measurement-needs-a-census-of-its-copies.md` already answers "is that
  everything, or a subset shaped like my pattern?", and this is that question one level up, in a
  superlative rather than a count.

  Verification: see the acceptance checklist above.
  Commit: `ARCHOGEN-M1-0082 (leaf M1.13.2)`

- ID: `M1.13.3`
  Status: `done`
  Goal: put the version identifier on the surface — a description states which language version it is
  written in, and §15's "a source description retains its meaning under its locked semantic version"
  becomes something a reader can determine from the description rather than assume.
  Acceptance: the identifier is a stated rule in a reference chapter executed by
  `crates/eadl-front/tests/reference.rs`, with what absence means decided explicitly and not left to
  the reader's default; any diagnostic it introduces is a §4 row over the declared sources, so the
  census in both directions covers it; every corpus file and example either carries the identifier or
  is covered by the absence rule, **measured by census rather than assumed**; RED arms for a wrong
  version, an unparseable one and an absent one; `make focused` exit `0`.
  Priority: **high** — this is the acceptance criterion the whole leaf is named for, and `.4`'s
  baseline has to digest a version it can name.

  ### The design constraint that decides this, measured rather than argued

  ⛔ **The identifier cannot ride the comment-header convention, however convenient that looks.** The
  header route needs no grammar change, §5 already makes one shape of comment data, and
  `Document::comment_headers()` already reads it — so it is the obvious candidate and it is wrong here.
  §3 states that canonical form carries **no comment**, and §12 M4 hashes canonical plans. A version
  that lives in trivia is therefore a version the hashed artifact does not capture: two descriptions
  differing only in the language version they claim would hash identically, and any tool that strips
  comments would silently strip the lock with them. A version identifier has to survive canonicalization,
  which means it has to be a **form**.

  What that costs, stated so the choice is made with the price visible: a new top-level form is a
  grammar change, so `docs/semantics/grammar.md`'s `document` production and
  `crates/eadl-front/tests/conformance.rs`'s probe set both move, and the reference's §4 gains rows.
  That is exactly the work `.1` establishes the pattern for, and it is free only because it lands
  before `.4` writes the baseline.

  ⚠️ Decide and record, do not drift into it: whether an **absent** identifier means `eadl/1` or is
  refused. Defaulting is friendlier to the 62 existing corpus files and weaker as a lock — §15's word
  is *locked*, and a version nobody wrote is a version the toolchain inferred. Refusing is a
  strengthening that pre-freeze costs one pass over the corpus. Whichever is chosen, the reason goes in
  the decision record with the census of what it touched.

  ⭐ **This leaf also owns the language's *name*, and the director has now supplied the rationale that
  decides it.** Raised `2026-09-29`; the measurements and the three candidate readings are in
  `## Open Questions` above. In one line each: the acronym `eADL` is expanded **nowhere** in the
  repository, the director supplied **Extended Architecture Description Language** while unsure what it
  extends, and "Extended ADL" cannot be a claim about the syntax because the ADL tradition (Acme,
  Wright, Rapide, UniCon, C2, Darwin, AADL/AS5506) carries bespoke notations and none of it is
  s-expression based.

  ⭐ **The director's stated reason for the syntax picks the reading:** *"What I really like about
  Lispish language or S-expressions is that code and data wear the same cloth, that is they have the
  exact same syntax and it is inherently extensible."* That is reading **(b)** — extended as
  **extensible** — and it is not an aspiration in this repository, it is already executed: one `Form`
  type carries a description and any data inside it alike, and `defkind` grows the language using the
  language's own declaration construct (§7 of the reference, made a machine-read declaration by
  `M1.12.4`, with §5.6's prohibition enforced against the facility that declares the language).

  ⛔ **State the limit beside the claim, or the word oversells.** eADL is homoiconic in its *syntax* and
  deliberately not in its *execution*: there is no `eval` and no macro system, so a description cannot
  rewrite itself. That is `decision_eadl-engine-boundary` — eADL contains no implementation, and a macro
  is implementation. The engine interprets the forms; it is Rust, and it is not written in the language
  it reads. What "code and data wear the same cloth" buys here is that **the language is extensible by
  its own users without a new parser**, not that it is a Lisp.

  ⚠️ And the cost, which is this tree's actual history rather than a caveat: a uniform syntax makes
  well-formedness and meaning come apart, so the reader accepts forms the semantics must then refuse.
  Every finding in `M1.12` is that shape — `0x_10` read as 16 while the grammar refused it (F-B), a
  grammar accepting a raw line feed the reader refused (`M1.13.1`), and a well-formed literal outside
  the value domain being *refused* rather than *errored* (`M1.13.2`), a distinction that exists only
  because the two questions are separate. That is why `docs/semantics/reference.md` exists at all, in
  its own words: a grammar proves two implementations accept the same language and "says nothing about
  the value behind a token". Extensibility is the reason the language is s-expression shaped, and the
  second normative document is the price. ⭐ **`M1.13.3` paid that price again and it is worth naming:**
  `(eadl-version eadl/1)` is a perfectly well-formed form that the grammar always accepted, and three
  separate layers then had to be told what it *means* — the reader validates it, `module.rs` must not
  count it as a stray declaration, and `check.rs` must not validate it as a kind. Uniform syntax is what
  makes all three possible and what makes all three necessary.

  ### Four things this slice found that were not in its acceptance criteria

  1. ⛔ **The leaf's own design premise was false.** It said the identifier costs a grammar change and
     a conformance probe-set change. The grammar names no construct vocabulary — `grammar.md:109` says
     so in terms — so `(eadl-version eadl/1)` was already well-formed and the recognizer derived from
     that grammar accepts it unchanged. The leaf's cost argument ("free only because it lands before
     `.4`") was right about the timing and wrong about the reason.
  2. ⛔ **`version` was already taken**, by §6's `defmodule` clause, with four §4 rows and an
     implemented reader. Naming the new form `version` would have been a silent collision resolved only
     by nesting depth. Now `eadl-version` for the language and `version` for the module, and a test
     fixture carries both in one file so the distinction is executed.
  3. ⛔ **The model layer refused the new form** as `schema-unknown-kind` — §8 contradicted end-to-end
     by the layer furthest from it. Found by testing through `check`, not by reading `check.rs`.
  4. ⭐ **My first test of that break could not have seen the fix.** It called `validate` directly, and
     the exemption lives in `validate`'s *caller*, so it kept failing after the fix landed and would
     have kept failing whatever `check.rs` did. A test that bypasses the layer under test measures the
     wrong thing confidently — recorded in `DEV_NOTES.md`, promotion declined there with the reason.

  ⚠️ **Routed to `M1.13.4`, measured and sized rather than raised as a worry.** No tracked description
  carries the identifier, so the conformance suite `.4` builds from those files would exercise §8 only
  through the reference's rows and never through a shipped description — the same shape as the
  `decimal literals : 0` finding `M1.13.2` routed to the same leaf. Census, so the claim carries its
  population: `git ls-files '*.eadl'` → 75 files, and `grep -l eadl-version` over them → 0. `.4` owns
  what the suite proves and what its population must contain; whether to retrofit the identifier into
  the 62 non-evidence files is that decision, not this leaf's, and retrofitting it into `examples/`
  would move the book's rendered examples and the round-trip evidence `corpus.rs` pins.

  ⚠️ **Not closed here, and deliberately: the language's *name*.** This leaf owns it (see
  `## Open Questions`) and the director's rationale selects reading **(b)** — extended as *extensible*,
  which `defkind` already executes. But the director supplied "Extended" while unsure what it extends,
  so the expansion is **recorded and not published**: three drafted expansions were reverted
  byte-identical in `ARCHOGEN-M1-0085` and stay reverted. Publishing a word its own author is unsure of,
  into the surfaces `M1.13.5` is about to freeze, is the wrong order. The identifier work above is
  complete without it — `eadl/1` is the version string, and what the letters stand for is a separate
  question with a separate owner.
  Verification: see the acceptance checklist above.
  Commit: `ARCHOGEN-M1-0087 (leaf M1.13.3)`

- ID: `M1.13.4`
  Status: `done`
  Goal: make the existing corpora `eadl/1`'s conformance suite — one declared population instead of
  three tests each walking their own — and write the instrument that digests every frozen construct
  into a baseline.
  Children: `M1.13.4.1` … `M1.13.4.5`
  Acceptance: a single machine-read manifest names the suite's roots, what each root proves, and the
  version it conforms to; the population is enumerated **from the manifest**, and the two existing
  walks (`conformance.rs:496`, `reference.rs:473`) read it rather than restating it; the exclusion of
  `docs/feedback/linkedspec/issues/*/evidence/` is stated with its reason and enforced, so a feedback
  file cannot become a conformance case by being copied into a walked root; a baseline instrument emits
  one digest per frozen construct — the EBNF fence, the five machine-read tables, the two kind
  declarations, and the **canonical form** of every suite file; RED arms for a moved digest, an added
  file, a removed file and an excluded file that appears in the suite; `make focused` exit `0`.
  Priority: **high** — this is the second acceptance criterion, and `.5` cannot gate a baseline that
  does not exist.

  ### Why the corpus leg digests canonical form and not file bytes

  ⭐ A corpus file's comment header carries its `case:`, `why:` and `rationale:` prose, and editing a
  rationale is not a language change — digesting bytes would make every documentation fix to a corpus
  file look like a change to a frozen construct, and a gate that fires on prose gets disabled, which is
  `docs/knowledge/prose-beside-data-goes-unenforced.md`'s failure from the other side. Canonical form
  is the language's own normative printer and drops comments, so the digest moves **exactly when the
  described system moves**. It is also the artifact §12 M4 hashes, so the baseline and the build agree
  on what "the same description" means instead of maintaining two definitions of it.

  ⚠️ Honest limit to state on the leaf when it closes: canonical form does not retain comments, so a
  change to a corpus file's *header* is invisible to the baseline. That is deliberate — headers are
  prose about a case, and the ones that carry data are already pinned elsewhere, by `corpus.rs`
  against `docs/semantics/boundary/README.md`'s counts and by `reference.rs` against the reference's
  `comment-headers` table — but it means the baseline proves the described systems did not move, not
  that the files did not.

  ⛔ **This child writes the baseline and does not enforce it.** Enforcing a digest in the same commit
  that creates it is untestable: there is no prior state to differ from, so no RED arm can fire against
  the real tree. `M1.13.5` enforces, and can then be shown firing on a real mutation.

  ### Measurement — added `2026-09-29`, before any child was written

  ⛔ Measured with tools first (`TOOLBOX.md`), and the decisive measurement was **executed rather than
  reasoned**: the retrofit this leaf inherits as a question was applied to all 62 files and the whole
  suite run, then reverted. That is how a question about documentation value turned into a production
  defect, a false normative sentence, and a census figure this leaf itself had published nine commits
  earlier that had already moved.

  | # | Measured | Command | Result |

Neither is a reason not to retrofit; both are defects the freeze must not be built on top of |
  | M-J | root cause 1 is a **production** defect: the kind registry refuses the identifier | the failure text itself, `error[schema-not-a-kind]: expected a \`defkind\` declaration, found \`eadl-version\` --> docs/semantics/kinds/core.eadl:38:1`, rendered **43** times across 6 suites; `crates/eadl-model/src/check.rs:98` iterates `document.forms` and hands every one to `read_kind` | **36 of the 45**, including all five `ToolFailure`-instead-of-a-verdict failures in `crates/archogen-cli/tests/`. ⛔ §8 rule 8 says "the identifier is not a declaration, and **two** layers had to be told" — this is the third, and `is_identifier`'s own doc comment predicts it: "a rule each consumer re-implements is a rule the next consumer lacks". The model layer's rational path is also already unit-tested — `f03_units.rs:151` reads `1.5 ms`, `rational.rs::a_decimal_literal_is_exact` — so what is missing is a **shipped description**, not first coverage |

So the baseline must **enumerate** its constructs at run time and never carry a list — `PROGRAM.20`'s register shape, in a figure this leaf published itself |

  ### Two decisions this leaf owns, both settled by measurement rather than preference

  1. ⭐ **The retrofit happens: all 62 suite files state `(eadl-version eadl/1)`.** The reason that
     survives the cost M-I measured is not tidiness — it is that **§8 rule 2 is false as written
     today**. That rule justifies the absence-denotes rule by naming the population relying on it:
     "the descriptions relying on it are known rather than assumed: the frozen LinkedSpec evidence
     under `docs/feedback/`, whose bytes *are* the reproduction of another project's defect and which
     nothing in `crates/` reads". Measured, **62** live files rely on the rule too (`grep -l
     eadl-version` over the 75 → 0), so the small-and-frozen population the rule's acceptability rests
     on does not exist. Retrofitting makes the sentence true, and turns the rule's stated expiry —
     absence refused the day a second version exists — into a non-event for the suite instead of a
     62-file migration performed *under* the freeze gate `M1.13.5` is about to install. Second reason:
     the suite is what a user imitates, and a corpus that omits the identifier teaches omission. The
     cost is two root causes, both of which are defects (M-J is production, M-K is a rule re-implemented
     per leg) and both of which are cheaper to fix now than to freeze beside.
  2. ⭐ **One shipped description gains a decimal literal.** M-O: the census instrument exists to make
     an empty category visible, and `decimal literals : 0` is the one empty category, so §1 rule 2's
     exact rationals are stated by 55 executed reference rows and reached by no description. M-N says
     the pipeline handles one, so this is a coverage gap and not a capability gap. It goes into
     `docs/semantics/cases/` as a new **positive** case rather than into an `examples/` system, because
     an example's numbers are pinned by analysis expectations and published in the book — changing them
     to make a literal category non-empty would move figures this leaf has no business moving, while a
     case whose *subject* is the rational states why it exists in its own header.
  3. ⛔ **The exclusion is enforced by path, from the manifest, and never by content.** M-M measured
     the alternative false on the shipped tree. What the exclusion therefore proves is narrower and is
     stated as such: a path matching an exclusion never enters the population, the pattern is
     non-vacuous against the real evidence directory, and a malformed copy smuggled into a walked root
     is caught by the suite's own accept leg rather than by the exclusion.

  ### Decomposition — added `2026-09-29`, five children

  Five children rather than one, for the reason `M1.13`'s own decomposition gave and M-I repeats from
  the other side: **the retrofit edits every construct the baseline is about to digest.** A leaf that
  both moves 62 canonical forms and writes their digests cannot show a clean before→after for either,
  and a defect in the digesting leg would be indistinguishable from the edit it was measuring.

  | Child | Settles | Why in this order |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | `M1.13.4.1` | "the identifier is not a declaration", single-defined: the kind registry (M-J, production) and one accessor every leg can use (M-K) | a language fix that the retrofit depends on; landing it first means `.2`'s diff is 62 corpus files and the legs that read them, not a fix buried inside them |
  | `M1.13.4.2` | the retrofit: all 62 state their version, and the surfaces the insertion moves are re-rendered | needs `.1`. Must land before the baseline, or the commit that writes the digests is also the commit that changes every digested construct |
  | `M1.13.4.3` | the empty literal category: one shipped description carries a decimal | after `.2` only so the new file states its version like every other suite file; a different subject, so a different diff |
  | `M1.13.4.4` | the manifest: one declared population, both walks read it, the exclusion enforced and non-vacuous | needs a population that has stopped moving — `.2` and `.3` are the last edits to it |
  | `M1.13.4.5` | the baseline instrument and the baseline file | needs the manifest to enumerate from, and a population that will not move underneath it |

  ⚠️ **None of the five displaces `M1.13.5`, and `.5` still cannot start before them**: it gates a
  baseline, and `M1.13.4.5` is what writes one.

  Verification: see the closure review below — all five children are `done`, and the parent's own
  acceptance criteria were re-checked one by one rather than assumed from the children's.
  Commit: `ARCHOGEN-M1-0089 (leaf M1.13.4)` decomposed it; the children are `ARCHOGEN-M1-0091`,
  `-0092`, `-0094`, `-0096` and `-0097`.

  ### Closure review — the parent's acceptance criterion by criterion, `2026-09-29`

  | Criterion | Verdict | What closes it |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | a single machine-read manifest names the roots, what each proves, and the version | ✅ | `docs/semantics/conformance.md`, three `machine-read` tables, **no count in it**, read by one module (`tests/common/suite.rs`) — `M1.13.4.4` |
  | the population is enumerated from the manifest and both walks read it | ✅ | `conformance.rs::corpus()` and `reference.rs::corpus()` are each one call to `suite::population`; the private walks are gone — `M1.13.4.4` |
  | the exclusion is stated with its reason and enforced | ✅ | stated in the manifest with its reason, and enforced three ways: a root reaching an excluded path is a violation, every exclusion must match a real description, and no suite file may be byte-identical to an excluded one — `M1.13.4.4`. ⛔ The criterion's own wording ("a feedback file cannot become a conformance case **by being copied into a walked root**") needed the third leg, because a copy has a suite path; and the byte-identity leg is usable only because `M1.13.4.2` diverged the one legitimate pair |
  | a baseline instrument emits one digest per frozen construct | ✅ | **70** constructs — 1 EBNF fence, **6** machine-read tables (enumerated, not listed), 63 canonical forms — in `docs/semantics/BASELINE.txt`, written by `scripts/language_baseline.sh` from `examples/language_freeze.rs`, deterministic across runs and cross-checked against independent re-computations — `M1.13.4.5` |
  | RED arms for a moved digest, an added file, a removed file, an excluded file in the suite | ✅ | six arms over synthetic baselines and populations in `tests/language_baseline.rs` (`Moved` / `Added` / `Removed`, a row nothing can parse, an unsorted or duplicated file, an empty one), plus mutation I on the real tree showing one added table row moving exactly one digest — `M1.13.4.5` |
  | `make focused` exit `0` | ✅ | `passed — 3 / 0 / 0` at every child's commit, and **535 passed / 0 failed** over 39 suites at closure |

  ⭐ **What the parent did not anticipate, and the children found.** Three defects outside the acceptance
  criteria, each owned rather than noted: a **production** refusal in the kind registry that made
  `archogen` answer `tool-failure` for every description (`M1.13.4.1`); a normative sentence in §8 rule 2
  that was **false** for the one commit it existed, because 62 live descriptions relied on the absence
  rule it says only the frozen evidence does (`M1.13.4.2`); and **five** live false figures in the book —
  a corpus size stale for 71 commits, a per-verdict table wrong in three of five rows, a transcript
  rendered from a fixture shape no tracked command writes, an abridged hint, and "23 probes, one per
  production" in a normative document against a measured 25 and 24 (`M1.13.4.2`, `.3`, `.4`). The last of
  those is filed as **`M1.27`**, because deleting the count does not retract the claim underneath it.

- ID: `M1.13.4.1`
  Status: `done`
  Goal: make "the identifier is not a declaration" single-defined, and fix the production defect M-J
  measured: the kind registry refuses `(eadl-version eadl/1)` in a kind file as `schema-not-a-kind`,
  contradicting §8 from a third layer.
  Acceptance: `crates/eadl-model/src/check.rs`'s `shipped_registry` iterates declarations rather than
  every top-level form, through **one** filter — a `Document` accessor in `eadl-front` beside
  `stated_version`, not a second spelling of `is_identifier` at the call site; a kind file carrying the
  identifier loads and registers exactly the kinds the same file without it registers; a **malformed**
  identifier in a kind file is still reported with its own §4 code, because `shipped_registry` already
  surfaces the reader's diagnostics (`check.rs:94`) and a filter that swallowed them would trade one
  false refusal for a silent one; §8 rule 8 and `is_identifier`'s doc comment both say **three**
  consumers and name the third; mutation arms show each new leg failing when its filter is removed;
  `make focused` exit `0`.
  ⚠️ **One acceptance item moved to `M1.13.4.2`, and the reason is measured rather than convenience.**
  This leaf's first draft also required "the existing legs M-K names are adapted to the accessor".
  Those legs — `corpus.rs`'s "exactly one declaration" and `f27_boundary.rs`'s `forms[0]` seed — read
  *corpus* files, and no corpus file carries the identifier until `.2` writes it in, so adapting them
  here would be a change nothing exercises: green before and green after, which is the shape
  `docs/knowledge/a-gate-is-only-as-sharp-as-its-fixtures.md` warns reads as work. `.2` owns them, and
  there they are proven by the suite going green. What this leaf does convert is the three test helpers
  that hand-rolled a **kind-module** loader, because those are the test-side copy of the defect it fixes.
  Priority: **high** — this is a live contradiction between a normative document and the implementation,
  and `M1.13.4.2` cannot land without it.
  Verification: see the acceptance checklist below.
  Commit: `ARCHOGEN-M1-0091 (leaf M1.13.4.1)`

  ### Acceptance Checklist (enforced by `TASK-ACCEPTANCE`)

  - [x] **ROOT CAUSE (WHY + WHERE)** — WHY: §8 makes the identifier a top-level **form** that is not a
    declaration, so every consumer that iterates top-level forms and treats one *as* a declaration owes
    the rule; `is_identifier`'s own doc comment said so and named two consumers, and predicted the third
    ("a rule each consumer re-implements is a rule the next consumer lacks"). WHERE:
    `crates/eadl-model/src/check.rs`'s `shipped_registry`, which iterated `document.forms` and handed
    every one to `read_kind`. Measured at the parent commit `f956714` by writing `(eadl-version eadl/1)`
    into all **62** suite descriptions and running `cargo test --all --no-fail-fast`:
    **45 failed / 447 passed over 9 suites**, with
    `error[schema-not-a-kind]: expected a `defkind` declaration, found `eadl-version` -->
    docs/semantics/kinds/core.eadl:38:1` rendered **43** times and **36** of the 45 failures traceable
    to that one consumer — including all five CLI tests that expected a verdict and got `ToolFailure`.
    The tree was restored and the restoration proven: `git status --porcelain` → **0 entries**,
    `git diff --stat` → **0 lines**. It is a production defect, not a test artifact: `archogen check`
    answered `tool-failure: the shipped kind modules could not be loaded` for *every* description, and
    no description-level test could reach the loader, because nothing reads a kind module except it and
    no fixture had ever put the construct in one.
  - [x] **ADDRESSED (verified)** — one accessor, not a third hand-rolled filter:
    `eadl_front::language_version::declarations(&[Form])` beside the predicate, plus
    `Document::declarations()`. `shipped_registry` iterates it; `check()` filters once, by move, so the
    boundary, schema and presence passes and the `Outcome::declarations` field all hold declarations;
    `module.rs`'s §6 count uses the accessor instead of its own `filter`. Proven **end to end through
    the CLI's own loader** rather than through a test double: with the identifier written into the real
    `docs/semantics/kinds/core.eadl`, `cargo run -q -p archogen-cli -- check
    examples/periodic-three/system.eadl` → `accepted against profile `rt-static-up-v1` (8
    declaration(s))` at `exit=0` — the same line `docs/book/src/checking.md:5` publishes — where the
    unfiltered loader answers `tool-failure`. Restoration byte-identical (`diff -q` silent,
    `git status --porcelain docs/semantics/kinds/` → empty). Four new tests: a kind module stating its
    version registers **the same kinds** as one that does not (`with.heads() == without.heads()`, and
    `with.len() == without.len()`, so the identifier is not registered as a kind of its own); a
    malformed one is still refused with §8's own `language-version-unknown` and with **no** `schema-*`
    code, so the filter drops identifiers and not problems with one; `declarations()` keeps source order,
    ignores a *nested* form of the same name, and drops nothing from a description that states no
    version; and stating a version does not change the reported declaration count.
  - [x] **NO REGRESSION** — `cargo test --all` → **496 passed, 0 failed** over 37 suites (baseline 492,
    delta = the four new tests); `make focused` → `passed — 3 passed, 0 failed, 0 unavailable, 0 not
    built`, `exit=0`; `bash scripts/check_doctrines.sh` → `=== all doctrines green ===`;
    `mdbook build docs/book` → `rc=0`; `bash scripts/check_no_background_jobs.sh` → `handoff: OK`.
    **Three mutations, each seen firing, each restoration proven byte-identical** (`diff -q` against a
    copy taken before the mutation): A, `shipped_registry` iterating `&document.forms` again →
    `a_kind_module_may_state_its_language_version` FAILED printing `error[schema-not-a-kind]`; B,
    `check()` not filtering → `stating_a_version_does_not_change_how_many_declarations_a_description_has`
    FAILED (`left: 2, right: 3`) and `M1.13.3`'s `the_language_version_identifier_survives_the_model_layer`
    FAILED with it; C, the identifier written into the shipped kind module → the CLI leg above, then
    restored. ⛔ **The gate fired on this leaf's own prose**: the citation leg rejected
    `docs/semantics/kinds/*.eadl` as a path that does not exist, because leg 6 resolves a glob only in
    its trailing-`/*` form, and **12** arms and legs reported the extra violation before the wording was
    corrected to name the directory. A normative document cannot cite a suffixed glob, which is worth
    knowing before writing one rather than after.
  - [x] **FIX** — `crates/eadl-front/src/language_version.rs` (the accessor, the three-consumer doc
    comment, one unit test); `crates/eadl-front/src/module.rs` (the accessor replaces its own filter);
    `crates/eadl-model/src/check.rs` (the loader, the single filter in `check`, and the
    `Outcome::declarations` contract written down where the field is declared);
    `crates/eadl-model/tests/kinds.rs` and `tests/examples.rs` (three hand-rolled kind-module loaders
    replaced by calls to `shipped_registry`, plus the four new legs).
  - [x] **LOCKSTEP** — `docs/semantics/reference.md` §8 rule 8 now says **three** layers, names the
    third with its diagnostic and its blast radius, states why the rule is an accessor rather than a
    predicate, and records why `crates/eadl-model/src/check.rs` is **not** added to the
    normative-sources table: §4's census runs in both directions over exactly that list, so declaring a
    source demands a §4 row for every code it can emit, and this one emits the model layer's — which no
    normative document states yet. That is `M1.26`'s gap (a), and it is what has to close first, so the
    omission is a sequencing fact and not an oversight. `docs/book/src/kinds.md` tells the reader a kind
    module may state its language version; `DEV_NOTES.md` carries the lesson and
    `docs/knowledge/enumerate-the-population-from-the-specification.md` gains it as a promoted section
    with a fifth `answers:` line, so `LESSON-PROMOTION` is satisfied by promotion and not by decline.
    Frontier moves to `M1.13.4.2`; `docs/TASK_TREE.md`, `MEMORY.md`, `LIVE_STATUS.md` and
    `CHANGELOG.md` follow in this commit.

- ID: `M1.13.4.2`
  Status: `done`
  Goal: the retrofit — every file in the suite states `(eadl-version eadl/1)`, so §8 rule 2's
  justifying sentence becomes true and the rule's expiry stops being a 62-file cliff.
  Acceptance: all 62 non-evidence descriptions carry the identifier as their first top-level form, in
  the position §8's own rows write it; the **13** frozen evidence files under `docs/feedback/` are
  untouched, and their bytes still hash to what `FEEDBACK-SELF-CONTAINED` sealed (M-M's pair therefore
  diverges, which is the point — the copy is a reproduction of a four-form description and stays one);
  a census leg requires every suite file to state its version, so the invariant cannot rot the next
  time a case is added (`grep -L eadl-version` over the population → empty, and the leg is written
  against the frontend rather than the grep); every leg M-K measured is green through the accessor and
  not through a re-typed expectation; every book transcript whose line numbers the insertion moves is
  **re-rendered from a real run** rather than incremented by hand; `make focused` exit `0` and
  `make integration`'s book step green.
  Priority: **high** — it makes a normative sentence true, and it is free only before `M1.13.4.5`
  writes the baseline.

  ⚠️ **Known cost, measured rather than discovered (M-I): 45 failures over 9 suites**, of which
  `M1.13.4.1` fixes 36 and this leaf the other 9. ⛔ **Also known and not gated by anything: the book
  carries line-numbered diagnostic transcripts** (`grep -rn '^[0-9]\+ |' docs/book/src/*.md` → 14 lines
  over 8 chapters), and inserting two lines at the top of a description moves every one of them that
  quotes a suite file. Nothing in the repository compares a transcript to the run it claims to be, so a
  stale line number is invisible — the `M1.23`/`M1.24` defect class in a surface neither of them
  reached, and a **seventh shape** of `PROGRAM.20`'s register, which now carries it. This leaf
  re-renders the ones it moves and does not pretend to close the class.
  Verification: see the acceptance checklist below.
  Commit: `ARCHOGEN-M1-0092 (leaf M1.13.4.2)`

    in `crates/` reads". At the parent commit `bc3bf27` that sentence was **false**: the census
    `git ls-files '*.eadl' | while read -r f; do grep -q eadl-version "$f" || echo "$f"; done | wc -l`
    → **75 of 75**, so 62 live descriptions relied on the rule too and the "known rather than assumed"
    population it rests on did not exist. Nothing gated the sentence, which is why it stayed false for
    the one commit between §8 being written and this leaf — and why the fix here is a **leg** and not
    only 62 file edits. WHERE: the 62 descriptions under `docs/semantics/` and `examples/`; the 9 test
    legs M-K measured that read a top-level form as a declaration; and four book transcripts whose line
    numbers the insertion moves.
  - [x] **ADDRESSED (verified)** — the identifier written into all **62** as the first top-level form,
    the position §8's own rows write it, and the **13** frozen evidence files untouched:

    doctrine run. The census re-run afterwards —
    `git ls-files '*.eadl' | while read -r f; do grep -q eadl-version "$f" || echo "$f"; done | wc -l`
    → **13** of `git ls-files '*.eadl' | wc -l` → **75**, and every one of the 13 under
    `docs/feedback/linkedspec/issues/*/evidence/` — is exactly the population §8 names, so the
    normative sentence is now a measurement; `cargo test -p eadl-front --test reference` →
    `test result: ok. 42 passed; 0 failed` with the leg and its three arms in it. The leg that keeps it

    `every_description_in_the_suite_states_its_language_version` in `crates/eadl-front/tests/reference.rs`,
    asking `Document::stated_version` rather than a text search, with three arms: a synthetic population

    category holding nothing is visibly zero", and exactly one was zero — `decimal literals : 0` over
    every description the repository ships, against `194` integer occurrences over `24` distinct values
    and `13` strings. So §1 rule 2's exact rationals were executed by the reference's rows and by
    nothing a reader would actually write, and a conformance suite for `eadl/1` that never writes a
    decimal does not demonstrate the language's rational half to a second implementation, which is what
    §12 M2's independence standard asks of it. It was a coverage gap and not a capability gap: `archogen
    check` on a scratch copy of `examples/periodic-three/system.eadl` carrying `(period 12.5 ms)` and
    `(release-accuracy (at-most 0.5 ms))` returned `accepted against profile `rt-static-up-v1` (8
    declaration(s))` at `exit=0`, and the model layer's rational path is unit-tested already
    (`crates/eadl-model/tests/f03_units.rs:151` reads `1.5 ms`,
    `crates/eadl-model/src/rational.rs::a_decimal_literal_is_exact`). WHERE: no file under
    `docs/semantics/cases/` carried a decimal — `ls docs/semantics/cases/*.eadl | wc -l` → 29 cases at
    the parent commit, and the coverage leg in `crates/eadl-front/tests/reference.rs` names the literal
    population it walks.
  - [x] **ADDRESSED (verified)** — one new positive case,
    `docs/semantics/cases/positive-decimal-quantity.eadl`, whose subject *is* the rational: an 80 Hz
    control loop at `(period 12.5 ms) (deadline 12.5 ms)`, which no integer number of milliseconds can
    spell, with the `case:` / `expect:` / `why:` headers `semantic_corpus.rs` reads, a `rationale:` that
    says why the case exists, and `(eadl-version eadl/1)` like every other suite file. It went into

- ID: `M1.13.4.4`

  the exclusion is **non-vacuous** — a leg walks `docs/feedback/` and requires the pattern to match the
  real evidence files, because an exclusion that matches nothing passes by excluding nothing;
  a leg requires that a path inside a declared root which matches an exclusion is a **violation** and
  not a silent skip; census legs pin the population so the suite cannot silently iterate over nothing;
  `docs/semantics/grammar.md`'s Conformance section cites the manifest instead of restating the roots;
  the book says where the suite is declared; `make focused` exit `0`.

  required "a stated, minimal **glob** semantics" and named the exclusion
  `docs/feedback/linkedspec/issues/*/evidence/`. Both are replaced: the matcher has **no glob grammar at
  all**, because a prefix matcher that quietly understood `*` would make the manifest's stated rule false
  and every pattern in it ambiguous, and the exclusion is the wider prefix `docs/feedback` — measured
  before choosing: all thirteen excluded descriptions are under an `evidence/` directory and nothing else
  under `docs/feedback` is a description at all, so the wider prefix excludes nothing that should be in
  and needs no matcher with a grammar. The arm `the_exclusion_grammar_is_segment_wise_and_nothing_wider`
  pins that `docs/*` matches nothing, so a glob in a future manifest is a leg failure rather than a
  silent widening.

- ID: `M1.13.5`
  Status: `done`
  Goal: enforce the freeze — a change to a frozen construct requires a migration note, mechanically,
  and the book carries the baseline so the director can read what was frozen and what changing it costs.
  Acceptance: `scripts/check_language_freeze.sh` recomputes `.4`'s baseline and fails on any difference
  that no migration note covers; it is registered in `scripts/check_doctrines.project.sh` and mirrored
  in `DOCTRINE_ENFORCEMENT.md`'s table of archogen's own doctrines; the migration-note form is stated —
  what changed, why, which descriptions it invalidates, and which version it lands in — and a note-less
  change is refused with the command that lists the difference and the note that would satisfy it; RED
  arms in `--self-test`, each with its mutation confirmed applied; a book chapter covers the frozen
  baseline and cites the repository paths it freezes, so `BOOK-ANCHORS` holds it; `make integration`
  runs it; `make focused` exit `0`.
  Priority: **high** — this is the third acceptance criterion, and without it "frozen" is prose.

  ### Two rules this gate has to get right, decided here so the child does not improvise them

  1. **Any movement of the baseline requires a note, including a correction.** §15's word is "any
     changed behavior must be explicit", and a gate that tried to distinguish a bug fix from a language
     change would need judgement it cannot have. A note may say *"correction: the specification was
     wrong and no description changes meaning"* — that is still explicit, which is the whole
     requirement, and it costs one paragraph.
  2. **Amending the baseline must be an explicit act, never a side effect.** The check has to name the
     command that regenerates the baseline, and regeneration without a note must fail rather than pass,
     or the first legitimate language change is impossible and the gate gets waived. This is the shape
     `FROZEN-EVALUATION` already uses — integrity, completeness, and a documented unsealing procedure —
     and `scripts/check_frozen_evaluation.sh` is the idiom to follow for the manifest format, the
     portable `sha256` helper and the stated honest limit.

  ⚠️ **Honest limit to state, not to discover later.** A digest proves a construct *moved*; it cannot
  prove the migration note that covers the movement is *correct*, or complete, or that every
  invalidated description was found. That residue is review, exactly as `BOOK-ANCHORS` states its own:
  the gate removes the cheapest failure — a frozen construct edited silently — and leaves the expensive
  one to the reader.
  Verification: see the acceptance checklist below.
  Commit: `ARCHOGEN-M1-0098 (leaf M1.13.5)`

  ### Acceptance Checklist (enforced by `TASK-ACCEPTANCE`)

  - [x] **ROOT CAUSE (WHY + WHERE)** — WHY: `M1.13`'s third acceptance criterion is that "a migration
    note is required for any change to a frozen construct, and a check enforces it", and
    `docs/semantics/BASELINE.txt` existed with nothing comparing it to anything — a record, not a
    freeze. WHERE: measured at the parent commit `145baab` — `git grep -ln BASELINE 145baab -- scripts/`
    → one file, `scripts/language_baseline.sh`, the one that *writes* it; and
    `git show 145baab --name-only | grep -c check_language_freeze` → `0` with `exit 1`, so no gate existed
    and `scripts/check_doctrines.project.sh` listed four project doctrines, none about the language. ⛔ And
    the obvious single-leg design has a hole this leaf's own rule 2 names: a gate that only compares the
    tracked baseline with a fresh run is **waived by regenerating it** — edit the construct, run
    `--emit`, green. So the check needs a second leg comparing the tracked baseline with `HEAD`'s, which
    is what makes amending it an explicit act rather than a side effect.
  - [x] **ADDRESSED (verified)** — `scripts/check_language_freeze.sh`, two legs. **Integrity**: a fresh
    run against the tracked file. **Explicitness**: the tracked file against `git show HEAD:`'s, and on
    any difference a `status: pending` note in `docs/semantics/migrations/` must name every construct the
    amendment moves (or say `constructs: all`), so a note from an earlier migration cannot cover a later
    one and a spent note is a record rather than an open permission. A refusal names the difference, the
    command that shows it raw (`scripts/language_baseline.sh --print | diff docs/semantics/BASELINE.txt -`),
    the note that would satisfy it and where the form lives. The **classification** is the Rust
    comparator from `M1.13.4.5`, reached through a new `--diff <fresh> <tracked>` mode on
    `crates/eadl-front/examples/language_freeze.rs` (exit `0` agree, `1` differ, `2` unreadable), so the
    gate and its arms share one implementation of "what moved" — a second classifier in shell would be a
    second thing that can disagree about whether a construct was added or removed. The note form, the
    workflow and both honest limits are in `docs/semantics/migrations/README.md`. Registered in
    `scripts/check_doctrines.project.sh` and mirrored in `DOCTRINE_ENFORCEMENT.md`'s table; on the clean
    tree it prints `language-freeze: OK (70 frozen construct(s) agree with the working tree)`.
  - [x] **NO REGRESSION** — `cargo test --all` → **535 passed, 0 failed** over 39 suites (unchanged: this
    leaf adds a gate and an example mode, no test); `make focused` → `passed — 3 passed, 0 failed,
    0 unavailable, 0 not built`, `exit=0`; `bash scripts/check_doctrines.sh` → `=== all doctrines green
    ===`; `mdbook build docs/book` → `rc=0`; `bash -n` clean on the new script. `make integration` →
    `tier integration: failed — 6 passed, 1 failed`, and the one failure is the **pre-existing**
    `emulator` step (`TARGET_VERIFIED=no`, owned by `M2.8`), not this leaf: its `doctrines` step, which now
    runs the freeze gate, is green. ⛔ **Two defects in the gate, both found by its own arms rather than
    by reading it**: `if report="$(classify …)"; then return; fi` followed by `case "$?"` — bash returns
    **0** from an `if` whose condition failed with no `else`, so every real movement would have taken the
    "not a readable baseline" branch; and the arms' id parser read the classifier's *summary* line as a
    difference, producing an id of `1` that no note could name, so a covered movement looked uncovered
    (arm 6 failed on it, `8 pass / 1 fail`, before the fix). Both are comments in the script now.
    **Cost measured, not assumed**: `time ./scripts/check_language_freeze.sh` → **1.52 s, 1.60 s, 1.66 s**
    warm over three runs, nearly all of it the `cargo run` that prints canonical forms, so the pre-commit
    doctrine path roughly doubles. Kept there rather than moved to CI, because a freeze that runs only in

    the cheap fallback is a shape-only leg in the hook with the comparison in `integration`.
  - [x] **REPRODUCE** — `scripts/check_language_freeze.sh --self-test` → `9 pass / 0 fail (9 arms)`,
    `exit=0`. Three arms per leg and one against the real tree: a moved digest, a construct the baseline
    no longer freezes, a construct nobody declared, an agreeing baseline passing (so the refusals are not

    by a pending note, that note spent (`status: applied`) and therefore refused, and a pending note for a
    *different* construct; and — the arm the parent's ⛔ said only this leaf could have — a real
    description edited (`(defblock extra (offers x))` appended to
    `docs/semantics/cases/positive-minimal-system.eadl`), which fires the gate **naming that file**, with
    the restoration proven by `diff -q` inside a `trap` so an arm that fails still leaves the tree clean.
    Every arm's oracle requires this check's own word in the output as well as the exit code, because an
    oracle of "not zero" scores passes on a command that never ran —
    `docs/knowledge/verify-the-mutation-applied.md`.
  - [x] **LOCKSTEP** — `docs/book/src/verification.md` gains "The freeze, and the note it demands": both
    legs and what each catches, why the second is the one that makes the freeze mean something, the
    correction-is-still-a-change rule, and the honest limit. ⛔ It quotes **neither command's output**,
    and says why: both print counts, and a count in prose is the figure class `PROGRAM.20` registers —
    the same chapter had already been the place where `M1.13.4.3` found two live false ones.

- ID: `M1.14`
  Status: `done`
  Goal: answer the open question left by `M1.11` — whether an **external** engine can serve as a
  third independent recognizer of `docs/semantics/grammar.md` — by measuring LinkedSpec's Rust
  backend against real eADL, and route what was found back to that project as reproducible
  feedback rather than an opinion.
  Acceptance: the verdict rests on executed commands, not on reading the upstream guide; every
  defect reported upstream carries a reproducer that runs **without archogen**; anything archogen
  got wrong on first reading is corrected in the report rather than dropped.

  Commit: `pending`

  ### Acceptance Checklist (enforced by `TASK-ACCEPTANCE`)

  - [x] **ROOT CAUSE (WHY + WHERE)** — `M1.11` made the grammar normative and proved the reader
    and the EBNF agree on acceptance *and* token segmentation, but both arms are archogen's own.
    §12 M2's standard — a checker "sharing the same erroneous recurrence with its reference does
    not qualify as independent" — applies to the frontend exactly as it does to the runtime, and
    nothing in the tree supplied a reader written by anyone else. WHERE: the gap is `M1.11`'s own
    closing paragraph, which scopes the conformance claim to "the normative grammar and the
    reader" and leaves a third opinion unowned. LinkedSpec was proposed as that third opinion;
    the question is decidable only by running it, and the upstream guide names ARCHOGEN in its
    own example, so the evaluation was owed in both directions.
  - [x] **ADDRESSED (verified)** — measured, not read. The documented vendoring layout does not
    build inside a Cargo workspace: `cargo` reports `could not compile` upstream of two manifests
    with "current package believes it's in a workspace when it's not", and the PGEN bootstrap
    exits `rc=101` for the same reason. With an empty `[workspace]` table added to both, the
    bootstrap reaches `Bootstrap complete` (`rc=0`) and `lispish_file` builds
    (`cargo build … Finished` in 33.94s, rustc 1.95.0). Against that working build, twelve probes
    drawn from real eADL constructs were executed and frozen in
    `docs/feedback/linkedspec/probes/EXPECTED.txt`. The decisive one is not a probe but the real
    fixture: `lispish_file --grammar specs/Lispish.spec examples/s0-heartbeat/system.eadl`
    returns only `["defblock","console.uart",…]` at `exit=0` — three of four top-level
    declarations silently discarded on a success exit. **Verdict: the shipped grammar cannot be
    archogen's reader nor an independent cross-check**, because eADL's normative production
    `document = { trivia } , { form , { trivia } } , end` requires every form and an explicit
    end-of-input, and a recognizer that accepts by ignoring agrees on every accept and refuses
    nothing. The engine is not disqualified — `linkedspec-runtime` is correctly insulated by its
    own workspace root — so the route stays open behind an upstream complete-input mode.
  - [x] **NO REGRESSION** — no archogen crate, manifest or test was touched by this leaf: the
    staged set is documentation, two reproducer scripts, a probe corpus and a submodule pointer.
    `bash scripts/check_doctrines.sh` → `=== all doctrines green ===`; `bash -n` clean on both
    new scripts; the reproducer is self-checking and reports
    `RESULT: reproduced (2/2) and fixed by the proposal (2/2).` at `rc=0`. The `M2.9` working set
    is untouched and still unstaged — `git diff --name-only` lists exactly the seven `M2.9` files
    and nothing from this leaf.
  - [x] **REPRODUCE** — `bash docs/feedback/linkedspec/repro/repro-workspace-collision.sh
    <linkedspec>` reproduces the build blocker from any LinkedSpec checkout and asserts the
    proposed fix resolves it; `bash docs/feedback/linkedspec/run-probes.sh` replays the twelve
    Lispish observations and diffs against the frozen `EXPECTED.txt`.
  - [x] **LOCKSTEP** — `docs/feedback/README.md` indexes the new outbound-feedback layer;
    `.gitignore` excludes the application-owned `.app-data/` build root the upstream guide
    requires. The frontier does not move: `M1.12` remains next, so `docs/TASK_TREE.md` is
    unchanged by design.

  ### What this establishes, and what it does not

  It establishes that **the shipped Lispish grammar is an extraction tool, not a validator**, and
  that archogen measured this rather than inferring it from the upstream guide — which documents
  the behaviour honestly and predicted it. It does **not** establish that LinkedSpec's engine is
  unsuitable: the one change that would decide it, a complete-input mode, is upstream work
  archogen does not own. Until that exists, `M1.11`'s two arms remain archogen's own, and the
  third independent opinion stays an open question rather than a closed one.

  ⚠️ This leaf deliberately produced **no archogen code**. The vendored submodule is a
  development-time evaluation dependency and is not referenced by any crate; nothing in the
  build, the tiers or the shipped toolchain depends on LinkedSpec.

  It establishes that the upstream report is **actionable per-defect**: LinkedSpec can set

  ### Acceptance Checklist (enforced by `TASK-ACCEPTANCE`)

  - [x] **ROOT CAUSE (WHY + WHERE)** — `M1.15` gave each bug an ID and a state but left the
    materials pooled: one `repro/` and one `evidence/probes/` served all seven, so acting on

    which were irrelevant to it. WHERE: the coupling was in the layout, and it had a measurable
    consequence — a single shared `EXPECTED.txt` meant **any** upstream fix changed the frozen
    file for every issue at once, so no issue could be moved to `verified` independently of the
    rest. There was also no register: the status board lived inside prose, and nothing recorded
    totals, so "what is still open" could not be answered without reading seven files.

    not run). All seven were executed after the split: `reproducers: 7 pass / 0 fail`, including
    two written for this leaf — `LS-004` now proves its three symptoms independently
    (`failed cargo invocations in log … 2`, `'seeded.' messages printed … 1`,
    `files actually in generated/ … 0`) rather than being a side-observation of `LS-001`, and
    `LS-005` checks the guide's section order mechanically (`'Add and pin' … line 17`,
    `'Initial PGEN preparation' … line 89`, `forward references … 0`). `INDEX.md` is the
    per-vendor register — every bug, state, severity, opened and last-verified date, plus totals
