    the layer reports `broken links: 0`; `bash -n` parses all seven reproducers; every `repro.sh`
    is mode `-rwxr-xr-x`; no frozen observation contains a checkout-specific path (a census
    over the three absolute home/volume prefixes DOCPATH names → no match). `bash scripts/check_doctrines.sh` →
    `=== all doctrines green ===`. The `M2.9` working set is untouched and still unstaged.
  - [x] **FIX (root cause, not symptom)** — two defects in this leaf's own scaffolding were found
    by running it rather than by reading it, and both were fixed at source: the generated
    `EXPECTED.txt` files were first written by a redirect that created the file **before** the
    script ran, so each froze its own "observation DIFFERS" diff instead of the observation; and
    probe `06`'s diagnostic embedded the absolute input path, which would have made every frozen
    file machine-specific. The reproducer now substitutes the basename into diagnostics, so the
    frozen files are portable by construction rather than by sanitising afterwards.
  - [x] **REPRODUCE** — three issues need only a checkout
    (`bash issues/LS-00{1,4,5}-*/repro.sh /path/to/linkedspec`); four share one build described in

    every issue's scratch directory is covered. The frontier does not move: `M1.12` remains next.

  ### What this establishes, and what it does not

  It establishes that the tracker is **usable by its recipient**, not only by its author: one bug
  can be reproduced, fixed and re-verified without touching another's directory, and the register
  answers "what is still open" in one table. It does **not** add or change a finding — all seven
  measurements are `M1.14`'s, re-executed unchanged. Two blockers remain open upstream, and
  archogen's own question — whether an external engine can be an independent recognizer — stays
  open behind `LS-002`.

  ### Acceptance Checklist (enforced by `TASK-ACCEPTANCE`)

  - [x] **ROOT CAUSE (WHY + WHERE)** — `M1.16` gave each bug its own directory but left four of
    them pointing at a shared `../../SETUP.md` for the build, and several cross-linking each other

    invisible locally, because the neighbour file exists here — the census found `13` escaping
    references across seven issues, and `grep` proved they were in prose *and* in the scripts.
  - [x] **ADDRESSED (verified)** — every issue now carries its own `SETUP.md` describing only the
    steps it needs (three need a checkout; four describe the full build from clone to binary), its
    own copy of the state model, and no reference leaving its directory: a census over
    `issues/*/` for `../` returns no match. All seven reproducers were re-run after the split —
    `reproducers: 7 pass / 0 fail` — and the tracker's own root `SETUP.md` was deleted rather
    than kept, because two copies of a build procedure drift. `INDEX.md` and both README layouts
    were rewritten to match.
  - [x] **NO REGRESSION** — `git diff --cached --stat` reports 26 files, confined to
    `docs/feedback/`, `scripts/` and `DOCTRINE_ENFORCEMENT.md`; no archogen crate, manifest or
    test touched. A link census over the layer reports `broken links: 0`;
    `bash scripts/check_doctrines.sh` → `=== all doctrines green ===`. The `M2.9` working set is
    untouched and still unstaged.
  - [x] **FIX (root cause, not symptom)** — the property is now **enforced**, not promised:
    `scripts/check_feedback_self_contained.sh` proves four legs per issue directory —
    completeness, closure, portability and registration — and is registered in the
    `PROJECT-SPECIFIC` slot, so a breach blocks the commit. ⭐ It paid for itself immediately:
    on its first run it caught **five** escaping references I had missed, all inside `repro.sh`
    files rather than the prose I had been reading.
  - [x] **REPRODUCE** — `bash scripts/check_feedback_self_contained.sh --self-test` →
    `self-test: GREEN arm passes` and `self-test: 6/6 red arms fired`, covering a missing
    `SETUP.md`, an escaping reference, a checkout-specific path, a non-executable `repro.sh`, an
    empty `evidence/`, and an issue absent from its register.
  - [x] **LOCKSTEP** — `DOCTRINE_ENFORCEMENT.md` gains the `FEEDBACK-SELF-CONTAINED` row with its
    four legs and its honest limit; `scripts/check_doctrines.project.sh` registers it;
    `docs/feedback/README.md` and `docs/feedback/linkedspec/README.md` both describe the new
    shape and say the property is enforced rather than promised. The frontier does not move:
    `M1.12` remains next.

  ### A defect in the check itself, found by running it

  The first version of the self-test re-executed the script through `$0` against a temporary
  fixture. It **hung**, and a bounded trace showed why: the recursion's guard compared the base
  directory against a literal, which held for the parent and not the child, so the fixture arm
  and the real arm could interleave. Rewriting the doctrine as a `scan()` function called
  directly — no re-execution at all — removed the recursion rather than guarding it. ⚠️ Worth
  recording because the failure was in the *test of the check*, which is the one place a green
  result is least likely to be questioned.

  ### What this establishes, and what it does not

  It establishes that a single bug directory is a **deliverable**: complete, closed, portable and
  registered, verified by a gate with six demonstrated RED arms. It does **not** establish that
  any reproducer still reproduces — that is what each issue's exit-code contract carries, and it
  is re-measured by running them, as this leaf did (`7 pass / 0 fail`).

  in its issue's `evidence/REMEASURED.txt`; LS-002 and LS-003 measured on the document route, with
  the historical extraction re-run as a guard and matching its frozen `EXPECTED.txt`; no report
  needed the "still fails" branch, because all five came back gone — and the register says so only
  where an archogen rerun is behind it; states and totals match all seven sub-trees; LS-006 and
  LS-007 unchanged; and no frozen observation, reproducer or probe input was edited at any point
  (`git status --porcelain` on each, empty, in every child leaf).
  Commit: `ARCHOGEN-LINKEDSPEC-0057 (leaf M1.20.7)`

  **Why this leaf was split.** One re-measurement is five measurements with three different
  prerequisites: a documentation check that needs no build, a workspace check that needs only
  `cargo metadata`, and three checks that need RGX's prepared parser and a freshly built native
  consumer. A single leaf would hold all of that uncommitted for the whole run, which is exactly
  what the pivot rule and the crash-recovery requirement exist to prevent. The children were
  ordered cheapest-and-most-independent first, and each one committed its own evidence — six
  commits, so a crash at any point lost at most one measurement.

  ⭐ **What the split found that a single leaf would probably have missed.** Because `M1.20.3` was
  its own commit, its preparation ran before anything depended on it, and it caught a generated
  parser dated a week earlier and built at the *previous* PGEN pin — which a bootstrap that is
  idempotent on existence would have silently reused, making `M1.20.4`–`.6` measure the wrong
  revision while every exit status read `0`. Two further findings were filed rather than folded in:
  a 753 MB log from one successful preparation (`M1.21`), and the reopening of `M1.14`'s
  independent-recognizer closure now that a complete-input mode exists (`M1.22`).

    among them. WHY the row could not move: the frozen `repro.sh` decides by patching the vendored
    manifests (its Part 2), and the guide at the pin forbids that remedy — "do not modify dependency
    manifests or add vendored crates to the application's workspace members to work around this
    error" — so no instrument could reach a `verified` verdict without writing into a submodule.

    manifest with its own boundary must come back `0`; a manifest failing for an unrelated reason

- ID: `M1.20.3`
  Status: `done`
  Goal: re-measure **LS-004** (bootstrap false success) at `2ac834913` through RGX's **public**
  `make bootstrap`, as the completion notice directs — not by reconstructing PGEN's internal
  generation steps. Four arms: two independent empty-store offline failure controls, one fresh
  successful preparation, one prepared reuse.
  Acceptance: each arm records its command, exit status and the log lines that matter; the failure
  arms stop at the first missing prerequisite with **no** later named step and **no** seed-success
  text; the success arm exits `0` and leaves the generated parser present; the reuse arm exits `0`
  as a no-op; the stale generated sources inherited from the `8763a0e6`-era build are regenerated
  rather than silently reused, which the guide at the pin warns about explicitly.
  Verification: see the acceptance checklist below.
  Commit: `ARCHOGEN-LINKEDSPEC-0053 (leaf M1.20.3)`

  ### Acceptance Checklist (enforced by `TASK-ACCEPTANCE`)

  - [x] **REPRODUCE / ISSUE** — LS-004 was `fixed-upstream` on the notice's word, and the historical
    instrument could not be used to check it: `repro.sh` provokes the failure through LS-001's
    workspace collision, which `M1.20.2` just measured as fixed, and it does so by calling PGEN's
    internal target `regex_parser_bootstrap` directly. The guide at the pin states the boundary —
    "Applications should not reproduce PGEN's internal generation steps or modify either submodule" —
    so the frozen reproducer is now out of contract, and `git grep -ln 'remeasure' 2acc76e --
    docs/feedback/linkedspec/issues/LS-004-bootstrap-false-success` → no match, `rc=1`.
  - [x] **ROOT CAUSE (WHY + WHERE)** — WHERE, and it is not where the report was: the defect lived in
    PGEN's Makefile recipe, but at the pin the *measurement* problem was archogen's own checkout
    state. `ls -la vendor/linkedspec/rgx/subs/pgen/generated` → 12 files dated `Sep 20 10:07`, i.e.
    generated at PGEN `db6f8c68`, while the adopted pin carries `d9d41c28`
    (`git -C vendor/linkedspec/rgx submodule status` → ` d9d41c28… subs/pgen`). RGX's published
    downstream contract says why that is fatal: "`make bootstrap` is idempotent on *existence*, so
    after bumping the PGEN pin you must force a regeneration … or you will keep building against the
    previous pin's parser." A rerun would therefore have exited `0`, changed nothing, and let every
    later leaf measure the wrong revision. WHY nothing caught it before: no leaf between `M1.19` and
    this one had a reason to look at build-output timestamps, and the pin adoption leaves measured
    only tracked paths.
  - [x] **ADDRESSED (verified)** — four arms, all through the published interface, all frozen in
    `docs/feedback/linkedspec/issues/LS-004-bootstrap-false-success/evidence/REMEASURED.txt`
    (224 lines). `remeasure.sh self-test` → `self-test: 9/9 arms passed`, `rc=0`. Failure control 1
    (empty store, `CARGO_NET_OFFLINE=true`) → `make` exit `2`, log 14 lines: one
    `error: no matching package named … found`, then `make[1]: *** [regex_parser_bootstrap] Error
    101` and `make: *** [bootstrap] Error 2`, with **nothing after them**; classifier:
    `'^error' lines 1 · missing build product invoked 0 · seeding messages printed 0 · named steps
    after the first failure 0 · files in generated/ afterwards 0` → verdict `rc=0`, GONE. Failure
    control 2 (a second, independent empty store) → identical. Fresh preparation → `make` exit `0`,
    `✅ Bootstrap complete.`, `files in generated/ .......... 12`, and
    `parser sources ............... regenerated: 50eec63c9ba79b16 -> 196db2eefed767ff`. Prepared
    reuse → exit `0`, `[rgx] PGEN parser already generated — nothing to bootstrap.`,
    `parser sources ............... UNCHANGED (196db2eefed767ff)`, exactly the no-op the guide says
    to distinguish by exit status alone.
  - [x] **NO REGRESSION** — nothing tracked in either submodule was modified:
    `git status --porcelain` at the repository root → empty after the arms, and
    `git submodule status` still reads ` 2ac834913…` with no `+` or `-` prefix, so neither gitlink
    moved. The frozen artifacts are untouched (`git status --porcelain` on `repro.sh` and
    `evidence/OBSERVED.txt` → empty). The removals were confined to build output the published
    contract tells a consumer to remove, and the parser was backed up before the first removal —
    `backed up 12 generated files … (verified by count)` — with the stale copy kept for comparison.
    `bash -n remeasure.sh` → clean; `bash scripts/check_feedback_self_contained.sh` → `rc=0` and its
    own self-test `6/6`; `bash scripts/check_doctrines.sh` → `=== all doctrines green ===`. No Rust or
    manifest path is staged — `git diff --cached --name-only | grep -cE '\.rs$|Cargo\.'` → `0` — so
    the application suite is unaffected by construction; `make focused` → exit `0` and
    `cargo test --all` → **421 passed, 0 failed** over 36 suites, unchanged.
  - [x] **FIX** — an instrument with two layers, because the arms are expensive and the decision is
    cheap: a pure `classify` function over (log, exit status, generated-file count, arm kind) with
    the criteria pre-registered in its header from the report's own "Expected" sentence, and thin arm
    runners that drive the published command and feed it. `--self-test` exercises the classifier
    against nine logs, the first of which is the historical tail from `evidence/OBSERVED.txt`
    verbatim and must come back `STILL PRESENT`.
    ⛔ **The classifier was wrong first, and an arm caught it.** It counted any seeding message as
    symptom 1, so the successful preparation printed `symptom 1 — PRESENT` over a clean run. A seed
    claim indicts a run only if the run *failed*: `🌱 Seeding …` is printed before the step that can
    fail and is ordinary logging, while `generated/ebnf.rs seeded.` is a completion claim and the only
    form that can be false. The condition is now `seeds >= 1 AND rc != 0`, and arm 9 pins it. The
    arms were then all re-run, so the frozen evidence comes from one coherent instrument rather than
    two versions of it.
    ⭐ **The destructive arms were sequenced and made reversible.** A failure control has to remove
    `generated/` to provoke anything, so both controls ran last and the freshly generated parser was
    restored from a copy verified by digest: `196db2eefed767ff` before, `196db2eefed767ff` after,
    12 files both times. The prepared state `M1.20.4`–`.6` need is therefore intact.
  - [x] **LOCKSTEP** — the issue's `README.md` (State → `verified`, `Fixed in` and `Re-measurement`
    rows, the do-not-re-run warning with its reason, the four-arm table, the two classifier traps,
    the staleness warning, the cost finding, a History line), its `SETUP.md` (both revisions, the
    documented storage exports, a per-arm cost/write table, both exit contracts),
    `evidence/REMEASURED.txt`; `docs/feedback/linkedspec/INDEX.md` (row, totals `fixed-upstream 2` /
    `verified 3`, the pending paragraph, the new-finding notice, the instrument-needs table) and its

  - [x] **REPRODUCE / ISSUE** — the register's last outstanding **blocker**, and the item the tracker

    exit, at the current revision.

    — a grammar whose entry rule dispatches to one form and never requires end-of-input cannot
    implement eADL's normative `document = { trivia } , { form , { trivia } } , end`. Upstream's
    remedy is a **second route**: `specs/SExprDocumentV1.spec` (entry rule `Document`) plus the
    `sexpr_file` adapter, and the notice is explicit that "changing only the old adapter's grammar
    argument does not adopt the new result contract". Confirmed independently here: pointing
    `sexpr_file` at `Lispish.spec` exits `1` with the typed diagnostic `entry_rule_not_found` —
    "entry rule 'Document' is not defined". WHY the row could not move before now: no build of the
    document consumer existed in this repository (`M1.20.4` produced it), and no instrument scored
    *form counts* against a written expectation.
  - [x] **ADDRESSED (verified)** — all eight frozen probes on the document route, each against an
    expectation written into the instrument before the run: `01-multiple-top-level-forms` → **2 of 2**
    forms `rc=0`; `12-two-forms-one-line` → **2 of 2** `rc=0`; `system.eadl` → **4 of 4** `rc=0`;
    `08-comment-no-newline` → 1 of 1 `rc=0`; `10-semantically-invalid` → 1 of 1 `rc=0`;
    `07-trailing-garbage`, `09-unbalanced-close`, `06-unterminated-form` → **rejected** `rc=1` with no
    partial value on stdout. Instrument: `probes ........ 8 · as expected 8 · defect 0 · undecided 0`,
    `rc=0`, `RESULT — the defect is GONE`. The decisive probe was then re-read independently of the
    instrument: the frozen JSON for `system.eadl` parses to `format: linkedspec-sexpr-v1 |
    top-level forms: 4`, heads `['defblock', 'defplatform', 'defservice', 'defsystem']` — all four
    declarations, where the original observation returned only `defblock`.
    `--self-test` → `9/9 arms passed`, including one form where two are expected (must be `1`),
    accepting trailing junk (must be `1`), rejecting while still printing a value (must be `1`, P3),
    more forms than the input has (must be `1`), an unreadable result (must be refused, `2`), and a
    document expected to be accepted being rejected (must be `2` — over-rejection is a *different*
    defect and is not scored as either).
    **The regression guard agrees with the frozen record:** `repro.sh` on the historical route →
    `rc=0`, `RESULT — observation matches evidence/EXPECTED.txt`, all eight lines identical. So the
    extraction route is unchanged, as documented, and nothing about this verdict rests on it having
    changed.

    `evidence/EXPECTED.txt`, `repro.sh` and every `evidence/*.eadl` probe → empty; the only new files

    historical route and the guard just re-confirmed it byte for byte. No Rust or manifest path is
    staged — `git diff --cached --name-only | grep -cE '\.rs$|Cargo\.'` → `0` — `make focused` →
    exit `0`, `cargo test --all` → **421 passed, 0 failed** over 36 suites, and
    `bash scripts/check_doctrines.sh` → `=== all doctrines green ===` on the staged set. The
    self-contained doctrine holds on the new files (no parent-relative path, no checkout-specific
    absolute path), which is why the instrument resolves its arguments to absolute paths internally
    and prints only basenames.
  - [x] **FIX** — a property instrument with a pure evaluator at its centre: `evaluate <probe> <rc>
    <stdout> <expectation>` returns as-expected / defect / undecided, and every arm of `--self-test`
    exercises that function on synthetic results, so the nine arms cost nothing and need no binaries.
    Form counting is delegated to `python3` and **refuses** without it rather than falling back to a
    regex over JSON — counting top-level forms by pattern-matching `"kind":"list"` would have counted
    nested lists too and reported `system.eadl` as having far more than four.
    ⭐ One real bug found and fixed while building it: the guard was invoked with the relative paths
    the caller supplied, from a subshell that had changed directory, so `repro.sh` reported
    `--bin <lispish_file> is required` and the guard silently did nothing. Paths are now resolved
    before use. A guard that reports "could not run" is at least honest; one whose failure is
    scrolled past is not, and only reading the output caught it.

  - [x] **REPRODUCE / ISSUE** — the last row in the register still resting on the vendor's word, and
    the one whose erasure archogen called *semantic rather than cosmetic*: a quoted string is a value
    and a bare symbol is an identifier that must resolve. At the pin, before writing anything, the
    historical route still collapses the two: `repro.sh --bin <bin>/lispish_file --grammar
    <checkout>/specs/Lispish.spec` → `04-quoted-string.eadl | 0 | ["name","ARCHOGEN"]` and
    `05-bare-symbol.eadl | 0 | ["name","ARCHOGEN"]` — two different inputs, one output, on a success
    exit.
  - [x] **ROOT CAUSE (WHY + WHERE)** — WHERE: the erasure was in the *route's result type*, not in a
    bug: the historical `SExpression` adapter has only `Atom(String)` and `List(...)`, and the guide
    states that Lispish's parent rules discard the distinction between symbols, quoted strings and
    numeric tokens. The document grammar's contract is the opposite — every atom carries `kind` and a
    string-valued `lexeme`, quotes included, with no numeric conversion and no escape decoding. WHY
    the row could not move before: the same two reasons as `M1.20.5` — no build of the document
    consumer existed here until `M1.20.4`, and no instrument compared *kinds and spellings* against a
    written expectation. `git grep -ln 'remeasure' b1d514f --
    docs/feedback/linkedspec/issues/LS-003-token-kind-erasure` → no match, `rc=1`.
  - [x] **ADDRESSED (verified)** — all three frozen probes flattened to `kind:lexeme` atoms in
    document order and compared with expectations written into the instrument before the run:
    `03-number-unit-tokens` → `symbol:task symbol:beat symbol:period number:10 symbol:ms
    symbol:deadline number:10 symbol:ms`; `04-quoted-string` → `symbol:name string:"ARCHOGEN"` —
    **both quotes inside the lexeme**; `05-bare-symbol` → `symbol:name symbol:ARCHOGEN`. Instrument:
    `checks ........ 4 · as expected 4 · defect 0 · undecided 0`, `rc=0`, `RESULT — the defect is
    GONE`. The fourth check is the cross-probe one that *is* this defect — probe 04 and probe 05 must
    not produce the same atoms: `probe 04 vs probe 05 ......... DIFFERENT — the distinction
    survives`. `--self-test` → `9/9 arms passed`, including the historical erasure of a quoted string
    (must be `1`), a numeric conversion `10` → `10.0` (must be `1`), an **untagged** result (must be
    refused as `2`, since scoring the wrong adapter's output either way would be a lie), unreadable
    JSON (`2`), and a valid one-form input being rejected (`2`, not scored as this defect).
    **The guard agrees with the frozen record:** `repro.sh` → `rc=0`, `RESULT — observation matches
    evidence/EXPECTED.txt`, all three lines identical.
  - [x] **NO REGRESSION** — the frozen artifacts are untouched: `git status --porcelain` on
    `evidence/EXPECTED.txt`, `repro.sh` and all three `evidence/*.eadl` probes → empty; the only new
    files are `remeasure.sh` and `evidence/REMEASURED.txt` (95 lines, assembled by running the
    instruments). No Rust or manifest path is staged —
    `git diff --cached --name-only | grep -cE '\.rs$|Cargo\.'` → `0` — `make focused` → exit `0`,
    `cargo test --all` → **421 passed, 0 failed** over 36 suites, and
    `bash scripts/check_doctrines.sh` → `=== all doctrines green ===` on the staged set. The
    self-contained doctrine holds on the new files: no parent-relative path and no checkout-specific
    absolute path in either, which is why the instrument resolves its arguments internally and prints
    only basenames.
  - [x] **FIX** — the same shape as `M1.20.5`, deliberately: a pure scorer over (probe, exit status,
    result) whose expectations are a table in the script, checked by nine arms that need no binaries.
    Reading the tagged result is delegated to `python3` and **refuses** without it, because judging
    kinds by pattern-matching text would confuse a nested list for an atom.
    ⛔ **The verdict is scoped, and the scope is written down.** These are the *document grammar's*
    lexical kinds, not eADL's: its published rule makes `0x4_0000` a number and `1__0` and `1.`
    symbols, while eADL's exactness rules — no floating point anywhere, canonical number spelling —
    are archogen's own, stricter, and enforced by archogen's reader. The issue page and the evidence
    both say that a consumer adopting this route inherits a lexical contract, not eADL's, so nobody
    reads `verified` as "this grammar classifies tokens the way `docs/semantics/grammar.md` does".
    That distinction is exactly what `M1.22` will measure rather than assume.

- ID: `M1.21`
  Status: `done`
  Goal: file the **cost finding** from `M1.20.3` as its own row in the LinkedSpec register — one
  successful run of the published preparation interface wrote a **753 MB** log of 4 008 986 lines,
  1 151 376 of them `[PGEN][DBG]` generator progress lines — while the vendor's guide instructs a
  consumer that if the command fails it must "stop before building the application and preserve its
  exit status and full log".
  Reproduce / issue: measured, not estimated. `du -h` → `753M`, `wc -l` → `4008986`,
  `grep -c '\[PGEN\]\[DBG\]'` → `1151376`, on the log of the fresh-preparation arm at LinkedSpec
  `2ac834913` / RGX `f6e5acdc9` / PGEN `d9d41c28`, Darwin arm64, Rust 1.95.0. Impact: the documented
  failure procedure costs three quarters of a gigabyte per attempt, which is why the log was measured
  and then released rather than kept; a consumer that follows the guide literally on a small volume
  or in CI fills a disk, and a consumer that does not follow it loses the evidence the guide says to
  preserve. It is a property of the interface, not of any reported defect, which is why it was kept
  out of LS-004's verdict.
  Acceptance: a self-contained `LS-008` sub-tree exists with `README.md`, `SETUP.md`, an executable
  `repro.sh` whose exit code is its verdict, and frozen `evidence/`, meeting
  `FEEDBACK-SELF-CONTAINED`; the reproducer measures log volume from a preparation run **or** from a
  supplied log, so it can be replayed without a five-minute bootstrap; the register row, totals and
  tracker counts are updated in the same commit; the report states the measurement, the guide
  sentence it collides with, and a proposed remedy (a quiet or log-level flag on the published
  interface, or a documented volume expectation) without prescribing an implementation; whether it is
  a defect or a documented cost is left to the vendor, and the row's severity says so.
  Priority: **low** — nothing archogen builds depends on it, and the workaround (do not capture the
  log, or capture and release it) is what this repository does today. It is filed because finding an
  issue creates an obligation to own it, and because a consumer following the guide literally would
  pay for it.
  Verification: see the checklist — measured again on a fresh run at the same revisions, because two records of the
  first measurement disagreed; `LS-008` filed, self-contained and registered.

- ID: `M1.22`
  Status: `done`
  Goal: settle by measurement whether LinkedSpec's `SExprDocumentV1.spec` can serve as a **third
  independent recognizer** of archogen's normative surface syntax (`docs/semantics/grammar.md`) — the
  question `M1.11` raised and `M1.14` closed, on the single ground that no complete-input mode
  existed. `M1.20.5` removed that ground: the document route returns every top-level form of
  archogen's real four-form description and rejects leading, intervening and trailing input with a
  typed error and no partial value, on archogen's own frozen probes.
  Reproduce / issue: `M1.14`'s verdict was "the shipped grammar cannot be archogen's reader nor an
  independent cross-check … the route stays open behind an upstream complete-input mode." That mode
  now exists and is `verified` at the adopted pin. Leaving the closure standing would mean the
  normative grammar keeps exactly two independent opinions — archogen's reader and archogen's
  conformance suite, which are the same codebase's two views — when a genuinely third one is
  available for the cost of a harness.
  Acceptance: a differential harness runs archogen's reader and the vendored recognizer over a named
  corpus (the S0 fixtures, the semantic corpus, the grammar conformance cases) and reports agreement
  or disagreement per document, as a development-time tool with **no dependency added to the engine
  workspace** — the recognizer is invoked as an external process, per the zero-dependency decision
  and §4.4; a ratchet-style test fails if a previously agreeing document starts to disagree, and
  never passes by deletion; the comparison is scoped to what archogen's grammar actually defines
  (token boundaries and document completeness), **not** to eADL semantics, and the leaf says so
  explicitly, because the recognizer classifies `-1` as a number while eADL's exactness rules are
  archogen's own; every divergence found is enumerated and classified as archogen stricter, the
  recognizer stricter, or a genuine mismatch needing a decision; a written verdict lands in the leaf
  and in the book chapter that carries the verification story, since the book is the director's only
  view of this.
  Priority: **medium** — it is the third opinion `M1.11` asked for, now unblocked. It does not gate
  `M1.12` or `M1.13`, and it must not be started before `M1.20` closes, because its corpus and its
  build depend on the state that tree leaves behind.
  **Verdict, `2026-09-30`: the third opinion is real, and it earned its place on its first run.** Over every tracked
  description, 119 of 120 agree with archogen's reader on their forms. The one that does not is `LS-007`'s evidence,
  where the recognizer refuses and archogen accepts. The grammar-derived recognizer sides with LinkedSpec: archogen's
  reader is more permissive than `grammar.md`, which neither of archogen's two opinions had seen. Filed as `M1.37`.
  Verification: see the checklist — `scripts/third_opinion.sh` with nine arms; the first record written and checked;
  the ratchet shown firing on the real corpus.
  Commit: `ARCHOGEN-M1-0167 (leaf M1.22)`

  ### Acceptance Checklist (enforced by `TASK-ACCEPTANCE`)

  - [x] **REPRODUCE / ISSUE** — the route was closed on one ground, and that ground is gone:

    ```text
    $ .app-data/target-2ac834913/debug/sexpr_file --grammar <SExprDocumentV1.spec> examples/periodic-three/system.eadl
      {"format":"linkedspec-sexpr-v1","forms":[…]}   — 9 forms; and on "(a b) junk": {"type":"document_parse_error",…}
    ```
  - [x] **ROOT CAUSE (WHY + WHERE)** — `grammar.md` had two opinions from one codebase, the reader and the conformance

- ID: `M1.23`
  Status: `done`
  Goal: two **live** surfaces still publish the schema/classifier reach that `M1.7` superseded
  **47 commits ago**, and the number they publish is *carried* rather than derived — so nothing
  would have noticed the next time it moved. Restate the current measurement in both, and make
  the number a gated one.
  Reproduce / issue: `crates/eadl-model/src/kind.rs:35` and `docs/book/src/kinds.md:70` both say
  the schema refuses **10 of the 11** rejected boundary cases, that `execution-bound` is caught
  *only* by the classifier, and that the two mechanisms' "reach is not identical" with a test
  that "pins the split in both directions". All four clauses are false at `HEAD`:
  `the_schema_now_reaches_every_rejected_case` asserts `out_of_reach.is_empty()` over a corpus it
  counts at **13** (`test result: ok. 1 passed`), `defsystem`'s clause is
  `(holds kind task)` (`core.eadl:82`), and the classifier refuses the same 13. Both sentences
  were written at `b53eb85` (`M1.2`) when the reject corpus really was 11, and were superseded at
  `9030111` (`M1.7`) — which updated `tests/kinds.rs`, `workload.md` and the corpus, but not
  these two. Impact: **the book contradicts itself** — `kinds.md:70` says 10 of 11 while
  `workload.md:64` says 13 of 13, both reachable from `SUMMARY.md` — and the book is the
  director's only window into the project. `kind.rs`'s header is worse than stale prose: it
  tells a reader the split is still pinned, which sends them looking for a test that no longer
  exists.
  Acceptance: all three live surfaces that state the reach (`kind.rs`'s header, `kinds.md`,
  `workload.md`) agree with the measurement and with each other; the number is **gated** — a test
  measures `(refused, total)` from the corpus and fails if any of the three publishes a different
  pair, in the `profile.rs::the_published_page_lists_exactly_the_excluded_capabilities` idiom, so
  a corpus change lands on whoever made it; the *historical* statements (`workload.md`'s past-tense
  "was 10 of 11", `CHANGELOG.md`, `M1.2`/`M1.7`'s closed leaves, and `M1`'s Decisions log, which
  already carries its own superseding entry) are deliberately left untouched, because a record of
  a commit's state is not a live claim; the prose keeps the part that is still true and still
  load-bearing — the two mechanisms are independent, and equal reach on *one* corpus is not
  equivalence.
  Priority: **medium-high** — a false statement in the director's only view of the project, and a
  self-contradiction between two chapters of the same book. Not blocking `M1.12`, but it is the
  same failure `M1.12` exists to end for the language: a figure nothing re-derives. Census behind
  that claim, classified in the ROOT CAUSE box below:
  `grep -rnE '[0-9]+ of (the )?[0-9]+' crates/*/src/*.rs docs/book/src/*.md` → 9 hits, 2 live and
  false, 7 correct records or past tense.
  Verification: see the acceptance checklist below.
  Commit: `ARCHOGEN-M1-0061`

  ### Acceptance Checklist (enforced by `TASK-ACCEPTANCE`)

  - [x] **ROOT CAUSE (WHY + WHERE)** — WHY: `M1.7` (`9030111`) moved the measurement and updated
    the test that pinned it, the corpus, and `docs/book/src/workload.md` — but the same figure had
    been **hand-copied** into two other live surfaces, and nothing compared prose to measurement.
    A number copied into prose is copied out of the reach of the test that took it, which is the
    anti-pattern `docs/CLAIM_VERIFICATION.md` §5B names ("derived or gated, never carried").
    WHERE: `crates/eadl-model/src/kind.rs:35` and `docs/book/src/kinds.md:70`. Measured, not
    assumed — the figure was **true when written** and was superseded later:
    `git ls-tree --name-only b53eb85 docs/semantics/boundary/reject/ | grep -c '\.eadl$'` → `11`
    (the denominator the prose names), versus `ls docs/semantics/boundary/reject/*.eadl | wc -l` →
    `13` and `git ls-tree --name-only HEAD … | grep -c '\.eadl$'` → `13` today.
    `git rev-list --count 9030111..HEAD` → `47`, so both surfaces were false for 47 commits.
    The contradicting measurement: `cargo test -p eadl-model --test kinds
    the_schema_now_reaches_every_rejected_case` → `test result: ok. 1 passed`, asserting a corpus
    total of `13` and `out_of_reach.is_empty()`.
    CENSUS of every `N of M` reach claim in the tree, classified before acting —
    `grep -rnE '[0-9]+ of (the )?[0-9]+' crates/*/src/*.rs docs/book/src/*.md` → **9** hits, of
    which **2** were live-and-false (the two above) and **7** were correct: `workload.md`'s
    past-tense history, `tests/kinds.rs`'s "previously asserted" comment, two `CHANGELOG.md`
    entries, and three inside `M1.2`/`M1.7`'s closed leaves and `M1`'s Decisions log — all records
    of a commit's state, which §B of that policy says are deliberately never re-derived.
  - [x] **ADDRESSED (verified)** — before → after on the symptom, measured on the same predicate.
    BEFORE: `grep -n '10 of the 11' crates/eadl-model/src/kind.rs docs/book/src/kinds.md` → 2 hits,
    both present tense, and the book contradicted itself (`kinds.md:70` said 10 of 11 while
    `workload.md:64` said 13 of 13, both reachable from `SUMMARY.md`).
    AFTER: the same grep over `crates/eadl-model/src/kind.rs` → **no match** (the header now states
    the rule and names `the_schema_now_reaches_every_rejected_case` instead of quoting a count);
    `kinds.md` carries exactly two figures — `was **10 of 11**` (history, past tense) and
    `now **13 of 13**` (the measurement) — and agrees with `workload.md`.
    The figure is now **gated**, not merely retyped: `cargo test -p eadl-model --test kinds
    the_live_surfaces_publish_the_measured_reach` → `test result: ok. 1 passed`, and the suite went
    `15 passed` → `16 passed; 0 failed`. Five RED arms were measured firing, each with the mutation
    confirmed applied and the crate confirmed compiling first: (A) revert the current figure to
    `10 of 11` → `does not publish the measured reach 13 of 13 anywhere`; (B) strip the past tense
    off the historical figure → `states \`10 of 11\` as though it were current`; (C) put a figure
    back into the module header → `carries a reach figure again: [(49, 13, 13, …)]`; (D) rename the
    measuring test → `no longer names the test that measures the reach`; (E) the corpus-grows
    regression, prose claiming `14 of 14` → `does not publish the measured reach 13 of 13`.
    Control restored → `test result: ok. 1 passed`.
    ⛔ **Arm C's first attempt was a false green and is recorded as such.** The mutation was applied
    with `perl -0pi -e 's{(…)}{…\$1}'`, where single quotes made `\$1` a *literal* `$1` rather than
    a backreference, so line 50 became `$1 is not equivalence.` and the crate failed to compile:
    `error: expected item, found \`$\`` / `error: could not compile \`eadl-model\` (lib)`. The arm's
    filter printed nothing, which read as a pass. The harness was rebuilt to report
    `mutation applied` and `compiles` before any verdict, and the arm was re-run with a compilable
    mutation — which is how it came to fire on `(49, 13, 13, …)` above. This is the failure
    `DEV_NOTES.md`'s "a mutation that did not apply is a false green" already records, reproduced
    here by the person who had just read it.
  - [x] **NO REGRESSION** — `make focused` → `tier focused: passed — 3 passed, 0 failed,
    0 unavailable, 0 not built`, `EXIT=0` (fmt `0.14s`, clippy `0.86s`, tests `2.92s`).
    `cargo test --all` → `suites=36 passed=422 failed=0`, against a baseline of **421** — the delta
    is exactly the one new gate, so no test was lost or skipped. `make gate` →
    `=== all doctrines green ===`, all 13 including `TABLE-ARITY-RATCHET` (this leaf adds table-free
    prose) and `BOOK-ANCHORS` (both edited chapters still cite real paths).
  - [x] **FIX** — three changes, the minimum that makes the number derived rather than carried.
    (1) `crates/eadl-model/src/kind.rs`'s module header: the figure is **gone**; it now states the
    rule (`(holds forms)` is opaque, `(holds kind …)` recurses), names the test that measures the

    the section is retold as a gap that **closed**, with the history in the past tense and the

  - [x] **LOCKSTEP** — `TOOLBOX.md` gains a row for the reach drift gate naming its 5 RED arms;

  ### ROUTING EVIDENCE

  Three findings leave this tree. §15 requires each be logged and owned where it was found; the
  `ROUTING-EVIDENCE` doctrine requires saying what was measured before deciding it belongs elsewhere.

  - **`S0.8`, routed to tree `S0`** — `docs/book/src/s0.md:41` says "Three descriptions in
    `examples/s0-heartbeat/`". Measured independently of the boundary corpus, so it is not the same
    producer wearing a different name: `find examples/s0-heartbeat -name '*.eadl' | wc -l` → `4`, and
    line 306 of the *same chapter* says "The four descriptions survive S0's retirement". The fourth
    arrived at `699a068` (leaf `S0.4`); `git rev-list --count 699a068..HEAD` → `38`, so line 41 has

- ID: `M1.26`
  Status: `active`
  Goal: close the two gaps `M1.12.5`'s closure review measured — both of them §4's diagnostic table
  pinned against the wrong neighbour. Legs 4 and 5 pin the table against the **code that emits** each
  row, in both directions, and that pin is exact. Nothing pins a row against an **input that produces
  it**, and nothing pins the model layer's codes against **any normative document at all**.
  Acceptance: gap (a) is closed by a normative statement of the model layer's diagnostics that a
  mechanism reads and executes, in the shape `M1.12.3` and `M1.12.4` established — a machine-read
  declaration of the sources it governs and a census in both directions — or by a recorded decision
  that those codes are deliberately not normative anywhere, with the reason and the consequence for
  the book's rendered examples; gap (b) is closed by an executable input column on §4's table, so a
  row whose call site can no longer be reached fails, with the notation problem solved rather than
  routed around; RED arms for each, in the `assert_reported` idiom with the violation count pinned;
  `make focused` exit `0`.
  Priority: **medium** — neither gap is a live wrong number. Both are absences of evidence, measured
  rather than suspected, and both are the shape this tree has already been burned by twice (`M1.23`,
  `M1.24`): a claim that was true when it was written and false after the population moved, with
  nothing re-deriving it.
  Verification: `pending`
  Commit: `pending`

  ⚠️ **Neither gap blocks `M1.13`, and `M1.13` does not block either.** The freeze settles the
  *language* — the integer domain and the escape set, findings F-F and F-G. Gap (a) is about the model
  layer's diagnostics, which are not language constructs, and gap (b) is about evidence for rules
  already stated. Recorded so a future session does not sequence them against each other by instinct.

  ⭐ **`M1.13.1` solved gap (b)'s notation problem while closing F-G, and moved gap (b)'s population.**
  The reason `M1.12.3` deferred an executable input column was that no input producing
  `read-unexpected-character` is writable in the reference's own notation — a raw control byte is
  invisible in a table cell, and a raw line feed ends the row. The reference's "Notation for the
  tables" section now carries `<0xNN>`, decoded by `source_text` in
  `crates/eadl-front/tests/common/reference_table.rs` and shared
  by both consumers of the document, and §2 has four rows using it. ⛔ Two consequences for this leaf,
  both of which mean **re-run the census rather than reuse a figure here**: §4's population is now
  **55** rows and not 53, and the two new codes are already pinned by an input, so the "25 named by no
  test" count was true of the population as it stood on `2026-09-28` and is stale the moment this leaf
  is picked up. That is the `M1.23`/`M1.24` lesson applied to this leaf's own recorded evidence.

  ### Gap (a) — the model layer's diagnostics are stated normatively nowhere, and the book renders eight of them

  Measured `2026-09-28` by `M1.12.5`, with two instruments that agree: a `python3` census over every
  production half in `crates/*/src/`, and the shell censuses below.

  ```text
  census: grep -rnoE 'error\[[a-z0-9-]+\]' docs/book/src | sort -u | wc -l          → 20 renderings
  census: grep -rhoE 'error\[[a-z0-9-]+\]' docs/book/src | sort -u | wc -l           → 14 distinct codes
  census: grep -rhoE 'error\[(read|module|schema)-[a-z0-9-]+\]' docs/book/src \
            | sort -u | wc -l                                                        →  6 governed by §4
  census: (the complement)                                                           →  8 not governed
  ```

  The eight: `boundary-implementation-in-description`, `infeasible-configuration`,
  `invalid-description`, `missing-fact`, `quantity-missing-unit`,
  `quantity-non-positive-frequency`, `refinement-violated`, `unsupported-profile`. Their emitters are
  six sources `docs/semantics/reference.md` deliberately does not declare —
  `crates/eadl-model/src/{boundary,check,presence,quantity,refinement,workload}.rs`, which between them
  emit **15** distinct codes:

  ```text
  census: for f in crates/eadl-model/src/{boundary,check,presence,quantity,refinement,workload}.rs; do
            awk '/#\[cfg\(test\)\]/{exit} {print}' "$f"; done | tr '\n' ' ' \
            | grep -oE 'Diagnostic::error\( *"[a-z0-9-]+"' | grep -oE '"[a-z0-9-]+"' \
            | tr -d '"' | sort -u | wc -l                                            → 15
  ```

  ⭐ **Why this was invisible until now.** `M1.12.3` censused the book for code citations and reported
  "eight hits, seven of them real". That census was correct and **incomplete in a way its own shape
  hid**: its pattern was `(read|module|schema)-[a-z]+(-[a-z]+)*`, so it could only ever find the three
  surface prefixes. The other eight renderings were in the population and outside the pattern, and a
  census that finds seven of fourteen reads exactly like a census that found all of them. This is
  `docs/knowledge/a-moved-measurement-needs-a-census-of-its-copies.md`'s question — *is the population
  empty, or is my pattern wrong?* — with a third answer nobody had listed: **the pattern is right and
  the population is bigger than the pattern.**

  What `M1.12.5` did about it, so this leaf inherits a ratchet and not only a complaint: leg 8 of
  `crates/eadl-front/tests/reference.rs` requires every rendered code — governed or not — to be one a
  production source really emits, so a renamed model-layer code fails the build today. What leg 8
  cannot do is ask whether the *rule* behind one is written down anywhere, because no document declares
  itself over those sources. That residue is this gap.

  Options, none of them chosen yet, because the choice is this leaf's work and not its filing:
  1. widen `docs/semantics/reference.md`'s declaration to the model sources and write the chapters —
     which makes the reference normative over *meaning*, against its own §7 rule 5 ("it does **not**
     interpret the constraint vocabulary inside a clause");
  2. a second normative document for the model layer, with its own declaration and its own census, and
     leg 8 widened to read every normative document's declaration rather than one file's;
  3. a recorded decision that these codes are not normative anywhere, with the consequence stated: the
     book's rendered examples are then gated for existence only, forever, and that is accepted.

  ### Gap (b) — a stated code's reachability is pinned by nothing, and the reason recorded for deferring it is false

  `M1.12.3` deferred an executable `fires on` column for §4's table and recorded why: it needs notation
  for a control character, and *"the reachability it would add is already covered per-code by
  `reader.rs`'s own unit tests."* `M1.12.5` measured that second half, and it holds for the table as it
  stood in `M1.12.3` and not for the table `M1.12.4` wrote:

  ```text
  census: (53 codes read out of §4's machine-read table; the test population is every
           crates/*/tests/*.rs plus the half of every crates/*/src/**/*.rs after its first
           `#[cfg(test)]`)
          → 53 stated · 28 asserted by some test · 25 asserted by NO test
          → read-   7/7  asserted      (M1.12.3's claim, true of the codes it could see)
          → module- 11/24 asserted     (13 with no test)
          → schema- 10/22 asserted     (12 with no test)
  ```

  Both instruments agree — the `python3` probe and a shell loop over the same two populations, which is
  the re-derivation leg `docs/CLAIM_VERIFICATION.md` §3 asks for. ⛔ **What this does and does not
  show.** It shows no test *names* those codes. It does not show any of them is unreachable: a test can
  trigger a diagnostic while asserting only `has_errors()`. So this is an absence of evidence, not
  twenty-five defects, and it must not be filed as though it were.

  Why it matters anyway: legs 4 and 5 scan a production half for `Diagnostic::error(`. A call site that
  can no longer be reached still satisfies both legs forever, so §4 would keep stating a rule the
  language no longer enforces — and §4 rule 3 says a code names a *rule*, which is precisely a claim
  about what an input can trigger. `M1.12.5` also measured the one thing that makes the census sound
  rather than lucky: **every `Diagnostic::` constructor in a production half takes a string literal**,
  so no code reaches a diagnostic dynamically and a literal-shaped scan sees the whole population.
  That is worth re-measuring here, because it is the assumption both gaps rest on:

  ```text
  census: grep -rn 'Diagnostic::\(error\|warning\|note\)(' crates/*/src --include='*.rs' \
            | grep -v '"'                                                    → no dynamic code argument
  ```

  The notation problem `M1.12.3` named is real and is this leaf's design work: `read-unexpected-character`
  fires on a control character, and §2's escape set is closed, so no input that triggers it is writable
  in the reference's own notation. A column that cannot express one row is a column that silently omits
  it — the same shape as a header table without its negative cases, which `M1.12.4` refused to write.
  ⛔ **This paragraph is stale and is corrected by the decomposition below rather than left to
  contradict the ⭐ paragraph above it.** `<0xNN>` landed with `M1.13.1`, so an input that triggers
  `read-unexpected-character` *is* writable in the reference's own notation and §2 has four rows using
  it. The notation problem that is still open is a **different** one, measured `2026-09-29` and named
  F-I below: a cell cannot hold a line break, and five of the 24 `module-*` codes can only fire over a
  set of two or more modules.

  ### Measurement — added `2026-09-29`, before any child was written

  ⛔ Re-run rather than reused, because this leaf's own text says so and because both recorded figures
  moved. Instruments: `python3` censuses over the whole population of each claim, plus `grep`/`sed` over
  the call sites. Every command below is re-runnable as written.

  | # | Measured | Command | Result |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | N-A | §4's population has moved **again**, and the figure this leaf carries is stale | `python3` walk of the rows under `<!-- machine-read: diagnostics -->` in `docs/semantics/reference.md` | **60** rows / **60** distinct codes — `read-` 9, `module-` 24, `schema-` 22, `language-version-` 5. ⛔ The leaf says **55**: true when `M1.13.1` measured it, and `M1.13.3` then added five `language-version-*` rows. Second instance of exactly the defect class this leaf exists to close |
  | N-B | the "named by no test" census, re-run over the moved population | the same 60 codes against every `crates/*/tests/**/*.rs` plus the half of every `crates/*/src/**/*.rs` after its first `#[cfg(test)]`, matched as a **quoted literal** | **33** named by some test · **27** named by none — `read-` 2/9 (`read-control-character`, `read-escape-out-of-range`), `module-` 13/24, `schema-` 12/22, `language-version-` **0**/5. The count agrees with the leaf's 27 and the **composition does not**: the two `read-` codes it credited to `M1.13.1` are still named by no test — what `.1` pinned is their *reachability from an input* in §2's table, which is a different property and the one gap (b) is about |
  | N-C | the ungoverned population, workspace-wide rather than over the six sources `M1.12.5` named | `python3` over every production half in `crates/*/src/**/*.rs`, matching `Diagnostic::{error,warning,note}("…")` | **76** distinct codes over **11** emitting files; **60** governed by §4's four declared prefixes and **16** governed by **no** normative document. The 16 are the model layer's 15 (`M1.12.5`'s figure, re-derived and confirmed) plus `analysis-inconclusive` from `crates/archogen-s0/src/interpret.rs`, which that census could not see because it walked six named files rather than the workspace |
  | N-D | the book's rendered population has moved too | `grep -rnoE 'error\[[a-z0-9-]+\]' docs/book/src \| sort -u \| wc -l` and the `sort -u` over codes | **26** renderings / **20** distinct codes / **12** governed / **8** not. `M1.12.5` measured 20/14/6/8, so the growth is entirely on the governed side — six renderings of the `language-version-*` codes `M1.13.3` added. The ungoverned eight are unchanged and are still the eight this leaf names |
  | N-E | a new normative document costs no migration note, and nothing says why | `grep -n TABLE_DOCUMENTS crates/eadl-front/examples/language_freeze.rs` | `const TABLE_DOCUMENTS: &[&str] = &["docs/semantics/reference.md"]`, so the frozen population is scoped by **which file** a machine-read table lives in and not by what it states. §4's diagnostic rows are frozen constructs; an identical table in a sibling document would not be. Filed as F-I |
  | N-F | a §4 row states a mechanism that does not exist in the code | the three censuses under F-H below | ⛔ **`module-too-large`'s row is false**, and it is the one row no writable input can fire. Filed as F-H |

  ### Two findings this measurement turned up, both owned

  - **F-H — §4's `module-too-large` row states a rule no call site implements, and the row is
    unreachable besides.** Owner: `M1.26.2`. A live false normative sentence of the same class
    `M1.13.4.2` closed, and one legs 4/5 cannot see because they pin a code's *existence* and not its
    meaning — which is precisely what gap (b)'s column is for, and why the column found this before it
    existed.

    ```text
    census: grep -rn "module-too-large" --include='*.rs' --include='*.md' . | grep -v '^./target'
            → crates/eadl-front/src/module.rs:605   (the only call site)
            → docs/semantics/reference.md:388       (the only row)
    census: sed -n '602,611p' crates/eadl-front/src/module.rs
            → the site fires when `self.sources.add(display, text)` returns Err, and nothing else
    census: sed -n '191,203p' crates/eadl-front/src/source.rs
            → `add` fails only on `u32::try_from(text.len()).is_err()`, i.e. a source of 2^32 bytes
    census: grep -rn "addressable\|instance identifier" crates/eadl-front/src/module.rs
            → no match, so the row's "more addressable parts than an instance identifier can hold"
              names a limit that exists nowhere in the elaborator
    census: grep -rn "TooLarge\|too large to address" crates/ --include='*.rs'
            → ONE condition, FOUR surfaces: `module-too-large` (module.rs:605), `tool-failure`
              (check.rs:95), and a bare `archogen:` line that is no diagnostic at all
              (build_cmd.rs:113, check_cmd.rs:90)
    ```

    Impact: an author reading §4 is told to "split the module" for a refusal that only a 4 GiB source can
    provoke, and the code's own message ("too large to address") and repair direction are both shaped for
    the false rule. Three ways out — correct the row to the rule the code enforces, implement the rule the
    row states (a language change, so a migration note), or remove the code — and the choice is
    `M1.26.2`'s, recorded rather than assumed.
  - **F-I — the frozen population is scoped by file, and the reason is written down nowhere.** Owner:
    `M1.26.1`, which is the leaf that adds a second normative document and therefore has to answer it.
    `TABLE_DOCUMENTS` names `docs/semantics/reference.md` alone (N-E), so §4's 60 diagnostic rows are
    frozen constructs of `eadl/1` while a sibling document's identical table would not be. ⭐ The
    distinction that makes this defensible rather than arbitrary is real and is the one the child has to
    state: §4's codes are the **language's** refusals — what a description *is* and what it is *worth* —
    while the model layer's codes are the **engine's** semantic verdicts, which grow with every profile
    and knowledge base `ROADMAP.md` §12 admits later. Freezing those at `eadl/1` would make adding a
    profile a language migration. The decision and its reason land on `M1.26.1`'s leaf, in the freeze
    instrument's own header where the next reader looks.

  ### Decomposition — added `2026-09-29`, two children

  Two children rather than one, for the reason `M1.12`'s and `M1.13`'s decompositions gave: the two gaps
  are dimensionally different work and neither is a signoff-quality slice together with the other. Gap
  (a) writes a **new** normative document and widens one leg; gap (b) rewrites a **frozen** table's shape
  and fills 60 executable cells. They share no file except `reference.rs`, and the order between them is
  a preference rather than a dependency.

  | Child | Settles | Why in this order |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | `M1.26.1` | gap (a) — the model layer's 16 ungoverned codes gain a normative home, a declaration and a both-directions census, and leg 8 stops checking the book for existence only | first because it moves no frozen construct (N-E), so it costs no migration note and cannot conflict with `.2`'s baseline re-emit |
  | `M1.26.2` | gap (b) — §4's `fires on` column, the notation that makes a cell executable, the leg that runs every row, F-H's decision, and the migration note the column's movement requires | second because it re-emits `docs/semantics/BASELINE.txt`, and a leaf that both moves a construct and regenerates its baseline cannot show a clean before→after for either |

  ⛔ **Gap (b) is deliberately one child and not one per emitter.** Two splits were priced and both
  produce a defect this tree has already measured: filling the column for some rows and leaving others
  empty is a *silent omission* — `TABLE-ARITY-RATCHET` forbids raising the number of rows whose cell
  count disagrees with their header, so the column lands for all 60 rows in one commit or not at all —
  and landing the notation and leg before the rows that use them is *dead code* in a `tests/common/`
  module that strict lint refuses. A deferral list pinned two-sidedly was the third option and is the
  shape `M1.12.3` was burned by. If `.2` grows past one signoff-quality slice it decomposes on
  measurement, the way `M1.13.4` did, and not before.

- ID: `M1.26.1`
  Status: `done`
  Goal: close gap (a) — give the model layer's diagnostics a normative home, so a code the book renders
  is checked for **meaning** and not only for existence.
  Reproduce / issue: measured `2026-09-29` (N-C, N-D above): **16** of the workspace's **76** distinct
  diagnostic codes are stated by no normative document — the model layer's 15 over
  `crates/eadl-model/src/{boundary,check,presence,quantity,refinement,workload}.rs`, plus
  `analysis-inconclusive` from `crates/archogen-s0/src/interpret.rs` — and the book renders **8** of
  them (`boundary-implementation-in-description`, `infeasible-configuration`, `invalid-description`,
  `missing-fact`, `quantity-missing-unit`, `quantity-non-positive-frequency`, `refinement-violated`,
  `unsupported-profile`). Leg 8 of `crates/eadl-front/tests/reference.rs` can prove each one is a code a
  production source emits, and cannot ask whether the rule behind it is written down anywhere, because
  no document declares itself over those sources. Its own ⚠️ paragraph says so and names this leaf.
  Acceptance: option **2** of the three this leaf filed — a second normative document under
  `docs/semantics/`, with a machine-read declaration of the sources it governs, a machine-read
  diagnostics table stating each code's rule and its §5.5 repair direction, and chapters stating the
  rules — read by **one** reader shared with `reference.rs`, because two parsers for one table shape are
  two things that can disagree about what a rule says; a census leg over that declaration in **both**
  directions, each refusing an empty population rather than passing on it; leg 8 widened to read
  **every** normative document's declaration, with a code stated by two documents a violation (one rule,
  one home); `crates/archogen-s0/src/interpret.rs` explicitly **not** declared, with the reason recorded
  (a prototype carrying an expiry `S0-RETIREMENT` enforces; declaring it normative freezes a temporary
  surface) and the consequence for `analysis-inconclusive` stated rather than left; the F-I decision —
  whether the new document's tables belong in the frozen population — recorded in the freeze
  instrument's own header; RED arms in the `assert_reported` idiom with the violation count pinned, at
  least one per new leg and one proving leg 8's widened **scoping** (a rendered model-layer code the new
  document does not state is reported; one it does state is not); `make focused` exit `0`.
  ⛔ **Where stating a code's rule shows it names more than one rule, that is a finding filed and owned,
  not a silent split.** Measured, three codes have call sites in two or three sources with materially
  different messages — `infeasible-configuration` (`presence.rs:326` a fact required but declared absent
  / `check.rs:330` a refinement target not declared), `missing-fact` (`presence.rs:352` a required fact
  nothing describes / `workload.rs:107` a task with no release model) and `unsupported-profile`
  (`check.rs:277` an exclusion the profile does not admit / `workload.rs:154` a deadline longer than its
  separation / `workload.rs:190` two tasks sharing a priority). §4 rule 3's test — a code names a *rule*,
  not a call site — is the question to answer per code, and changing what the engine **emits** changes
  what the book renders and what an author sees, which is its own leaf and not a side effect of this one.
  Priority: **medium** — no live wrong number. An absence of evidence, measured, of the shape `M1.23`
  and `M1.24` were both burned by.
  ⛔ **Gated on `M1.28`, and the gate is a truth requirement rather than a preference.** This leaf writes
  a normative document whose subject includes the eight `quantity-*` codes, and `archogen check` today
  accepts a description whose quantity is malformed — three discard sites and no quantity notion in the
  schema vocabulary, measured on `M1.28`'s leaf. A normative document stating "a quantity with an
  unknown unit is refused" over a pipeline that accepts it would be *born* false rather than become false
  later, which is the defect class `M1.26` exists to close one level up.
  Verification: see the checklist — `docs/semantics/model.md` with its declaration and diagnostics table; the census
  over it green in both directions; leg 8 reading both documents; nine new tests, two catalogued mutations.
  Commit: `ARCHOGEN-M1-0159 (leaf M1.26.1)`

  ### F-J — `quantity-invalid`'s only call site is an arm no input can reach

  Measured `2026-09-29`, and it is the model layer's own instance of the shape gap (b) was filed for: a
  code an existence census counts as emitted and no description can produce.

  ```text
  census: grep -rn '"quantity-invalid"' crates/ --include='*.rs'   -> one site, quantity.rs:472
  census: sed -n '453,478p' crates/eadl-model/src/quantity.rs
          -> the arm is `other =>` inside `Self::new(value, unit).map_err(…)`
  census: sed -n '306,320p' crates/eadl-model/src/quantity.rs
          -> `Quantity::new` returns Err for exactly two variants, NonPositiveFrequency and
             NegativeDuration, and both have their own arm above the catch-all
  ```

  So the arm is **totality**, not a rule: it exists so that a refusal `Quantity::new` grows later is
  reported rather than dropped, which is the instinct `M1.13.1` applied to `encode_canonical`
  (`docs/knowledge/make-the-rule-a-constructor-precondition.md`). ⚠️ What this leaf must not do is write a
  row that reads like a live rule: the row says it is a totality arm, that no input produces it today,
  and what would — and the document's rules make such a row the only one of its kind, so a second is a
  question rather than a pattern. Whether the arm should instead give way to a narrower error type, so a
  new variant is a **compile** error and the code disappears, is priced rather than assumed: `Quantity::new`
  is `pub` and returns the whole `QuantityError`, so narrowing it is an API change and the catch-all is
  the cheaper honesty.
  ⭐ **The instrument that finds this without reading the code is `M1.26.2`'s column**, which is why that
  leaf's acceptance walks every normative document's diagnostics table and not only §4's.

- ID: `M1.37`
  Status: `done`
  Goal: archogen's reader refuses a string with no delimiter after it, as `docs/semantics/grammar.md` does.
  Reproduce / issue: found `2026-09-30` by `M1.22`'s third opinion, the one document where LinkedSpec's recognizer
  and archogen's reader disagreed (`LS-007`'s evidence, `(a" b"[c]{d})`). The grammar's `atom = ( string | number |
  symbol ) , ? delimiter` requires a delimiter after every atom, and `[` is not one; the grammar-derived recognizer
  in `crates/eadl-front/tests/conformance.rs`, probed:
  ```text
  "(a\" b\"[c]{d})" grammar-accepts=false     "(\"b\"c)" grammar-accepts=false     "(\"b\" c)" grammar-accepts=true
  $ cargo run -q -p eadl-front --example reader_shape <("b"c)>   → ("b" c)   — the reader accepts it as two atoms
  ```
  So the reader is more permissive than the normative grammar it implements: a string followed directly by a symbol
  character is read as two tokens. No conformance probe covers the case, which is why both suites were green.
  Acceptance: the reader refuses it with a code §4 states (a new row is a frozen-table change and needs a migration
  note; an existing code must fit its rule); a conformance probe for it, accepted by neither; the third-opinion
  record re-blessed, `LS-007`'s document moving to `agree`; the book's reading chapter if it states the rule.
  Priority: **medium** — the reader accepts text its own normative grammar refuses; no shipped description relies
  on it (the third opinion found it in one bug report's evidence and nowhere else).
  **Closed `2026-09-30`: the reader enforces the rule its grammar states.** After a string closes, a byte a symbol may
  contain is refused as `read-missing-delimiter`, a new §4 row under a migration note. A byte that can start nothing
  keeps `read-unexpected-character`. The census of every tracked description found the one file the third reader had
  found and nothing else. ⛔ The first cut labelled one byte, and `fuzz_smoke` caught it splitting `é`; the label now
  covers the whole character, and the reader's test pins that.
  Verification: see the checklist — the whole suite, both mutations killed, the third opinion re-blessed to all agree.
  Commit: `ARCHOGEN-M1-0168 (leaf M1.37)`

- ID: `M1.26.2`
  Status: `done`
  Goal: close gap (b) — pin every code §4 states to an **input that produces it**, executed, so a row

  Reproduce / issue: measured `2026-09-29` (N-A, N-B, N-F above): §4 states **60** codes, **27** of them

  before the imports resolve — **re-derive both figures by execution, they are read off call-site order
  here**); **all 60 rows land in one commit** (`TABLE-ARITY-RATCHET`, and a partly filled column is the
  silent omission `M1.12.4` refused to write); one reader in
  `crates/eadl-front/tests/common/reference_table.rs` shared with `conformance.rs`; a leg executing every
  row and requiring the stated code, refusing a cell it cannot parse as a **violation** and never a skip
  (the contract `Value::parse` holds); a stated-limit verdict for the row no writable input can fire,
  two-sided against the column — a row with no input must carry one and an entry must be a row — naming
  the reason and what would make it reachable, where the leg proves the *set* is explicit and the reason
  stays review (`LANGUAGE-FREEZE`'s own honest limit); **F-H decided and landed**, so the row and its
  call site agree afterwards and the choice is recorded; RED arms in the `assert_reported` idiom each
  pinning its violation count, including the one this leaf exists for — **a mutation of a production
  source** that makes a code unreachable, not a mutation of the document; the instrument walks **every**

  sentence on a leaf into a measured row; a migration note in
  `docs/semantics/migrations/` covering the movement, `pending` → `applied`, with
  `scripts/language_baseline.sh --emit` the explicit act it is; `make focused` exit `0`.
  Priority: **medium** — no live wrong figure remains once F-H lands, but 27 rows state a reachability
  nobody measures, and F-H shows the class is not hypothetical.

  `KIND_MODULES`); a kind module has to be run through `read_kind` and `Registry::register` directly. So the cell

- ID: `M1.27`
  Status: `done`
  Goal: make the conformance probe set's coverage claim either **measured** or **retracted** — the test
  is named for a property no assertion in it establishes.
  Reproduce / issue: **one live instance, measured `2026-09-29` by `M1.13.4.4`.**
  `crates/eadl-front/tests/conformance.rs:750` is named
  `the_conformance_probes_exercise_every_production`, and its body asserts one thing: that each probe is
  accepted by both the recognizer derived from `docs/semantics/grammar.md` and the reader. Nothing maps
  a probe to the productions it reaches.

  ```text
  census: grep -cE '^[a-z_]+ *=' docs/semantics/grammar.md                               -> 24 productions
          (every match is inside the EBNF fence: the same grep over lines 52-84 gives 24, and over the
          whole file gives 24, so the fence adds nothing to count and no fence marker has to be quoted)
  census: the probe list in that test, counted from its source                          -> 25 probes
  census: grep -n 'production' crates/eadl-front/tests/conformance.rs
          -> the word appears in the test NAME, in two comments, and in `Grammar::load`'s parsing; no
             assertion mentions a production, so no leg connects the two populations
  ```

  ⛔ The claim was also published, and false twice over: `docs/semantics/grammar.md`'s Conformance table
  said "23 probes, one per production" — a third figure, disagreeing with both counts above. `M1.13.4.4`
  deleted the count rather than retyping it, which is the fifth-shape prescription `M1.12.2` applied to
  this same file, but deleting a figure does not retract the claim underneath it.
  Impact: a reader of the test name takes "every production is exercised" as established, and
  `M1.12.2`'s own comment already concedes the real limit — "one probe per production never combines
  two, and every one of these divergences lived in a combination" — so the intent is a coverage *floor*
  and the name says *proof*. The gap is not hypothetical: `M1.12.2` exists because three literal forms
  disagreed with the reference while this test was green.
  Acceptance: one of the two, chosen on the leaf with the cost of (a) measured first rather than
  assumed —
  (a) a leg that maps every production in the EBNF fence to at least one probe that reaches it, which
  needs the recognizer to report the productions it used and is therefore real work in
  `crates/eadl-front/tests/conformance.rs`'s recognizer; or
  (b) the test renamed to what it asserts, with the coverage claim recorded as a stated limit and the
  population it does cover (every literal row the reference states, which `M1.12.2` added) named as the
  floor instead.
  Either way: a RED arm showing the claim failing when a production loses its probe, or when a probe is
  deleted, and `docs/semantics/grammar.md`'s Conformance section saying what is actually established;
  `make focused` exit `0`.
  Priority: **medium** — no live wrong figure remains (the count is deleted), but a test name that
  overclaims is read as evidence by the next person who wonders whether the grammar is covered.
  **(a) chosen `2026-09-30`, its cost measured first.** The recognizer is continuation-passing and already records
  the atoms of the *accepted* derivation, pushing on completion and popping when the continuation fails
  (`Grammar::matches`, the `atom` arm). Recording productions is the same move for every rule, one trace struct
  threaded where `atoms` is. One production can never be reached positively: `control` appears only as an exclusion
  (`string_char = … ( any - quote - "\\" - control )`), so an accepted derivation never contains it. It is counted
  where the exclusion fires, which is a second, stated kind of reach.
  **Closed `2026-09-30`: the name's claim is measured.** The recognizer reports what a recognition reached. A
  production is *used* when it is in the accepted derivation. An exclusion *fires* when it matches the very text
  its base matched. The probe test maps every production of the fence to the probes that reach it, and fails on
  an unreached one. The map: 24 productions, each with at least one named probe. `control` is reached by one
  malformed probe, added here, and `unicode_escape` by one well-formed probe. Three RED arms and two precision tests
  back it, and five catalogued mutations are killed by the test written for each.
  ⛔ Found on the way, my own: `M1.37`'s two catalogue entries were indented four spaces short. The catalogue strips
  four from every body line, so they matched only as substrings. That holds for one line and fails for two. Both
  are re-indented, and all five entries now run.
  Verification: see the checklist.
  Commit: `ARCHOGEN-M1-0170 (leaf M1.27)`

  ### Acceptance Checklist (enforced by `TASK-ACCEPTANCE`)

  - [x] **REPRODUCE / ISSUE** — the test named for coverage asserted acceptance only:
    ```text
    $ git show HEAD:crates/eadl-front/tests/conformance.rs | grep -n 'production' → the test's name (752), its
      comments, Grammar::load's parsing, and one assertion (571) in another test, that the grammar HAS each
      production; nothing asserts that a probe reaches one
    ```
  - [x] **ROOT CAUSE (WHY + WHERE)** — `Grammar::matches` recorded only atoms of the accepted derivation, so nothing
    could say which productions a probe reached. `git grep -n 'fn segment' HEAD -- crates/eadl-front/tests/conformance.rs`
    → the only thing a recognition returned was its atom spans.
  - [x] **FIX** — `Trace` (atoms, productions, exclusions) replaces the atom cell. Every rule is recorded on completion
    and rolled back when its continuation fails, and a lookahead rolls back what it recorded when what follows fails.
    An exclusion records the productions of the forbidden match when it fires. `Grammar::recognize` returns a
    `Recognition`. The probe lists are hoisted to `PROBES` and `MALFORMED`, and `MALFORMED` gains the raw
    control-character probe. `reach` and `unreached` compute the map; the probe test asserts it and prints it.
    Tests: `a_production_tried_and_abandoned_is_not_reached`, `a_lookahead_that_leads_nowhere_is_not_reached`
    (a synthetic grammar, since the shipped one cannot show it), `arm_6`–`arm_8`. `grammar.md`'s Conformance section
    gains the row and a paragraph, and `reading.md` a sentence.
  - [x] **ADDRESSED (verified)** —
    ```text
    $ cargo test -q -p eadl-front --test conformance the_conformance_probes_exercise_every_production -- --nocapture
      reach: control          raw control character in a string
      reach: sign             signed integers · signed decimal
      reach: unicode_escape   string with a general escape         … 24 `reach:` lines, none empty
    probes=27 malformed=9 (counted from the source's PROBES and MALFORMED)
    $ cargo run -q -p xtask -- mutate --only string-delimiter-unchecked missing-delimiter-label-one-byte \
    ```
