- ID: `M1.28`
  Status: `done`
  Children: `M1.28.1`, `M1.28.2`
  Goal: make `archogen check` **report every quantity it cannot read**, so a description the toolchain
  cannot realize is not *accepted* — and so the two commands that read one description agree about it.
  Reproduce / issue: **a live production defect, measured `2026-09-29` at `c4cc929` on the unmodified
  tree**, while picking up `M1.26.1` and asking which pass surfaces each of the model layer's codes.
  `archogen check` accepts a description holding an unknown unit, and `archogen build` then refuses the
  same bytes — so the command `docs/book/src/checking.md` tells an author to run is the one that is
  wrong, and it is wrong in the unsafe direction.

  ```text
  fixture: examples/s0-heartbeat/system.eadl with `(period 10 ms)` -> `(period 10 parsec)`,
           written to target/tmp/m1261/s0-badunit.eadl (untracked, repository-derived, on-volume)
  $ cargo run -q -p archogen-cli -- check target/tmp/m1261/s0-badunit.eadl
    target/tmp/m1261/s0-badunit.eadl: accepted against profile `rt-static-up-v1` (4 declaration(s))
      this checks the description, not a system: no resolution, generation or analysis has run
  $ cargo run -q -p archogen-cli -- build target/tmp/m1261/s0-badunit.eadl --out target/tmp/m1261/s0b
    error[quantity-unknown-unit]: `parsec` is not a known unit
      --> target/tmp/m1261/s0-badunit.eadl:36:16
    archogen: tool-failure: … was accepted, but the S0 path cannot realize it — nothing was generated
  ```

  The same holds for the quantity `ROADMAP.md` §13.1's **F03** is about. `(tick-rate (exactly 0 MHz))`
  on a `defblock` produces **no diagnostic at all** from `check` — measured on
  `target/tmp/m1261/zero-freq.eadl`, which reports two unrelated `missing-fact` rows and then accepts.
  Impact: three separate surfaces are wrong rather than one.

  1. **F03 is verified at the type and not at the pipeline.** `crates/eadl-model/tests/f03_units.rs`
     drives `Quantity::new` and `Quantity::read` directly, and its own header is honest about the
     property it proves ("refused at **construction**, so no such quantity exists to divide by"). That
     property holds. What nothing tests is whether a *description* holding such a quantity is refused,
     and `grep -rn "parsec" crates/*/tests/*.rs` → one hit, inside `f03_units.rs`, at the type level.
  2. **`docs/book/src/quantities.md` publishes two transcripts no command produces.** Its
     "F03: refusal before arithmetic" section renders `error[quantity-non-positive-frequency]` over
     `--> platform.eadl:3:16` and `error[quantity-missing-unit]`, and `platform.eadl` exists nowhere
     tracked. Both codes are real and both are emitted by a production source, so leg 8 of
     `crates/eadl-front/tests/reference.rs` is green — this is leg 8's stated honest limit ("checked for
     *existence* only") with a live instance in it, and `PROGRAM.20`'s seventh shape (a transcript whose
     input no tracked command writes) with a second one.
  3. **`docs/semantics/cases/invalid-zero-clock-frequency.eadl` passes for the wrong reason.** Its
     header says "A zero tick rate. F03: refused as a constraint error before any arithmetic divides by
     it" and it expects `invalid-description`. It gets that verdict — from
     `error[schema-unknown-clause]: `defblock` declarations have no `refines` clause`, because
     `refines` is a clause of `defplatform` (`docs/semantics/kinds/core.eadl:59`) and the case puts it
     on a `defblock`. The zero frequency is never reached, so the suite's one F03 case exercises a
     clause-name typo. ⛔ Recorded as **F-K** and owned here, not by the suite's own legs: the case
     declares the verdict it expects and gets it, which is everything `conformance_suite.rs` asks.

  Root cause (WHY + WHERE), three discard sites and one absent notion:

  ```text
  census: grep -rn "Quantity::read" crates/ --include='*.rs' | grep -v 'src/quantity.rs' | grep -v tests
          -> crates/eadl-model/src/refinement.rs:177   `… .ok()?`        (discarded)
             crates/eadl-model/src/refinement.rs:188   `… .ok()`         (discarded)
             crates/eadl-model/src/workload.rs:178     `… .map_err(|_| ())`  (discarded)
             crates/archogen-s0/src/interpret.rs:200   `… ?`             (propagated — the only one)
  census: grep -n "holds values" docs/semantics/kinds/*.eadl
          -> `number symbol` for exactly period, min-separation, deadline and jitter, and `symbol` /
             `integer` elsewhere. So the schema checks that a period is a NUMBER THEN A SYMBOL and
             never asks whether the symbol is a unit — `parsec` is a symbol and passes
  census: ValueType::parse, crates/eadl-model/src/kind.rs:156 -> symbol, integer, decimal, number,
          string, any. There is no quantity type, so no pass CAN ask the question at schema level
  census: grep -n "the schema/unit pass" crates/eadl-model/src/workload.rs
          -> :176, a comment naming the pass that would report a malformed quantity. ⛔ It does not
             exist, and the comment is why the discard at :178 reads as safe
  ```

  So the quantity is read by exactly one consumer that surfaces what it finds — the S0 prototype, which
  is the realization `S0-RETIREMENT` exists to delete. When it goes, the last route from a malformed
  quantity to an author goes with it.

  ⭐ **Also measured, and owned here rather than separately: F-M, the refusal that does surface is
  mis-verdicted.** `archogen build` reports the unknown unit and then prints
  `archogen: tool-failure: … was accepted, but the S0 path cannot realize it`. §5.5 is explicit that a
  tool failure is "never reported as a valid system" and is about the toolchain, not the description —
  and `crates/eadl-model/src/check.rs:131`'s own `tool_failure` helper says so in its repair direction.
  An unknown unit is a property of the description, so the verdict is wrong, and it is wrong in the
  direction that tells an author to file a bug rather than fix a symbol.

  Options, to be chosen on the leaf after pricing (a) rather than assuming it:
  - **(a) propagate from the three discard sites.** Small, and ⛔ **incomplete in a way that is worse
    than doing nothing**: it covers only the clauses those two passes happen to read. A
    `(tick-rate 0 MHz)` on a `defblock` that nothing refines is read by nobody, so F03's own subject
    stays unreported — `docs/knowledge/a-fix-that-adds-a-route-does-not-retire-the-old-one.md`.
  - **(b) a `quantity` value type in the kind vocabulary**, so a clause declares
    `(holds values quantity)` and the **schema** pass refuses a pair that is not one. Covers the four
    task clauses by declaration and makes a unit part of *well-formedness* rather than of a later pass,
    which is where §5.2's "rational quantities with units" belongs. Does not reach a quantity inside
    `(holds forms)`, which `docs/semantics/kinds/core.eadl:34` deliberately does not interpret.
  - **(c) (b) plus propagation from `refinement.rs`**, the only reader of a quantity inside
    `(holds forms)`. This is the complete one, and it is the recommendation this leaf starts from.

  ⛔ **(b) and (c) are language changes and cost a migration note.** `ValueType`'s list is what §4's
  `schema-bad-value-type` row states in its repair direction (`docs/semantics/reference.md:411`, a
  frozen construct), and `docs/semantics/kinds/os-rt.eadl` is in the conformance suite, so its
  canonical form is digested in `docs/semantics/BASELINE.txt:76`. Measured before choosing, so the cost
  is not discovered mid-slice: the note names `docs/semantics/reference.md#diagnostics` and
  `suite/docs/semantics/kinds/os-rt.eadl`, and the whole 63-description suite must be re-run because a
  new schema rule can refuse a description that reads today.
  Acceptance: `archogen check` reports a diagnostic for **every** quantity in a description that
  `Quantity::read` refuses, over both populations — the four `number symbol` task clauses and a
  quantity inside `(holds forms)` — with the three fixtures above as the minimum RED set and each
  asserted through `eadl_model::check::check` rather than through the CLI alone; `archogen check` and
  `archogen build` agree on the same bytes, and the verdict for a malformed quantity is
  `invalid-description` and not `tool-failure` (F-M); `docs/semantics/cases/invalid-zero-clock-frequency.eadl`
  exercises a zero frequency and is refused for it (F-K), with the case's own header true afterwards;
  `docs/book/src/quantities.md`'s two transcripts re-rendered **from a tracked command over a tracked
  input** rather than retyped, so the chapter shows what an author can produce;
  `crates/eadl-model/tests/f03_units.rs` gains a leg at the pipeline level beside its type-level ones,
  so F03 is verified where §13.1's gate reads it; a migration note in `docs/semantics/migrations/`
  covering every construct the change moves, `pending` → `applied`, with the baseline re-emitted as the
  explicit act it is; RED arms in the `assert_reported` idiom with the violation count pinned;
  `make focused` exit `0` and `make integration`'s non-emulator steps unchanged.
  Priority: **high** — a live production defect in the safe direction's opposite: the command an author is
  told to trust says *accepted* about a description the toolchain cannot realize, and §13.1's F03 is an M1
  gate fixture. It is sequenced **ahead of `M1.26.1`**, which cannot state the model layer's quantity rules
  normatively while `archogen check` does not enforce them — a normative document that says "refused" over
  a pipeline that accepts is the defect class `M1.26` exists to close, one level up.
  Verification: closed `2026-09-29` by its two children, and the acceptance re-checked criterion by
  criterion against measurement rather than against their claims — the table below.
  Commit: `ARCHOGEN-M1-0102 (leaf M1.28)` (filing) · `ARCHOGEN-M1-0103` (decomposition) · closed by
  `ARCHOGEN-M1-0104 (leaf M1.28.1)` and `ARCHOGEN-M1-0105 (leaf M1.28.2)`

The flip to `applied` cannot be in this commit — leg B reads the working tree, so an `applied` note cannot cover the movement it is landing in — and ⛔ that question is how **F-O** was found: leg B is inert on the real tree, so nothing enforced the note at all. `PROGRAM.27` owns the gate; the flip follows it |

  ⛔ **What closing this leaf does not claim.** The rule "a number immediately followed by a symbol denotes

  ### Decomposition — added `2026-09-29`, two children

  Two children rather than one, because the two halves are independently verifiable and one of them is
  small enough to be wrong about on its own. ⭐ The order is a dependency and not a preference: `M1.28.1`
  changes the **verdict** a refused build reports, and `M1.28.2` re-renders
  `docs/book/src/quantities.md`'s transcripts from real runs — so a transcript rendered before the verdict
  is fixed would be false one commit later, which is the seventh shape `PROGRAM.20` registers.

  | Child | Settles | Why in this order |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | `M1.28.1` | **F-M** — a description defect is verdicted `tool-failure`, a process-level status `crates/archogen-cli/src/status.rs:8` reserves for the invocation | first: it is one default in one place, it moves an exit code (70 → 10), and every transcript `.2` renders is printed through it |
  | `M1.28.2` | the quantity enforcement itself — the `quantity` value type, propagation instead of discard, **F-K**'s case, the book's transcripts and the pipeline-level F03 leg | second: it is a language change with a migration note, and it needs `.1`'s verdict to be the one it publishes |

  ⛔ **F-K cannot be fixed before `.2` and is therefore not its own child.** Rewriting
  `docs/semantics/cases/invalid-zero-clock-frequency.eadl` so it actually declares a zero frequency makes
  the suite **fail** until the pipeline refuses one: the case's header declares `expect:
  invalid-description` and today's pipeline answers `ok`. A case that is correct and a suite that is red is
  a worse state than a case that passes for the wrong reason, because the red is indistinguishable from a
  regression. So the case and the enforcement land together.

- ID: `M1.28.1`
  Status: `done`
  Goal: close **F-M** — a diagnostic whose code is not a §5.5 verdict slug gets the verdict the pipeline
  already uses for one, in **one** place, so `archogen build` stops reporting a description defect as a
  toolchain failure.
  Reproduce / issue: measured `2026-09-29` at `c4cc929`.

  ```text
  $ cargo run -q -p archogen-cli -- build target/tmp/m1261/s0-badunit.eadl --out …
    error[quantity-unknown-unit]: `parsec` is not a known unit
    archogen: tool-failure: … was accepted, but the S0 path cannot realize it — nothing was generated
  $ … ; echo $?
    70
  ```

  `quantity-unknown-unit` is a property of the description. `crates/archogen-cli/src/status.rs:8` is
  explicit that `ToolFailure` is a **process-level** status describing the invocation, and
  `crates/eadl-model/src/check.rs:131`'s own `tool_failure` helper says in its repair direction that "this
  is a failure of the toolchain, not a verdict about the description". So the refusal is right and its
  classification is wrong, and it is wrong in the direction that tells an author to file a bug rather than
  fix a symbol.
  Root cause: `crates/archogen-cli/src/build_cmd.rs:136-137` reads
  `Verdict::parse(diagnostic.code).map_or(Status::ToolFailure, Status::from_verdict)` — a **third**
  consumer of the "which verdict does this code carry?" rule, and the only one whose default is
  `ToolFailure`. `check.rs:212` and `:223` both use `unwrap_or(Verdict::InvalidDescription)`. That is
  `M1.13.4.1`'s lesson exactly: "a rule each consumer re-implements is a rule the next consumer lacks".
  Acceptance: one accessor carries the rule and all three consumers call it, so a fourth cannot pick its
  own default; `archogen build` over the fixture above reports `invalid-description` and exits **10**;
  `docs/book/src/cli.md`'s exit-code table agrees with `Status::ALL` afterwards (it is the contract
  scripts branch on); a RED arm showing the old default reproduced — a diagnostic whose code is not a
  verdict slug classified as `ToolFailure` — and an arm proving the accessor total over `Verdict::ALL`;
  `make focused` exit `0`.
  ⚠️ **An exit code moves, and that is a contract change rather than a bug fix in isolation.** Measured
  before choosing: 70 → 10 for a build that fails inside `interpret`. No tracked script branches on 70 for
  this case (`grep -rn '70' scripts/ xtask/` to be re-run on the leaf), and §5.5's own rule — a tool
  failure is never reported as a valid system — is what makes 10 the correct code.
  Priority: **high** — a live wrong verdict on the CLI's stable contract, and the smallest of the two
  halves of `M1.28`.
  Verification: closed `2026-09-29` — the checklist below and the verification log.
  Commit: `ARCHOGEN-M1-0104 (leaf M1.28.1)`

  ✅ **Closed `2026-09-29`.** One accessor, `Verdict::of_code`, carries the rule and all three consumers
  call it, so a fourth cannot pick its own default. The exit code moved **70 → 10** on the fixture this
  leaf was filed on, measured both ways through the published CLI. **545 passed / 0 failed** over 39
  suites, from 541 — four new arms, three unit and one end to end, and four mutations seen firing.

  ⛔ **What closing this leaf does not claim.** It fixes the *classification*, not the reachability:
  `archogen check` still accepts `(period 10 parsec)`, because three consumers of `Quantity::read` discard
  what it finds. That is `M1.28.2`, and until it lands the two commands still disagree about the same
  bytes — the build's own message now says so out loud ("though the check pipeline accepted it") rather
  than leaving the author to discover it.

  ### Lesson promotion

  `a rule each consumer re-implements is a rule the next consumer lacks` →
  `promotion: declined (second instance of the shape M1.13.4.1 closed, and the prescription is identical
  both times — extract the rule into one named accessor. The first instance is recorded on that leaf and
  the second is now cited in the accessor's own doc comment, which is where the next consumer reads it, so
  the lesson is retrievable at the point of use and not only by question. This repository promotes at
  recurrence — docs/knowledge/a-leafs-claims-about-the-repository-are-hypotheses.md was declined at one
  instance and promoted at four — and a card written at two, whose prescription is already a named
  function, would answer a question the function answers better.)`

  ✅ **Closed `2026-09-29`.** `archogen check` and `archogen build` now agree on the same bytes — both
  `invalid-description`, both exit **10** — and a census over all **76** tracked descriptions finds

  ### Three findings this leaf's own work turned up, all owned

  - **F-N — a book claim and an accepted corpus case contradicted each other, and the discard hid it.**
    `docs/book/src/quantities.md` opened "A number in an eADL description **always** carries a unit" and
    rendered `error[quantity-missing-unit]` as the refusal, while
    `docs/semantics/boundary/accept/counter-width-and-rate.eadl` — verdict `accept`, one of the ten F27
    reads — holds `(counter-modulus 4294967296)`. Both were "true" only because every pass that read a
    quantity threw the answer away. Surfacing the refusal forced the rule to be stated, and the rule is a
    **shape**: `<number> <symbol>` is a quantity, a lone number is a count. Corrected here, in the chapter
    and in the code, with the guard pinned by two legs.
  - ⛔ **F-O — `LANGUAGE-FREEZE`'s second leg is inert on the real tree, and this leaf is the first to run
    the workflow that would have found out.** Reproduced, not inferred:
    ```text
    $ mv docs/semantics/migrations/eadl-1-quantity-value-type.md target/tmp/   # NO note at all
    $ scripts/check_language_freeze.sh
    language-freeze: OK (72 frozen construct(s) agree with the working tree)     # rc=0, a FALSE GREEN
    $ grep -rl '^- status:[[:space:]]*pending' docs/semantics/migrations
    docs/semantics/migrations/README.md
    $ grep -n '^- constructs:' docs/semantics/migrations/README.md
    36:- constructs: <construct id>, <construct id>   — or: all
    ```
    Two holes that close only together: the population grep matches the README's own **form template**
    (`- status: pending | applied`), so the README is permanently a pending note; and `names_construct`'s
    `case "$line" in *all*) return 0 ;;` matches the template's trailing `— or: all`, so that note covers
    **every** construct. Leg B is the one `M1.13.5`'s own leaf called "the one that matters" — the leg that
    stops `--emit` being the waiver — and it has never been able to fail outside its own scratch
    directory, because all nine arms run against `$work/notes` and not against `docs/semantics/migrations`.
    `docs/knowledge/a-gate-is-only-as-sharp-as-its-fixtures.md` one level up: the arms proved the mechanism
    and not the deployed population. **Owner: `PROGRAM.27`**, filed this commit, priority **high**.
  - ⭐ **`f27_boundary.rs` had no leg that ran the pipeline.** Every leg in it asked the *classifier* or the
    *schema*, and neither reads a fact's value — so the false positive F-N's guard prevents was invisible
    to the whole suite and was found by a hand-run census over all 76 descriptions. The leg
    `f27_every_accepted_case_is_accepted_by_the_whole_pipeline` now exists, and mutation **F** below is the
    measurement that it is load-bearing rather than decorative.

  ### What this leaf hands to the next two

  - **`M1.26.1`** inherits a rule that needs a normative home: *a number immediately followed by a symbol
    denotes a quantity, so the symbol must be a unit the table holds; a lone number is a count.* It is
    enforced by `crates/eadl-model/src/refinement.rs` and stated in two chapters and two comments, and in
    no normative document — which is precisely gap (a).
  - **`M1.26.2`** inherits a moved reachability: `quantity-missing-unit` was unreachable from
    `archogen check` before this leaf and is reachable now (a bound written `(exactly 10)`), and
    `docs/semantics/cases/invalid-quantity-without-unit.eadl` is the tracked input that does it. The
    `fires on` column's instrument walks every normative document's table, so the model document's rows
    get the same treatment §4's do.

- ID: `M1.29`
  Status: `active` — **decomposed `2026-09-29` into four children on measurement**, because the ⛔ clause
  below fired: wiring the loader is not one slice. `M1.29.1` corrects the claims first; `.2` builds the
  disk-backed loader and makes §6's codes reachable from a command; `.3` states and implements the name
  rule an elaborated program needs before any later pass can read it; `.4` gives module parameters a
  consumer. The parent closes when all four are `done`.
  Goal: make the claim "`archogen check` **elaborates** a description" either true or untrue in every
  surface that makes it — and, since `ROADMAP.md` §10.1 step 1 requires elaboration as the pipeline's
  *first* step, that means making it true.
  Reproduce / issue: **measured `2026-09-29` at `1a43973`, answering the director's question "how do we know
  a `.eadl` has everything described to build a functional OS?"** Nothing in the toolchain elaborates a
  module tree, and three surfaces say otherwise.

  ```text
  census: git grep -n "elaborate(" -- crates | grep -v "src/module.rs"
          -> crates/eadl-front/tests/f01_f02_modules.rs:16 and :390 ONLY. No production caller.
  census: git grep -n "impl ModuleSource" -- crates
          -> crates/eadl-front/src/module.rs:160, `MemoryModules` — an in-memory map that exists for tests.
             There is NO disk-backed module loader anywhere in the workspace.
  census: git grep -n "defmodule" -- docs/semantics/kinds
          -> no match, so `defmodule` is not a registered kind and `check` cannot validate one
  $ archogen check <the (defmodule hw.timer (version 1 0) (param …) (export …) (defblock …)) shape
                    docs/book/src/modules.md opens with>
    error[schema-unknown-kind]: `defmodule` is not a known kind
  $ archogen check <a defsystem holding (import platform.timer (as timer))>
    error[schema-unknown-clause]: `defsystem` declarations have no `import` clause
  $ archogen help check
    archogen check — elaborate and type-check a description against a profile
  ```

  The three false claims are one sentence copied: `crates/archogen-cli/src/spec.rs:151`
  (`summary: "elaborate and type-check a description against a profile"`, which is what `archogen help`
  prints), `docs/book/src/cli.md:17` and `:30`, and by framing `docs/book/src/modules.md` — which opens "A
  description is not one file", shows a `(defmodule …)` with two imports, and cites
  `docs/semantics/reference.md` §6 as normative without saying that no command runs it. All 24 of §6's
  `module-*` rows are stated and all are emitted, so legs 4 and 5 are green, and **not one of those codes is
  reachable from any command**.
  Impact: `ROADMAP.md` §10.1 step 1 — "Parse and expand the locked eADL modules with bounded deterministic
  expansion" — is not wired, so every later step that depends on elaboration order (`Program::instances` is
  children-before-parents, and §10.1 steps 5 and 6 rely on it) has no input from a real invocation. F01 and
  F02 are green **at library level only**. And `docs/book/src/modules.md` teaches the director a capability
  the binary does not have, which is the drift `BOOK-ANCHORS` exists to catch and cannot see: every path that
  chapter cites resolves.
  Acceptance: a disk-backed `ModuleSource` — a module path, resolved by **declared name** and not by
  filename, with the naming rule stated rather than inferred — wired into `archogen check` ahead of the
  schema pass, so a description that imports is elaborated and a `defmodule` file is read as a module;
  `archogen check` and `archogen build` agree about a module tree the way `M1.28` made them agree about a
  quantity; F01 and F02 driven through `archogen` rather than only through `elaborate()`, so the fixtures
  §13.1 names are verified where the gate reads them; every one of §6's 24 codes reachable from a command,
  or the ones that are not recorded as a stated limit with the reason; `docs/book/src/modules.md` saying what
  a reader can actually run; `spec.rs`'s summary and `cli.md` true afterwards; RED arms with the violation
  count pinned, including one proving that a module tree no command reads is reported rather than skipped;
  `make focused` exit `0`. ⛔ If wiring the loader grows past one signoff-quality slice, the **claims** are
  corrected first and the loader becomes a child, because a false sentence on the CLI's own contract is a
  live defect while an unbuilt loader is a scheduled one.
  ⚠️ **Interaction recorded so `M1.26.2` does not trip on it:** §4's `fires on` column can be filled for the
  24 `module-*` rows today by calling `elaborate()` from the leg, which is legitimate — the library is the
  unit under test. But the column then pins reachability *from a library call* and not from a command, and it
  has to say so, because `M1.29` is exactly that difference.
  Priority: **high** — three surfaces claim a pipeline step that does not run, one of them is the CLI's own
  help text, and the roadmap puts that step first.
  Why decomposed — **measured, not estimated**, at `3a35708`:

  ```text
  $ archogen check <modules.md's opening (defmodule app.system …) example> ; echo $?
    error[schema-unknown-kind]: `defmodule` is not a known kind
    error[missing-fact]: `clock.time.monotonic` is required by this system and nothing describes it
    archogen: invalid-description: 2 diagnostic(s)                                   exit=10
  $ archogen build <the same bytes> --out … ; echo $?                                 exit=10, same two
  census: git grep -n "bindings" -- crates | grep -v "src/module.rs"
          -> crates/eadl-front/tests/f01_f02_modules.rs:146, :148, :162, :163 ONLY. Module parameters are
             bound and never substituted into a declaration: no production consumer.
  census: git grep -nE "\.declared|requested|needs" -- crates/eadl-model/src/presence.rs
          -> presence keys `declared`, `uses` and `needs` by the BARE name written in the source
  ```

  So the book's own opening example is told it is an invalid description twice over, and the second
  diagnostic is not even about the first: the imports were never resolved, so the presence pass looked for
  `clock.time.monotonic` in a file that only imports it. Wiring the loader is therefore three problems and
  not one. **(i)** There is no disk-backed `ModuleSource`. **(ii)** An elaborated program cannot be handed
  to the model passes as it stands: `Program::qualified_declarations` renames a declaration to its alias
  path, but a *reference* inside a child instance — `(uses x)` naming its own sibling — is written relative
  to that instance, and §6 states no rule for resolving it or for what an `export` hides. Its only sentence
  on the subject is rule 1's "addressed by the dotted path of aliases from the root". Running the passes
  over renamed declarations without such a rule would refuse a valid composition or accept an invalid one,
  depending on which names collide. **(iii)** Parameters have no consumer, which is §10.1 step 1's
  "expand" and §5.1's "all semantically meaningful fields need a consumer". Each is a slice, and (ii) is a
  language rule that has to be written into the reference before it is implemented.
  Children: `M1.29.1`, `M1.29.2`, `M1.29.3`, `M1.29.4`.
  Verification: see the children.
  Commit: see the children.

  description is a well-formed module and it is the tool that cannot read it. The honest answer is a

  (a command that exists, asked for a capability it does not have), naming the leaf that removes it.
  Acceptance: `crates/archogen-cli/src/spec.rs`'s `check` summary, `check_cmd.rs`'s module comment and

  because a syntax error is a verdict about the bytes whatever they were meant to be; a leg couples the
  summary to the capability, so restoring "elaborate" without the capability fails; a leg ties the refusal
  to its owner, so the refusal cannot outlive `M1.29.2` being closed; RED arms seen firing; `make focused`
  exit `0`.
  Priority: **high** — it is `M1.29`'s live defect.

- ID: `M1.29.2`
  Status: `done`
  Design, decided `2026-09-29` before any code and written into `docs/semantics/reference.md` §6 first:
  **(a)** an import of `a.b` is read from `a.b.eadl` in the **module path**, which is the directory holding
  the description the command was given — one directory, so no two candidates can compete and no search
  order has to be invented; a `--module-path` is deferred until a real layout needs one. **(b)** The file is
  found by the imported name and **verified** by its declared one — rule 4 stays firable, which is the
  reading of `M1.29`'s "resolved by declared name" that keeps `module-name-mismatch` a live rule (the ⛔
  constraint below). **(c)** A module name is dotted lowercase segments, because it becomes a file name: no
  separator may escape the module path and no two spellings may name one file on a case-insensitive
  filesystem and two on another; anything else is `module-bad-import`. **(d)** A module file that exists but
  cannot be read is a failure of the **invocation**, reported like an unreadable description (`usage`, exit
  2), not `module-not-found` — which would be a false statement about the description. **(e)** The root is
  the file the command was given and is never looked up, so its declared name is not compared with its file
  name. **(f)** After elaboration the command stops: diagnostics are `invalid-description`; a clean tree is
  `unimplemented` naming `M1.29.3`, because the model passes cannot read an elaborated program until the name
  rule exists. **(g)** Fixtures: one directory per case under a new conformance root
  `docs/semantics/modules/`, the root file carrying `case:` / `expect:` / `code:` / `why:`, imported files
  carrying no `expect:`.
  Goal: a disk-backed `ModuleSource` wired into `archogen check` and `archogen build` ahead of the schema
  pass, through the routing function `M1.29.1` lands — so a module file is **elaborated**, and every §6 code
  a module tree can produce is produced by a command.
  Acceptance: the naming rule — how an import's module name becomes a file, and where the module path
  comes from — stated in `docs/semantics/reference.md` §6 **before** it is implemented, with the reason;
  both commands elaborate through one function so they cannot disagree about a module tree; each of §6's
  24 `module-*` codes reached through `archogen` from a tracked fixture, or recorded as a stated limit with
  the reason (`module-too-large` fires only on a source of 2^32 bytes, which is **F-H**'s row); an
  elaborated tree that has no name rule yet to be type-checked with is refused as `unimplemented` naming
  `M1.29.3`, never checked with unresolved names; `docs/book/src/modules.md` shows transcripts rendered by
  the command; RED arms; `make focused` exit `0`.
  ⛔ **Design constraint found while decomposing, recorded so `.2` does not rediscover it.** `M1.29`'s
  acceptance asks for a module path "resolved by **declared name** and not by filename". Read literally —
  index every file in the module path by the name its `defmodule` declares — that makes §6 rule 4's
  "a module whose declared name is not the name it was imported by is refused" **unfirable**, and
  `module-name-mismatch` a row that reads like a live rule and can never fire, which is exactly what §4
  rule 4 calls a rotted row. Rule 4 presupposes that the name an import looks up and the name a file
  declares are two things that can disagree. So `.2` resolves the file by a **stated** rule from the
  imported name and keeps the declared name **authoritative** — a file that declares another name is
  refused, never trusted — and records that reading of the acceptance on the leaf.
  ⛔ **Second constraint: a tracked module fixture is a manifest decision.** `docs/semantics/conformance.md`
  rule 4 puts every tracked `.eadl` in the repository into the `eadl/1` conformance suite or requires it to
  be excluded, and every suite member's canonical form is a frozen construct in `BASELINE.txt`. So the
  fixtures `.2` needs are declared under a root whose purpose is stated in the manifest's `suite-roots`
  table, each carrying the verdict header the suite reads, and the baseline movement is covered by a
  migration note — which is why `M1.29.1` writes its one module file into `CARGO_TARGET_TMPDIR` instead.
  Priority: **high** — `M1.29`'s capability half.
  Verification: see the checklist — 26 cases through both commands, each producing exactly its one code;
  23 of §6's 24 codes reached from a command and the 24th stated; eight mutations seen firing.
  Commit: `ARCHOGEN-M1-0112 (leaf M1.29.2)`

  ⛔ **Two defects in the elaborator, invisible until a command could reach them, found by the first run of
  the fixture census and fixed here.** (1) `(version one zero)` produced **two** diagnostics, the second
  `module-missing-version: … declares no version` about a module that declares one. (2) `module-empty`
  labelled `Span::new(SourceId(0), 0, 0)` — and in a command source 0 is the first shipped kind module, so
  the author of an empty `hw.nothing.eadl` was sent to `docs/semantics/kinds/core.eadl:1:1`. Both sat behind
  library tests that asserted the code and never the second message or the location.

- ID: `M1.29.3`
  Status: `done`
  Design, decided `2026-09-29` and written into `docs/semantics/reference.md` §6 before any code:
  **(a)** rule 9 — an elaborated declaration is named by its instance path, `platform.timer.timer.counter`,
  which is the name `Program::qualified_declarations` already computes and `modules.md` already shows.
  **(b)** rule 10 — the operands of `uses`, `needs` and `refines` (the clauses `presence.rs` reads as names,
  and the only ones) resolve in the writing instance's scope: its own declaration first; then `alias.n`
  through one of its imports, where `n` must be **exported** by that import's module, else the new code
  `module-not-exported`; else the name is left as written, because it is a capability-vocabulary name and
  not a declaration's. An alias never shadows a local declaration. There is no re-export, so an importer
  sees exactly the exports of the modules it imports directly — `app.system` cannot reach
  `platform.timer.…` unless `hw.soc` declares and exports something that stands for it. **(c)** Layering:
  the frontend owns the scope (`Program::resolve`, and the new code's literal, so §4's census still finds
  every `module-` code in `module.rs`); the model layer owns *which positions are names* and applies it
  (`eadl_model::check::check_program`), because the kind schema holds those clauses as plain forms. **(d)**
  The passes then run over the resolved declarations unchanged — `check` and `check_program` share one
  pass sequence, so a module tree is judged by exactly the rules a single description is.
  Goal: the **name rule** an elaborated program needs — how a reference written inside an instance resolves,
  and what an `export` hides from an importer — written into `docs/semantics/reference.md` §6, then
  implemented, so an elaborated program can be handed to the model passes; and with it, `archogen check`
  elaborating **and** type-checking a module tree end to end.
  Acceptance: the rule stated with its reason before any code (§6 today says only that what an import
  brings in is "addressed by the dotted path of aliases from the root"); the model passes run over the
  resolved program; F01 and F02 driven through `archogen` from tracked fixtures, so the fixtures §13.1 names
  are verified where the gate reads them; `archogen check` and `archogen build` agree about a module tree;
  the `check` summary says "elaborate" again, under `M1.29.1`'s coupling leg; RED arms with the violation
  count pinned — including a non-exported name refused and a child's reference to its own sibling accepted;
  `make focused` exit `0`.
  Priority: **high**.
  Verification: see the checklist — every module case through both commands, the two F01 trees accepted,
  three rule-10 cases each discriminating, eight mutations seen firing.
  Commit: `ARCHOGEN-M1-0114 (leaf M1.29.3)`

  ⛔ **A hole in rule 9 found while writing its legs, and a pre-existing defect under it — filed as `M1.33`,
  not patched here.** A module that declares `inner.x` and imports a module as `inner` that declares `x`
  gives two declarations the one name `p.inner.x`. Checking whether anything catches duplicates at all: a
  **single** description with two `(defblock timer.counter …)` offering 32 and 16 bits is **accepted**, and
  `presence.rs`'s `or_insert` keeps the first — the silent choice §5.3 forbids. That is one missing rule,
  older than modules, so it is its own leaf, and §6 states the limit openly instead of letting rule 9 imply
  uniqueness.

- ID: `M1.29.4`
  Status: `blocked` — on the director's ruling, `decision_findings-for-director-review.md` §7
  Goal: module parameters get a consumer — §10.1 step 1's "expand" — or the reason they have none is stated.
  Reproduce / issue: `Instance::bindings` is computed by `resolve_bindings` and read only by
  `crates/eadl-front/tests/f01_f02_modules.rs` (census in `M1.29`). A `(param tick-rate (default …))` is
  bound, a missing one is refused, an unknown one is refused — and the value is then used by nothing, so an
  import's `(with (tick-rate …))` changes no declaration. §5.1: "All semantically meaningful fields need a
  consumer."
  Acceptance: the substitution rule (where a parameter may be referenced, and what it expands to) stated in
  §6 before it is implemented; an instantiated twice module whose two instances differ **in a declaration**
  and not only in `bindings`; RED arms; `make focused` exit `0`.
  Priority: **medium** — no surface claims substitution happens, but `modules.md`'s two-timers example
  implies it.
  ⛔ **Measured `2026-09-29` at `ef511ba`: a parameter's value has three shapes, so no consumer could use it
  as it stands.** A throwaway probe elaborated one `hw.timer` three ways and printed the binding's canonical
  form (the probe was deleted after the run):
  ```text
  PROBE default   -> tick-rate bound to (default (tick-rate 10 MHz))   <- the `default` keyword kept
  PROBE one form  -> tick-rate bound to (tick-rate 100 MHz)            <- the value, as written
  PROBE two forms -> tick-rate bound to (tick-rate 20 MHz)             <- the parameter's own NAME kept as head
  ```
  WHERE: `crates/eadl-front/src/module.rs`'s `read_module` stores `default` as the whole `(default …)` form,
  and `read_import` keeps a multi-form `with` binding whole "so `(tick-rate 10 MHz)` binds the quantity" —
  which also binds the name. `f01_a_parameter_default_applies_when_no_argument_is_given` asserts the binding's
  *name* and count, never its value, which is why this is invisible.
  ⭐ **The rest is a surface decision on a frozen language, so it is proposed here and surfaced to the
  director rather than chosen in a commit.** `ROADMAP.md` §5.1.1 asks for **typed** parameters and §10.1
  step 1 for "bounded deterministic expansion"; nothing records a design. The proposal:
  **(1) one value model** — a parameter binds the *sequence of forms* after its name (`(with (rate 20 MHz))`
  binds `20 MHz`) or after `default`, whichever supplied it; **(2) a type** — `(param rate (holds quantity)
  (default 10 MHz))`, reusing the schema's value types (§7 rule 4) so there is one vocabulary of shapes, with
  a mismatched argument refused under a new `module-` code; **(3) an explicit reference** —
  `(param-value rate)` inside a declaration, expanded by splicing the bound forms in its place, and allowed
  only in clause operands (never in a declaration's name or a clause's head). Rejected alternatives, with
  reasons: a **bare-name** reference (`rate` anywhere) captures vocabulary words a module did not mean to
  parameterize, and cannot be found by `grep`; a **sigil** (`$rate`) is a character-level convention the
  reader would have to treat specially inside an otherwise uniform symbol grammar. Expansion is bounded by
  construction: a value may not contain a reference, so nothing expands twice.
  Status of this leaf: **waiting on that call** — the value-model fix (1) is a defect and could land alone,
  but its representation depends on (3), so the two land together.
  Verification: `pending`
  Commit: `pending`

- ID: `M1.30`
  Status: `done`
  Goal: deliver §5.3's second half — "Unknown facts outside [the dependency closure] remain **visible in
  metadata** and do not fail generation" — or record that it is deliberately not delivered and say what an
  author loses by it.
  Reproduce / issue: **measured `2026-09-29` at `1a43973`.** The analysis computes it and the pipeline drops
  it.

  ```text
  census: git grep -n "outside_closure" -- crates | grep -v tests
          -> crates/eadl-model/src/presence.rs:106 (the field) and :366/:374 (where it is populated).
             NO production consumer.
  census: git grep -nE "\.closure|outside_closure|\.missing" -- crates/eadl-model/src/check.rs
             crates/archogen-cli/src crates/archogen-s0/src
          -> no match: check.rs:230 consumes `presence.diagnostics` and drops the other three fields
  census: git grep -n "archogen_evidence" -- crates/archogen-cli/src
          -> no match, and `archogen-evidence` is a dependency of `rt-analysis` only, so there is no
             metadata channel from the presence report to a user either
  census: git grep -n "Severity::Warning" -- crates | grep -v tests
          -> crates/eadl-front/src/diagnostic.rs:524, inside the `#[cfg(test)]` half. No production half
             emits a warning or a note, so §4 rule 1's "there is no warning and no note anywhere" holds
  ```

  Impact: F05's requirement is only half delivered. "An irrelevant capability left undescribed does not fail
  generation" is enforced and tested (`crates/eadl-model/tests/f04_f06_presence.rs`); "remains visible in
  metadata" is computed, asserted by that same test, and shown to nobody — so an author cannot see what their
  description leaves undescribed, which is precisely the information that tells them whether the closure the
  engine computed is the one they meant.
  ⛔ **And the category has nowhere to live, which is a design question and not an oversight.** §4 rule 1 of
  `docs/semantics/reference.md` states that every diagnostic is an error and that there is no warning and no
  note anywhere in the toolchain, with its reason recorded ("a severity that said 'probably fine' would be a
  lie") — while §5.3 requires a fact that is *not* an error to stay visible. Either it goes into report
  metadata through the `archogen-evidence` vocabulary, which is what §7.3's evidence categories are for and
  what the field's name suggests, or §4 rule 1 changes and a warning severity exists — a language change, so
  a migration note. The choice belongs on this leaf with both costs measured, and `M1.26.1`'s normative
  document has to state whichever is chosen.
  Acceptance: the closure boundary an author is told about — what is inside it, what is outside it and
  undescribed, and which request pulled each fact in — reaches a user through a **named** channel, with the
  channel chosen on the leaf and the reason recorded; a leg that fails when the report stops carrying it, so
  a computed-and-dropped field cannot silently return; `docs/book/src/presence.md` saying what the reader
  will see; §4 rule 1 either unchanged and consistent with the choice or amended through a migration note;
  RED arms with the violation count pinned; `make focused` exit `0`.
  Priority: **medium** — no live false claim, because nothing says the metadata is shown, but a roadmap
  requirement measured as undelivered and a second instance of the shape `M1.28` was: a computation whose
  result no consumer surfaces.
  **Channel chosen: the `archogen check` report, as metadata after the verdict** — not a warning severity. §4 rule 1's
  reason ("a severity that said 'probably fine' would be a lie") stands, and the fact outside the closure is not
  "probably fine" either: it is *not needed*, which is information, not a judgement. A warning would have been a
  language change (a migration note) to carry something that fails nothing; the report carries it for free, and
  `docs/semantics/model.md` §2 rule 4 states the channel. The `archogen-evidence` vocabulary was the other
  candidate and is for evidence about a *system*; this is a fact about a *description*, printed where the
  description's verdict is.
  Verification: see the checklist — the boundary in `Outcome`, printed on acceptance; a leg over every accepted
  description the repository records; F05's fixture shown to the author; a catalogued drop-the-report mutation.
  Commit: `ARCHOGEN-M1-0163 (leaf M1.30)`

  ### Acceptance Checklist (enforced by `TASK-ACCEPTANCE`)

- ID: `M1.31`
  Status: `done`
  Goal: a caret never runs past the line it is drawn under — a label whose span crosses a line boundary is
  rendered so that it still points at the text it names.
  Reproduce / issue: **found `2026-09-29` by `M1.29.1`'s reproduction, not looked for.** `archogen check` on
  `modules.md`'s opening example drew **236** carets under the 21-character line `(defmodule app.system`,
  because the primary label spans the whole seven-line form. Widened into a census over every tracked
  description, pairing each rendered source line with the marker line beneath it:

  ```text
  census (corrected — see the ⛔ below): for f in $(git ls-files '*.eadl'); do archogen check "$f"; done,
          and for each rendered label measure INDENT + MARKER RUN against the width of the line above it
          (a single caret at column len+1 is legitimate: it points just past the last character)
          -> 14 marker runs over 10 of 78 tracked descriptions: 8 of the 13 boundary `reject/` cases
             (register-programming-sequence.eadl: 183 carets under a 17-character line) and the two kind
             modules (one run under os-rt.eadl reaches column 2 474 under a 13-character line)
  census: the same pairing over docs/book/src/*.md -> 0: no chapter shows one, so it is live on the CLI only
  ```

  ⛔ **The figure filed with this leaf in `ARCHOGEN-M1-0108` was wrong, and the instrument was why.** It
  read "**23** of 78 — all 13 boundary rejects, 3 semantic cases, 7 LinkedSpec evidence files" and "264
  carets", and it was wrong **in both directions, for two independent reasons**, each pinned with a tool:

  ```text
  (1) OVER-count: the first instrument measured the WHOLE marker line, label text included, so a long label
      under a short line counted as an overrun. Run to completion on the pre-fix binary it reports 38 files
      (7 LinkedSpec, 13 boundary, 14 cases, 2 kinds, 2 examples) — and 38 again AFTER the fix, which is how
      it was caught: a census that does not move when the defect is fixed is not measuring the defect.
  (2) UNDER-count: the saved list was exactly 2048 bytes. It was written by `… | tee census.txt | head -20`;
      `head` exited after 20 lines, SIGPIPE stopped `tee`, and the file — and the "23" counted from it —
      held only the alphabetical head of the list. Both kind modules, the largest overruns there are, were
      in the part that was cut.
  ```

  The corrected instrument measures only the indent and the run, writes straight to a file with nothing
  downstream of it, and was re-run on the *before* state from
  `git stash push -- crates/eadl-front/src/diagnostic.rs`, not assumed. Every copy of the old
  figure — this leaf, the frontier, the `M1.29.1` commit-log row, `CHANGELOG.md`, `MEMORY.md`,
  `LIVE_STATUS.md`, `docs/TASK_TREE.md` and the renderer's own comment — is corrected in this leaf's commit,
  and the historical ones say so rather than being rewritten silently. Mutation **M-E** below reinstates
  the first instrument's error inside the new leg: it reports **53** phantom overruns on the *fixed*
  renderer.
  WHERE: `crates/eadl-front/src/diagnostic.rs:264` at `3a35708`, `render_label` — `caret_width` was
  `sources.snippet(label.span).chars().count()`, the character count of the **whole** span, while the line
  printed above it is only `source.line_text(position.line)`, the span's first line.
  Impact: a refusal whose label covers a multi-line form — how F27 labels a rejected multi-line body —
  printed a marker line that wrapped the terminal and pointed at nothing. §5.5 requires a diagnostic to be
  source-localized; this one was localized to a line and then smeared past it.
  Acceptance: the caret clipped to the first line's remainder, and a multi-line span **marked** as
  continuing rather than silently truncated; the census above re-run, **10 → 0**; the non-ASCII and
  zero-width legs in `diagnostic.rs` still green; any book transcript the fix changes re-rendered from the
  command, not retyped; RED arms with the count pinned; `make focused` exit `0`.
  Priority: **medium**.
  Verification: see the checklist — the census **10 → 0**, six new legs, five mutations seen firing.

  The file is the toolchain's own language definition, embedded in the binary by
  `crates/archogen-cli/src/check_cmd.rs`'s `KIND_MODULES`; `archogen check` reads it as a *description*
  and hands back a verdict about a system. An author extending the language (`ROADMAP.md` §5.6, `M6`)
  who checks their own `defkind` file meets the same false verdict.
  Acceptance: decide and state what `check` does with a kind module — refuse it as `unimplemented` naming
  the leaf that admits user kind modules, or validate it as a kind module with `shipped_registry`'s own
  reader — through the routing point `M1.29.1` introduced; both commands agree; RED arms; `make focused`
  exit `0`.
  Priority: **low** — no surface claims `check` reads a kind module, and the shipped ones are exercised
  by `shipped_registry` on every run.

- ID: `M1.33`
  Status: `done`
  Decided `2026-09-29`, before any code: the rule is **`docs/semantics/reference.md` §7 rule 6**, because §7
  rule 5 already defines the schema's job as the declaration *frame* — "does it carry a name when its kind
  requires one" — and uniqueness of that name is the same frame, one level across declarations. The code is
  `schema-duplicate-name`, beside `schema-duplicate-kind` and `schema-duplicate-clause`, emitted by
  `crates/eadl-model/src/kind.rs` so §4's census governs it, and it runs inside the schema pass of
  `check.rs`'s shared `passes`, over the declarations the boundary accepted — so one rule covers a single
  file and a resolved module tree, where rule 9's collision surfaces as the same duplicate.
  Goal: **a name is declared once** — in a single description, and in an elaborated module tree — and a
  second declaration of it is refused with both sites named, never merged.
  Reproduce / issue: **found `2026-09-29` by `M1.29.3`**, while writing the legs for §6 rule 9, and then
  measured to be older than modules:
  ```text
  $ cat dup-decl.eadl
    (defblock timer.counter (offers (counter-width 32 bit)))
    (defblock timer.counter (offers (counter-width 16 bit)))
  $ archogen check dup-decl.eadl ; echo $?
    dup-decl.eadl: accepted against profile `rt-static-up-v1` (2 declaration(s))              exit=0
  census: git grep -n "declared twice\|duplicate declaration\|already declared" -- crates/eadl-model/src
    -> no match outside `kind.rs:444` (a CLAUSE declared twice in a KIND) — nothing checks declarations
  ```
  WHERE the silence becomes a choice: `crates/eadl-model/src/presence.rs`'s `record` stores an offer with
  `entry(name).or_insert(…)`, so the first `counter-width` wins and the second is dropped without a word —
  the "choosing one" §5.3 forbids ("Reject contradictory declarations rather than choosing one"). And
  §6 rule 9 adds a second way in: a module that declares `inner.x` and imports a module as `inner` that
  declares `x` gives both the name `p.inner.x` (stated as an open limit in §6, owned here).
  Acceptance: the rule written first — where: a declaration's name is part of the declaration frame, so
  `docs/semantics/reference.md` §7 and a `schema-` code emitted by `crates/eadl-model/src/kind.rs`, which
  the census governs, is the natural home, decided on the leaf with the reason; the refusal names **both**
  sites; it runs over the declarations `check` and `check_program` both hand to `passes`, so one rule
  covers a single file and a module tree; §6's open-limit paragraph retired; a semantic case and a module
  case, each pinned to its one code; the book's per-verdict table and corpus figures re-derived, not
  retyped; RED arms; `make focused` exit `0`.
  Priority: **high** — an accepted description that contradicts itself is a wrong verdict, not a missing
  one, and presence silently picks a side.
  Verification: see the checklist — 118 tracked descriptions censused before and after, 0 changed; two
  cases; six library legs; four mutations seen firing.
  Commit: `ARCHOGEN-M1-0116 (leaf M1.33)`
  promotion: declined (the `DEV_NOTES.md` lesson — an `or_insert` over a fact map is the code shape of
  §5.3's "choosing one" — answers no question a future reader would search the knowledge layer by that
  `docs/semantics/reference.md` §7 rule 6 and §5.3 do not already state as rules; it is recorded where it
  happened, and the rule is where a reader looks)

- ID: `M1.34`
  Status: `done`
  Goal: `Rational`'s ordering is exact, so a quantity comparison can never report two different amounts as
  equal — and a description whose task deadline exceeds its period is refused however many digits it is
  written with.
  Reproduce / issue: found `2026-09-29` while designing `PROGRAM.9.2`'s fuzz properties, by reading the claim
  in `crates/eadl-model/src/rational.rs`'s `impl Ord`: "saturation preserves the sign of the comparison at the
  extremes where an exact product would overflow". It does not when **both** cross products saturate:
  ```text
  a = (2^100 + 1) / 2^30,  b = (2^100 + 3) / 2^30
  a == b -> false;  a.cmp(&b) -> Equal          (true order: Less)
  ```
  Reached from a description through `Quantity::compare`, which three verdicts use — the offered/required
  match (`quantity.rs:226`), `equals` (`:375`) and §3.1's constrained-deadline rule (`workload.rs:149`).
  Measured with `archogen check` on `examples/periodic-three/system.eadl`, its first task's period and
  deadline replaced:
  ```text
  deadline 2 ns                    period 1 ns                     -> exit 12, "longer than its period"
  deadline 1.003 ns                period 1.001 ns                 -> exit 12, "longer than its period"
  deadline 1.00000000000000003 ns  period 1.00000000000000001 ns   -> exit 0   ⛔ accepted
  ```
  In base units both are fractions over 10^26 s, so both cross products (~2^143) saturate to `i128::MAX`.
  Census: `git grep -n saturating_mul -- 'crates/*.rs'` → rational.rs:255–256 only; the other saturating calls
  are bounded index, length and time subtractions, not comparisons.
  Acceptance: `cmp` compares the cross products exactly (a 256-bit product from 64-bit limbs; no dependency);
  `Ord` agrees with `Eq` for every pair of normalized values; the reproduction pair orders correctly; the
  long-decimal deadline description is refused with the same diagnostic as the short one; the false comment
  is replaced by what is true.
  Priority: **high** — a wrong verdict from the checker's own arithmetic, the §5.3 failure mode.
  Verification: see the checklist — unit and description-level regressions, the old comparison restored as a
  mutation, every tracked description's verdict before and after, the CLI reproduction re-run.
  Commit: `ARCHOGEN-M1-0128 (leaf M1.34)`

  ### Acceptance Checklist (enforced by `TASK-ACCEPTANCE`)

  - [x] **REPRODUCE / ISSUE** — above: `a.cmp(&b)` → `Equal` for `a ≠ b`; `archogen check` exit 0 on a deadline
    longer than its period, where the same relation in short decimals exits 12.
  - [x] **ROOT CAUSE (WHY + WHERE)** — a saturating cross product, justified by a claim that fails when both
    products saturate, and reached by three verdicts through `Quantity::compare`:
    ```text
    $ git grep -n "saturating_mul\|saturation preserves" 522f669 -- crates/eadl-model/src/rational.rs
      522f669:crates/eadl-model/src/rational.rs:252:    /// than a checked one: ordering must be total to satisfy `Ord`, and saturation preserves
      522f669:crates/eadl-model/src/rational.rs:255:        let left = self.numerator.saturating_mul(other.denominator);
      522f669:crates/eadl-model/src/rational.rs:256:        let right = other.numerator.saturating_mul(self.denominator);
    $ git grep -n "Ok(self.in_base()?.cmp(&other.in_base()?))\|let Ok(ordering) = deadline_q.compare(separation_q)" 522f669 -- crates/eadl-model/src

      522f669:crates/eadl-model/src/workload.rs:149:    let Ok(ordering) = deadline_q.compare(separation_q) else {
    ```
    The existing `ordering_is_exact_and_total` compared `1/3`, `1/2` and `0` — no value large enough to tell a
    saturating comparison from an exact one.
  - [x] **FIX** — `cmp` compares signs, then the magnitudes of the cross products **exactly**, as 256-bit

- ID: `M1.35`
  Status: `done`
  Goal: the reader survives every text: an unknown escape before a multi-byte character is reported, never a
  panic.
  Reproduce / issue: found `2026-09-29` while choosing `PROGRAM.9.2`'s fuzz targets, by reading the escape arm
  of `crates/eadl-front/src/reader.rs`: an unknown escape takes one **byte** (`other as char`) and advances
  one byte, which strands the reader inside a multi-byte character.
  ```text
  $ archogen check <(eadl-version eadl/1) (defkind x (doc "a\éb"))>
    thread 'main' panicked at crates/eadl-front/src/reader.rs:292:38:
    start byte index 42 is not a char boundary; it is inside 'é' (bytes 41..43)          exit=101
  ```
  Probed alongside, each through `archogen check`: `(a é b)`, `é`, `(a \é)`, `"\u{é}"`, `"\u{1F600}é"`,
  `(a 1é)`, `(a 1.5é)`, `(a -é)`, `(a 0xé)`, `;é comment`, `(a "é")`, `)é` — ordinary diagnostics or
  exit 0, no panic. The escape arm is the one crash found by hand; `PROGRAM.9.2`'s fuzz is the wider search.
  Acceptance: an unknown escape is read as one whole character — the diagnostic names it (`\é`), its span
  ends on a character boundary, the string keeps the characters literally as today; a test at the reader
  and one through the CLI's exit contract; the fix restored away is caught.
  Priority: **high** — a crash on user input, where the contract is a diagnostic and exit 10.
  Verification: see the checklist — a reader test at two widths, an in-process CLI test, the byte-wise read
  restored as a mutation, every tracked description's verdict before and after.
  Commit: `ARCHOGEN-M1-0129 (leaf M1.35)`

- ID: `M1.36`
  Status: `done`
  Goal: printing a quantity never panics and never prints another value — `Rational::to_exact_string`, which
  every quantity's `Display` uses, is exact for every value the type can hold.
  Reproduce / issue: found `2026-09-29` by `PROGRAM.9.2`'s fuzz harness on its **first run** — property
  `checked-arithmetic-never-panics-and-stays-normalized`, then `exact-text-reads-back-to-the-value`:
  ```text
  panicked at crates/eadl-model/src/rational.rs:218:13: attempt to multiply with overflow
    (`multiplier *= 10`, the power of ten for a denominator 2^a·5^b with max(a, b) > 38)
  5 seeds × 20 000 cases, overflow checks on: the only failure, in both properties
  ```
  In a release build the multiply **wraps** instead: the fuzz step's first release run passed, and the
  function printed a wrong decimal — silently. Census: `awk` over `rational.rs`, `quantity.rs` and
  `rt-analysis/src/response.rs` for unchecked `*` / `*=` outside tests → line 218, the four limb products of
  `widening_mul` (each below 2^128 by construction, `M1.34`) and two `quantity.rs` constants.
  ⚠️ Process note: `PROGRAM.9.2`'s harness, which found this, is parked in `git stash` ("PROGRAM.9.2 fuzz
  harness, parked while M1.36 fixes what it found") so this tree is entered clean; it is restored the commit
  after.
  Acceptance: the power of ten is computed checked, and a value with no `i128`-scaled decimal prints as its
  exact fraction; a unit test at `1/2^100`, `1/5^39` and the largest value that still prints as a decimal;
  the old multiply restored as a mutation is caught; the harness's property passes once restored.
  Priority: **high** — a panic in the function that prints every quantity, and a wrong number in release.
  Verification: see the checklist — a boundary test, the unchecked multiply restored as a mutation, every tracked
  description's exit code **and output** before and after.
  Commit: `ARCHOGEN-M1-0130 (leaf M1.36)`

  ### Acceptance Checklist (enforced by `TASK-ACCEPTANCE`)

  - [x] **REPRODUCE / ISSUE** — above: `panicked at crates/eadl-model/src/rational.rs:218:13: attempt to multiply
    with overflow`, on every one of 5 fuzz seeds; a wrong decimal, silently, in release.
  - [x] **ROOT CAUSE (WHY + WHERE)** — the power of ten was built by an unchecked loop, and `10^max(a, b)` passes
    `i128` once the denominator carries more than 38 twos or fives:
    ```text
    $ git grep -n "multiplier \*= 10" 52d1cba -- crates/eadl-model/src/rational.rs
      52d1cba:crates/eadl-model/src/rational.rs:218:            multiplier *= 10;
    ```
  - [x] **FIX** — `10_i128.checked_pow(scale)`; a value with no `i128`-scaled decimal is written as its exact
    fraction, as a third already is.
  - [x] **ADDRESSED (verified)** —
    ```text
    $ cargo test -q -p eadl-model --lib
      test result: ok. 55 passed; 0 failed     (new: `1/2^100` and `1/5^39` print as fractions; `1/5^38`, the
                                               last that fits, prints all 38 decimals; `-2^112/5^15` prints the
                                               decimal whose digits are exactly 2^127)
    ```
    The unchecked loop restored as a mutation → `panicked at crates/eadl-model/src/rational.rs:222:13`,
    `test result: FAILED. 14 passed; 1 failed`; restored by `cmp`. The fuzz harness's two properties pass once it
    is restored (`PROGRAM.9.2`).
  - [x] **NO REGRESSION** — every tracked description through `archogen check`, from a stash of this change and
    from the change, comparing exit code **and a digest of the whole output**, since printing is what changed:
    ```text
    $ diff m136_before.txt m136_after.txt
      rc=0                                     -> 120 descriptions, identical exit codes and output
    $ cargo test --all -q
      test result: passed=610 failed=0 over 43 suites
    ```
    `make focused` passed; the doctrine driver green at the commit.
  - [x] **LOCKSTEP** — the book's `quantities.md` says when a value prints as a fraction and what this replaced.
    `promotion: declined (the lesson — a property run in a release build cannot see overflow — belongs to the
    fuzz step's design and is recorded and promoted with PROGRAM.9.2, which found it)`.

## Current Frontier

**`M1.27` is closed: "every production is reached" is measured.** The recognizer reports the productions of
the derivation it accepted, and the exclusions that fired. The probe test maps each production to the probes
that reach it, and fails on one that none reaches. `control` is reachable only as an exclusion, so a malformed
probe covers it.

**`M1.37` is closed: the reader refuses a string with no delimiter after it.** `("b"c)` read as two atoms, and
the grammar refuses it. It is now `read-missing-delimiter`, a new §4 row under a migration note, with conformance
probes that hold the reader and the grammar-derived recognizer to the same answer. The third reader now agrees on
every tracked description. The fuzz smoke caught the first cut labelling half a character.

**`M1.29.1` is closed: a module file is refused as what it is.** `archogen check` and `archogen build` answered `docs/book/src/modules.md`'s own opening example with `invalid-description`, exit **10** — `schema-unknown-kind` for the `defmodule`, then `missing-fact` for a name its unresolved import brings in — a verdict about a system the tool never read. Both now route through one pair in `crates/archogen-cli/src/check_cmd.rs` (`module_file`, `refuse_module_file`) and answer `unimplemented`, exit **20**, naming the module, where it is and `M1.29.2`; a module that does not *read* keeps the read pass's verdict. `archogen help check` says "type-check", and `crates/archogen-cli/tests/module_files.rs` couples the word "elaborate" to the capability and fails if `M1.29.2` closes while the refusal still names it. Measured: 78 tracked descriptions, 0 changed in verdict or code; six mutations, each firing with its count pinned. ⛔ The loader itself was measured past one slice, so `M1.29` is decomposed into `.1`–`.4` and `M1.31` is filed for the caret defect the reproduction exposed.

**`M1.28.1` is closed: one accessor now carries a rule that had three consumers and two answers.**
`Verdict::of_code` sits beside `parse` in `crates/eadl-front/src/diagnostic.rs` and defaults a code that is
not a §5.5 verdict slug to `invalid-description`, because such a code names a *rule* the description broke
and not a failure of the toolchain. `crates/archogen-cli/src/build_cmd.rs:136` was the third consumer and
the only one defaulting to `ToolFailure`, and it was the one facing the author: a build refusing
`(period 10 parsec)` exited **70**, the status `status.rs:8` reserves for the invocation, so an author who
mistyped a unit was told to file a bug about the tool. Measured both ways through the published CLI — 70 →
**10** — with the *before* taken from a stashed tree rather than remembered, and **four mutations seen
firing**: the old default restored (only the new end-to-end arm fails), the accessor's default set to
`ToolFailure` (arm 1, the original defect as a mutation), to `Ok` (arms 1 and 3, so silent *acceptance* is
pinned and not only the reported hazard), and the accessor made to ignore its argument (arms 2 and 3, so
the totality arm is load-bearing and `of_code` provably does not shadow `parse`). ⛔ It fixes the
classification and not the reachability: `archogen check` still accepts those bytes, which is `M1.28.2`,
and the build's message now says the disagreement out loud rather than leaving the author to find it.
**545 passed / 0 failed** over 39 suites, from 541.

**`M1.28` is closed, and closing it turned the migration workflow it was the first leaf to run into a
defect report.** Both children are `done`: `.1` put a rule that had three consumers and two answers into
one accessor (`Verdict::of_code`, exit **70 → 10**), and `.2` made the schema able to say that a clause
holds a *quantity* rather than a number and a name, and made the refinement pass propagate what it finds
instead of discarding it. `archogen check` and `archogen build` now answer the same way about the same
bytes, and a census over all **76** tracked descriptions finds exactly one that changed — F-K's case,
moving from a clause-name typo to the zero frequency its header always claimed. **559 passed / 0 failed**
over 39 suites. ⛔ Two things closing it does **not** claim, both owned: the rule "a number followed by a
symbol is a quantity" is enforced and stated in two chapters and two comments and in **no normative
document**, which is `M1.26.1`'s; and `LANGUAGE-FREEZE`'s explicitness leg is **inert on the real tree**
(**F-O**, reproduced with no note at all and an amended baseline printing `OK`), so the migration note
`.2` wrote is a record and not yet an enforced precondition — `PROGRAM.27`, priority high.

**`M1.28` was found by picking up `M1.26.1` rather than by looking for it.** The
question "which pass surfaces each of the model layer's codes?" — asked in order to write a normative

consumer that surfaces what it finds is `crates/archogen-s0/src/interpret.rs`, the prototype

it is **F-H's shape measured twice more**: an existence census — legs 4 and 5, `BOOK-ANCHORS`, leg 8 —
proves a code is emitted, stated and rendered, and cannot see that nothing routes it to an author, that
its row describes a mechanism the code lacks, or that its call site is an arm no input reaches. F-J is
the third instance and is recorded on `M1.26.1`'s leaf.

**`M1.26` is decomposed into two children** (`2026-09-29`), measured before either was written, and the

coded **four** ways across two crates and two bare `archogen:` lines. Legs 4 and 5 are green on it, which
is the whole argument for gap (b)'s column. Owned by `M1.26.2`.

**`M1.25` is closed: history is a list you can read, not a tense you can trip over.** The reach gate
excused any non-current `N of M` figure on a line containing `was`, `were`, `previously`, `before` or

gate that closed `M1.23` had never been observed firing, and the seven arms the sentence was describing

violation count, and the discriminating evidence is a meta-mutation: putting the marker scan back fails

**`M1.13` is closed: `eadl/1` is a version with a name on the surface, a declared conformance suite, and
a freeze that refuses to move silently.** All five children are done and the parent's four acceptance

that both moves a construct and freezes it cannot show a clean before→after for either, and the RED arm
that proves a gate fires needs a prior state to differ from. Three defects came out of it that no

`M1.13.4.2` (a normative sentence false for one commit) and `M1.27` (a test named for a coverage property

**`M1.13.5` is closed: the freeze is a gate, and the gate's own first design had the hole its rule 2
predicted.** `scripts/check_language_freeze.sh` runs two legs, because one has a hole the other closes:
integrity (the tracked baseline agrees with a fresh run) catches a construct edited and the baseline left
alone, and explicitness (the tracked baseline agrees with `HEAD`'s, or a **pending** note names every
construct the amendment moves) catches the direction that would otherwise be the waiver — edit the
construct, run `--emit`, green. Classification comes from `M1.13.4.5`'s Rust comparator through a new
`--diff` mode, so the gate and its nine arms share one implementation of "what moved". ⛔ Two defects in
the gate were found by its own arms and not by reading it: bash returns **0** from an `if` whose condition
failed with no `else`, so `case "$?"` after it would have taken the "unreadable baseline" branch for every
real movement; and the arms' parser read the classifier's summary line as a difference, producing an id of
`1` that no note could name. Cost measured rather than assumed — **1.5–1.7 s** warm, nearly all of it the
`cargo run` that prints canonical forms — and kept in the pre-commit path with a named trigger for
revisiting it. The arm the parent said only this leaf could have is the one against the real tree: a
description edited, the gate firing on that file by name, and the restoration inside a `trap`.

could reach.** `shipped_registry` — the loader that reads `docs/semantics/kinds/` — handed every
